Corollary 3.12 Preliminary version for comments Kirti Joshi Abstract This is a continuation of my work on Arithmetic Teichmuller Spaces. This paper establishes a number of important results including (1) a proof Mochizuki’s Corollary 3.12 (2) a ‘Rosetta Stone’ for a parallel reading [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] and [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] , and (3) a proof that Mochizuki’s gluing of Hodge-Theaters, Frobenioids along prime-strips à la [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] is naturally provided by the existence of Arithmetic Teichmuller Spaces. It is not enough to be in the right place at the right time. You should also have an open mind at the right time. Paul Erdos [ Sphechter, 1998 ] Contents 1 Introduction § 1.1 The goals of this paper [For a quick outline of results see § 1.15 .] This paper is a continuation of [ Joshi, 2023a ] which lays the foundations of global (i.e. over a number field) theory of Arithmetic Teichmuller Spaces. Familiarity with the results of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] is essential in reading this paper. The present paper has two goals: (1) To provide a ‘Rosetta Stone’ for a parallel reading of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] and the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper). This is established in § 8 with Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 and (also see § 10 , § 11 ). (2) The second goal of this paper is to establish the assertion known as (Shinichi) Mochizuki’s Corollary 3.12. This is introduced in § 1.8 while the (global) geometric case is discussed in § 1.9 . [Details appear in ( § 6 , § 7 , § 9 ) and the geometric case ( § 12 ).] The present paper deals exclusively with the (global) arithmetic case of the said corollary. This requires formulating an Arithmetic Teichmuller Theory for Number Fields which is detailed in [ Joshi, 2023a ] (Mochizuki asserts a less precise version of such a theory). In [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] , Mochizuki claims that the arithmetic case, [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] , leads to the proof of several important Diophantine conjectures, including the a ​ b ​ c abc -Conjecture. This is taken up in [ Joshi, 2024a ] . A detailed introduction to the present paper follows. § 1.2 Classical Teichmuller Theory as a model for the Arithmetic Case A familiarity with classical Teichmuller Theory is highly recommended for understanding the arithmetic case of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] established in the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper). From an algebro-geometric perspective, classical Teichmuller Theory may be understood as providing a natural variation of the periods of Riemann surfaces which complements and refines the variation of periods constructed by Riemann (for explicit examples see § 12 ). Likewise, the theory of the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper) and [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] (via Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 ), both arise from the existence of natural variations of p p -adic periods, at all primes p p simultaneously, of a fixed variety over a number field. For an explicit example of such a variation see [ Joshi, 2023a , § ] . So the perspective of the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper) (and [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] ) is to create, in the arithmetic case, a theory which parallels the classical Teichmuller Theory. [Mochizuki’s methods do not extend to higher genus curves or to higher dimensions. Specifically, the discussion in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] , is restricted to elliptic curves definable over a fixed number field. So my approach has an added advantage.] § 1.3 Mochizuki’s Key Principle of Inter-Universality: The role of arbitrary geometric base-points According to Mochizuki, his Key Principle of Inter-Universality [ Mochizuki, 2021a , § I3, Page 25] , lies at the foundation of the theory which he has called Inter-Universal Teichmuller Theory . In brief, Mochizuki’s Key Principal of Inter-Universality requires one to work with arbitrary geometric base-points for tempered fundamental groups. To put it succinctly: Mochizuki’s Key Principle of Inter-Universality i.e. working with arbitrary geometric base-points is an anabelian proxy for deformations (of arithmetic). My work provides the most natural way of quantifying this statement (this is discussed below in § 1.4 , § 1.5 ). As noted in [ Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023c ] , formulating the theory using Mochizuki’s Key Principle of Inter-Universality i.e. using arbitrary geometric base-points necessarily implies that (arbitrary) algebraically closed perfectoid fields enter [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] (and one cannot limit one-self to ℂ p {\mathbb{C}}_{p} (for all primes p p )). This necessarily forces one to take the path I have taken in the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper). [A detailed discussion of Mochizuki’s principle (and how it appears in my theory) is given in § 8.2.2 .] It should be noted that, no discussion of the centrality of the role of arbitrary geometric base-points in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] (required by Mochizuki’s key principle) appears in any of [ Fesenko, 2015 ] , [ Tan, 2018 ] , [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] , [ Yamashita, 2019 ] , [ Dupuy and Hilado, 2020b , Dupuy and Hilado, 2020a ] , [ Scholze, 2021 ] , [ Saïdi, 2022 ] . § 1.4 Number Fields are Deformable Mochizuki’s presentation of his ideas in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] is quite labyrinthine. Hence, unfortunately, even the sharpest of readers can loose sight of the profoundly original idea within [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . So let me explicate it for the readers: for the past hundred and fifty years, since the works of Ernst Kummer and Richard Dedekind, number theorists have viewed a fixed number field as a static background in which the drama of Diophantine Geometry unfolds. Mochizuki asserts that this static background itself is a dynamic, pliable, deformable entity i.e. the arithmetic of a fixed number field itself is bendable. [Mochizuki offers no convincing proof of this claim in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . In [ Joshi, 2023a ] , I have established this claim quite robustly and independently by demonstrating the existence of topological deformations of the arithmetic of a fixed number field (and hence my use of the term bendable is quite appropriate). So at this juncture, Mochizuki’s assertion itself is true.] Moreover, Mochizuki posits that this bendability of a number field may be harnessed to establish one of the most sought-after, fundamental conjectures in Diophantine Geometry. That is why I believe Mochizuki’s idea is pioneering and truly remarkable. § 1.5 Quantification of Deformations of Number Fields While Mochizuki has asserted that his approach may be understood as proceeding by dismantling, distorting and reassembling of the arithmetic of a fixed number field [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Page 12] , nevertheless, a precise mathematical quantification of what exactly this means has remained elusive in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] and its numerous expositions. That is why a clear understanding of its claims has not emerged in the decade following the initial public release of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] and skepticism regarding [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] has prevailed and solidified with the appearance of [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] (my discussion of this is in [ Joshi, 2023c , Joshi, 2021b ] ). In [ Joshi, 2023a ] , I have established the most precise version of Mochizuki’s claim. Notably, I demonstrate that primes of a fixed number field provide, in a precise sense, multiplicative coordinates for the number field and that the claimed distortions arise from general changes of these prime coordinates in a manner reminiscent of the Virasoro Uniformization in Conformal Field Theory [ Beilinson and Schechtman, 1988 ] ; and by [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] these changes also distort all local analytic geometries at all primes. Importantly, my work makes it clear that non-trivial distortions of the fixed number field (the theory literally permits topologically bending the rules of arithmetic in the precise sense of [ Joshi, 2023a ] ) and p p -adic metrics (at all primes), do arise and are mediated by these general changes of prime coordinates (readers familiar with the role of Virasoro Uniformization in Conformal Field Theory will recognize why my usage of the term ‘bendability of the number field is quite apt). [A less precise version of this appears as Mochizuki’s Indeterminacy Ind2 in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . Unfortunately a precise demonstration of the existence and quantification of the properties of Ind2 does not seem possible without invoking my approach. Without a precise quantification, it is not possible to say, for example, when two arithmetic holomorphic structures differ by an Indeterminacy of type Ind2. I also demonstrate precisely how Mochizuki’s indeterminacy Ind3 arises. [My discussion, with proofs, of how Mochizuki’s Three indeterminacies arise is in § 8.11 .] These are precisely the sort of assertions which are needed for a rigorous and satisfactory formulation (and proof) of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] .] § 1.6 The role of the Absolute Grothendieck Conjecture Let me say a few words which will clarify the role of the absolute Grothendieck conjecture in my theory (and Mochizuki’s theory). Understanding the consequences of the existence of realms where one has validity and invalidity of this conjecture turns out to be central in arriving at a clear understanding of my theory and Mochizuki’s theory. However, discussion of the failure of this conjecture (i.e. the existence of realms of its invalidity) does not occur in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] at all as it stays focused on validity of the conjecture. First let me discuss the realm where one has the validity of this conjecture. Mochizuki in [ Mochizuki, 2007 , Corollary 2.3] established that the absolute Grothendieck Conjecture is true for incomplete hyperbolic curves over p p -adic fields arising from hyperbolic curves definable over number fields. As noted in [ Joshi, 2022 ] this validity assertion is equivalent to the assertion that the arithmetic Teichmuller space of such a curve is connected and the field of moduli of this curve is its field of definition. [This is analogous to the theorem that a Shimura variety is defined over a suitable reflex field which is the minimal field of definition of its points definable over a number field.]. Now let me discuss two realms where the absolute Grothendieck conjecture fails to hold and why both the instances of its failure are central to the two theories. It is well-known that the absolute Grothendieck conjecture fails for hyperbolic Riemann surfaces and this failure arises from the existence of Teichmuller and moduli spaces of Riemann surfaces. Pursuing this idea in the p p -adic context, in [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2021b ] , I established that the absolute Grothendieck conjecture is false in the category of Berkovich spaces over algebraically closed, complete (rank one) valued fields containing ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} and that this signals the existence of an Arithmetic Teichmuller Theory (this is one of the principal results of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 ] ). The second realm where the absolute Grothendieck conjecture fails was observed in [ Joshi, 2020b ] where I established that the absolute Grothendieck conjecture fails for a complete Fargues-Fontaine curve X ℂ p ♭ , E {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E} for suitable p p -adic fields (and all primes p p ). By the main theorem of [ Joshi, 2021a ] , one may view X ℂ p ♭ , E {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E} as an approximate parameter space of arithmetic Holomorphic structures. Since the idea of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] is to average over arithmetic holomorphic structures, considering all Fargues-Fontaine curves X ℂ p ♭ , E ′ {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E^{\prime}} with étale fundamental groups isomorphic to π 1 e ​ t ​ ( X ℂ p ♭ , E ) ≃ G E \pi_{1}^{et}({X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E})\simeq G_{E} (for all primes p p ) ensures that this averaging of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] takes place over the largest possible set of arithmetic holomorphic structures permitted by the theory! In other words, Mochizuki’s Indeterminacy Ind1 (at all primes) arises from the failure of the absolute Grothendieck conjecture for Fargues-Fontaine curves [ Joshi, 2020b ] (at all primes). This is how Mochizuki’s Indeterminacy Ind1 emerges from the point of view of the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper). [A detailed discussion of Mochizuki’s Indeterminacies is in § 8 .] However, working with X ℂ p ♭ , E {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E} has one major drawback: residue fields of the closed classical points of X ℂ p ♭ , E {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E} do not come equipped with natural valuations. This is why one must work with incomplete Fargues-Fontaine curves Y ℂ p ♭ , E {Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E} whose closed classical points provide residue fields equipped with valuations [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 ] . This important point, together with the central observation of [ Joshi, 2023b , Appendix] that the Frobenius of Y ℂ p ♭ , E {Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E} has all the properties Mochizuki asserts for his 𝔩 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔤 \mathfrak{log} -link, establishes unequivocally that the definition of arithmetic holomorphic structures made in [ Joshi, 2022 ] is the most natural one even for [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . The next central point in [ Joshi, 2023b ] is the constructions of certain correspondences on Y ℂ p ♭ , E {Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E} which have all the properties of Mochizuki’s Θ g ​ a ​ u \Theta_{gau} -Link and notably I demonstrate precisely how Mochizuki’s asserted valuation scaling property (of a Θ g ​ a ​ u \Theta_{gau} -Link) and especially how a certain scaling factor j 2 j^{2} (for j = 1 , 2 , … , ℓ − 1 2 j=1,2,\ldots,\frac{\ell-1}{2} ) appears non-trivially (the global arithmetic consequence of this factor is established in Corollary 4.2.2.4 –this assertion is [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Theorem A(ii)] ). The non-triviality of this factor is a key sticky point in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] and especially in proof of the main theorem of [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] because of which its existence is denied in [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 , Section 2.2] . My proof of the non-triviality of this factor is Theorem 4.2.2.1 (the local case is detailed in [ Joshi, 2023b ] ) and its global arithmetic consequences are given by Corollary 4.2.2.4 . § 1.7 Global aspects: The central role of global arithmetic of number fields Let me say that the theory of the present series of papers is a global arithmetic theory and I want to point out how these global aspects play a central role in this paper. There are several important new ingredients needed for the global version of the discussion of the previous paragraph. One key ingredient is the theory of (global) Arithmetic Teichmuller Spaces of Number Fields described in [ Joshi, 2023a ] which provides the non-trivial deformations of any fixed number field; several other new ingredients which are required is detailed here. Let me briefly describe the global aspects. One key feature of the global theory of [ Joshi, 2023a ] is the fact that each avatar 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} of a fixed number field L L defines a hyperplane (see [ Joshi, 2023a , § ] ) given by the validity of the product formula for the number field H 𝐲 ⊂ ⨁ v ∈ 𝕍 L ℝ . H_{\bf y}\subset\bigoplus_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}}{\mathbb{R}}. While in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] , Mochizuki has indicated the key role of the product formula through the appearance of global realified Frobenioids in his theory, a key innovation, [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] and [ Joshi, 2023a , Remark ] , of [ Joshi, 2023a ] is that the product formula for the normalized arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 n ​ o ​ r \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}^{nor} defines a new arithmetic period mapping (1.7.1) 𝐘 𝐋 {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscrbf{Y}_{L}} ℙ ( ⊕ v ∈ 𝕍 L ℝ ) {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}}(\oplus_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}}{\mathbb{R}})} 𝐲 ⟼ H 𝐲 \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bf y}\longmapsto H_{\bf y}} On one hand, the valuations on untilts parameterized by a Fargues-Fontaine curve at some arbitrary prime (e.g. Y ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p {Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} ) cannot be all simultaneously normalized and on the other hand, local changes of valuations are globally constrained by the validity of the product formula. These two observations imply that the above arithmetic period mapping is a highly non-trivial global arithmetic period mapping in which an arithmetic avatar 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} provides a hyperplane H 𝐲 H_{\bf y} (as above) which moves under symmetries (such as the actions of the absolute Galois group, global Frobenius and L ∗ L^{*} ) established in [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] . This period mapping combined with Mochizuki’s Θ g ​ a ​ u \Theta_{gau} -links (encapsulated in the natural constructions of § 8.10 and [ Joshi, 2023b , § ] ) which scale valuations (via Theorem 4.6.1 and Remark 4.6.2 ) at primes of semi-stable reduction of an elliptic curve, also force renormalizations of valuations at primes of good or non-semistable reduction so that the product formula continues to hold ( Corollary 4.2.2.4 ). That these phenomenon surrounding these global product formula hyperplanes do occur is asserted without adequate proof in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] ( Remark 4.6.2 provides a detailed discussion of this point). However, the global-product-formula-as-a-period-mapping optik of [ Joshi, 2023a ] is my own innovation beyond [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . Another important point is that the aforementioned (global) action of L ∗ ​ ↷ ​ 𝐘 𝐋 L^{*}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} (given by [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] ), which plays an important role in the aforementioned period mapping is also incorporated in the construction of Mochizuki’s Adelic Ansatz given in § 4 . Hence through this, the action L ∗ ​ ↷ ​ 𝐘 𝐋 L^{*}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} also intervenes in the construction of the theta-values locii constructed here through the stability properties of Mochizuki’s Adelic Ansatz ( Definition 4.2.2 ) with respect to this global action ( Theorem 4.2.2.1 ). The effect of this action in the Diophantine contexts of heights is given in [ Joshi, 2023a , § ] . To put it succinctly, global properties of the number field L ′ L^{\prime} are woven into the construction of Mochizuki’s Global Ansatz Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} (through Theorem 4.2.2.1 ) and hence into the construction of all the theta-values sets constructed in this paper. Let me also remark that the existence of Initial Theta Data (see § 3 ) which is vital to the constructions of the theta-values locii in § 6 and hence to [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] is a global arithmetic fact (which cannot be proved locally). While this existence is detailed in [ Joshi, 2024a ] (Mochizuki’s proof of this existence is in [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] ), it is central to the present paper. My approach to constructing (global) theta-values locii is qualitatively the same as that adopted in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] . At any rate, these are some of the many ways in which the theory of this paper and [ Joshi, 2023a ] is a global theory. § 1.8 Mochizuki’s Corollary 3.12 Now to the main topic of this paper, namely [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] and its proof ( § 6 , § 7 , § 9 ). Since the claim of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] is so remarkable, its ideas rather original, a complete proof of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] , is surely needed for anyone wanting to be persuaded of the validity of the central assertion of [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] . Unfortunately, the approach to this corollary in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] is so convoluted that many readers have arrived at a limited understanding of what the corollary claims and what methods are used for arriving at its conclusion. Mochizuki’s [ Mochizuki, 2020 ] is readable but offers rudimentary and unworkable analogies while [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] deflects the main mathematical existential question, raised by [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] and myself (as noted earlier, in [ Joshi, October 2020 , Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 ] , I have successfully addressed the question of the existence of arithmetic holomorphic structures head-on by constructing them explicitly). § 1.9 The geometric case of Mochizuki’s Corollary 3.12 As is well-known, Szpiro formulated the Arithmetic Szpiro Conjecture based on his discovery (and proof) of the Geometric Szpiro Conjecture ( [ Szpiro, 1981 ] , [ Szpiro, 1990 ] ). Since then several proofs of the Geometric Szpiro Conjecture have appeared. The proofs [ Amorós et al., 2000 ] and [ Zhang, 2001 ] are Teichmuller theoretic in their approach. In § 12 , I provide a discussion, from the point of view of this paper and of [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] , of the proofs geometric Szpiro inequality due to [ Amorós et al., 2000 ] , [ Zhang, 2001 ] , exhibiting, in these proofs the existence of such avatars of a Riemann surface and related symmetries–especially the role of a global Frobenius morphism. Notably, in Theorem 12.17.1 , I prove the geometric case of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] in the context of Geometric Szpiro Inequality as established in [ Amorós et al., 2000 ] and [ Zhang, 2001 ] . Readers are strongly advised to read formulation of the geometric case of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] given in § 12 before proceeding with the arithmetic case which occupies the bulk of this paper. § 1.10 Many versions of Mochizuki’s Corollary 3.12 My important discovery is that, in fact, there are many versions of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] (Mochizuki considers a specific one). The formulation of any version of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] requires the construction of the respective theta-values sets–the main ones constructed here in § 6 , § 9 are Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} , Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} (and their varianFts). It should be emphasized that the central difficulty of establishing [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] is the construction of any of these theta-values sets with a certain list of properties. On the other hand, [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] have doubted that such a set could possibly exist and the conclusions of [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] are centrally dependent on their non-existence assertion. However , due to the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper) existence of such sets is no longer in doubt. Each of these sets is the collation of theta-values arising from a certain set, dubbed Mochizuki’s (global) Ansatz , consisting of certain tuples of distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures (each tuple is a Θ g ​ a ​ u \Theta_{gau} -Link in the sense of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] ). The decorations 𝔹 L ′ , 𝐈 {\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}},\mathscrbf{I} refer to the codomain used for obtaining theta-values. To be sure, the construction of each of these sets requires the existence of all of the following (1) the choice of a global arithmetic datum Initial Theta-Data (known by this name in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] ). (2) The existence of a plurality of (a) arithmetic holomorphic structures (at each prime), (b) 𝔩 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔤 \mathfrak{log} -Links, (c) Θ \Theta -Links, (d) global deformations of a number field, (3) and the establishment of the symmetry properties of these data. (4) a common codomain for collating theta-values provided by (1)-(3) . [From my perspective, the existence of these data is the construction of Arithmetic Teichmuller Spaces and a precise quantification of their properties documented in the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper).] Importantly, [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] asserts the construction of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} –subject to the existence of (1)-(4) above. The set Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} is referred to therein as the multi-radial representation of theta-values i.e. in the parlance of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] , each of the sets Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 , Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}},{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} are multi-radial representations of theta-values (with respect to their respective theta-value codomains). Here, 𝐈 \mathscrbf{I} is Mochizuki’s tensor packet codomain for theta-values. [Beware that the superscript 𝐈 \mathscrbf{I} in Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}},{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} refers to 𝐈 M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i \boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} and 𝐈 J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i \boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}} respectively and these two codomains are slightly different versions of each other, my version being simpler of the two. In [ Mochizuki, 2021c , § 3] , 𝐈 M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i \boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} is called the “tensor product log-shell.”] There are variants Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ~ , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}},{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} in which 𝐈 \mathscrbf{I} is replaced by a product codomain which is simpler and adequate for obtaining lower bound given by [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] ( 𝔹 L ′ \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}} in Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} refers to a product codomain and it also comes with its tensor product version). An important point is that 𝔹 L ′ \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}} is a ring (while 𝐈 \mathscrbf{I} is not) and so in this ring one can multiply theta-values arising from different arithmetic holomorphic structures. This allows me to demonstrate that Mochizuki’s tensor product structure (detailed in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , § 3] ) arises essentially from the product structure in this ring. However, the tensor packet codomain 𝐈 \mathscrbf{I} is better suited for tighter upper bounds on the theta-values sets and is indispensable for [ Joshi, 2024a ] and [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] . As I demonstrate here, the choice of any of these theta-values sets leads to a formulation of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] specific to this choice and hence one obtains several distinct avatars of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] (including Mochizuki’s version). In each case, the proof of the fundamental estimate of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] proceeds along the lines of the proto-type of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] which I have established in [ Joshi, 2023b ] . In [ Joshi, 2023a , Appendix I] , I establish the geometric case of this corollary. At this point, using Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 (and the above data constructed in the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper)) one may also obtain a proof of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] along the lines of [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] . Notably, I demonstrate that Mochizuki’s gluing of Hodge-Theaters and Frobenioids along prime-strips also arises quite naturally from my point of view. § 1.11 Why this work is needed: The incompleteness of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] The proof of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] , is incomplete as the proof rests on establishing the existence of a plurality of arithmetic holomorphic structures (and their symmetry properties). Here, I use the term plurality in the sense of being logically distinct. Thus for example, classical Teichmuller Theory asserts the existence of a plurality of complex structures on a fixed topological surface. One needs the existence of arithmetic holomorphic structures at all primes of a number field, and deformations of a number field itself. These central points are claimed, but not established in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . This was first pointed out in [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] , and later, more robustly in my works. On the other hand, [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] (and [ Scholze, 2021 ] ) ignore Mochiuzki’s Key Principle of Inter-Universality (see § 1.3 ) and also arrived at the incorrect conclusion that distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures cannot exist at all. That assertion of [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 , Scholze, 2021 ] has been disproved in [ Joshi, October 2020 , Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 ] , by constructing large families of distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures and establishing the existence of deformations of a fixed number field in [ Joshi, 2023a ] (the existence of such deformations is also asserted by Mochizuki). My approach to arithmetic holomorphic structures, detailed in [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] , puts the theory on par with the classical notion of complex holomorphic structures and provides arithmetic holomorphic structures in Mochizuki’s group theoretic sense. Notably, Mochizuki’s group theoretic arithmetic holomorphic structures may be distinguished by actual Berkovich analytic (i.e. holomorphic) structures and one obtains Mochizuki’s group theoretic arithmetic holomorphic structures by applying the (tempered) fundamental group functor to the relevant Berkovich analytic spaces. My theory may also be applied to a number field and this provides a deformation theory of number fields ( [ Joshi, 2023a ] ), whose existence is also asserted in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . § 1.12 On [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] Mochizuki in his rejoinder [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] to [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] asserted that their argument is incorrect. However, the arguments presented in [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] do not convincingly establish the said existence but rather mandates the existence of distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures. [Read in the classical context of Riemann surfaces, this mandate argument is tantamount to mandating that distinct complex structures exist on a topological surface.] My point is that this mandate argument of [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] , is (1) wholly inadequate because [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] also requires a way of distinguishing arithmetic holomorphic structures and precisely proving how these transform under symmetries of the theory (this is a key point needed in the construction of the set which underlies [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] ); (2) also completely unnecessary, since distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures do exist for canonical reasons [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 ] . Notably, [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] suggests the existence of distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures but it is not clearly established in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , nor can it be quantitatively established using the framework of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] or [ Mochizuki, 2012 , Mochizuki, 2013 , Mochizuki, 2015 ] . § 1.13 Other articles on [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] Apart from the present series of papers, several other articles on the subject of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] have appeared: [ Fesenko, 2015 ] , [ Tan, 2018 ] , [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] , [ Yamashita, 2019 ] , [ Dupuy and Hilado, 2020b , Dupuy and Hilado, 2020a ] , [ Scholze, 2021 ] , [ Saïdi, 2022 ] . Let me say a few words about these articles: [ Fesenko, 2015 ] , [ Tan, 2018 ] , and [ Yamashita, 2019 ] are surveys and they essentially repeat [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] verbatim on all its key points. In [ Dupuy and Hilado, 2020b , Dupuy and Hilado, 2020a ] , they do not state the inequality [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] as a theorem, but work relative to Mochizuki’s assertion. The reviews [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] , [ Scholze, 2021 ] have been discussed by me in [ Joshi, 2022 ] , [ Joshi, 2023c ] and in the present paper. It should be noted that except for a passing reference to Mochizuki’s philosophy of such structures in [ Yamashita, 2019 ] , the term ‘arithmetic holomorphic structure’ (coined and used by Mochizuki himself) does not appear in any of [ Fesenko, 2015 ] , [ Tan, 2018 ] , [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] , [ Yamashita, 2019 ] , [ Dupuy and Hilado, 2020b , Dupuy and Hilado, 2020a ] , [ Scholze, 2021 ] , [ Saïdi, 2022 ] . But importantly, as Mochizuki asserts in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Page 24] , arithmetic holomorphic structures are central to [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] and the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper) is the only one which treats arithmetic holomorphic structures in detail and establishes their existence in the context of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . § 1.14 Two points of departure from [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] Apart from the construction of many theta-value sets, there are two important points of departure between my treatment of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] and that of [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] . First point arises from my observation that Mochizuki’s rational log-shell can be naturally identified with a suitable Bloch-Kato subspace, of the form H e 1 ​ ( − , − ) H^{1}_{e}(-,-) , which consists of “Frobenius trivial, crystalline” Galois cohomology classes. [The reason for the quotes is a bit technical and as follows: such classes correspond to working with B e = B c ​ r ​ i ​ s φ = 1 B_{e}=B_{cris}^{\varphi=1} rather than with B B , but beware that since construction of B c ​ r ​ i ​ s B_{cris} requires fixing an algebraic closure of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} , a choice of ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} is tacit in writing this equality, while in the present theory ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} moves with an arithmetic holomorphic structure (the construction of B B does not require a choice of ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} ). Hence this equality is an isomorphism in the context of the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper). Because of this, the relevant Frobenius action on the Galois cohomology is by a non-identity automorphism (in general).] The identification of this cohomology with Mochizuki’s rational log-shell is via the inverse of the relevant Bloch-Kato exponential i.e. with a relevant Bloch-Kato logarithm. Mochizuki’s integral log-shell is the image of the relevant integral subgroup of this rational cohomology. This is the genesis of Mochizuki’s integral log-shell and one now has a natural reason why it is relevant in the first place. [To understand why “Frobenius triviality” is relevant in the context of the arithmetic Szpiro Conjecture, see [ Joshi, 2023a , Appendix I] where this property appears in the proof of the Geometric Szpiro Conjecture of [ Amorós et al., 2000 , Zhang, 2001 ] .] However, Theta-values do not live in this subspace. Hence, I construct “Frobenius trivial, crystalline” galois cohomology classes arising from a Tate elliptic curve (for any choice of a Tate parameter). Mochizuki’s approach to circumventing the fact that the theta-values do not live in the relevant subspace is to use a multiplicative action [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Figure 3.1] of theta values on the relevant local components of 𝐈 M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i \boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} for defining Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} . Using the inverse of the relevant Bloch-Kato exponential, one recovers Mochizuki’s multiplicative approach from mine. The second important point of departure is that I work with products of norms (i.e. absolute values) in ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -Banach spaces (in the case of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}},{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} , these spaces are finite dimensional over ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} ) while Mochizuki works with volumes in tensor product of finite dimensional ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -vector spaces. In the p p -adic context, translation invariance of p p -adic Haar measure (on a p p -adic field E E ) means volumes are related to absolute values (i.e. Vol E ​ ( α + β ​ O E ) = | β | E {\rm Vol}_{E}({\alpha+\beta{O}_{E}})=\left|\beta\right|_{E} ). This observation can be used to calculate (suitably weighted) volumes of tensor product regions of the type β 1 O E 1 ⊗ ℤ p β 2 O E 2 ⊗ ⋯ ⊗ ℤ p β n O E n \beta_{1}{O}_{E_{1}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\beta_{2}{O}_{E_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\beta_{n}{O}_{E_{n}} , which are contained in the set Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} considered in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] , by means of the cross-norm property of tensor product norm, available in the context of the present paper, asserts that | v ⊗ w | V ⊗ W = | v | V ⋅ | w | W \left|v\otimes w\right|_{V\otimes W}=\left|v\right|_{V}\cdot\left|w\right|_{W} and allowing one to relate volumes to absolute values (at least for the tensor product regions of the above type). This allows me to translate my inequalities (established using products of absolute values) to Mochizuki’s tensor product volume inequality asserted in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] . On the other hand, the point of [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] is that the volume of the region occurring in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] may be bounded from above by more direct means (complementing the lower bound given by [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] ). Let me also remark, at this juncture, that a proof of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] may also be obtained by combining [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] and the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper), and Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 . However, that is not a path I have chosen to follow here. § 1.15 An outline of this paper Here is a brief outline of this paper. A parallel reading of the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper) is strongly recommended. In § 2 , I setup the basic frame work of holomorphoids (expanding on [ Joshi, 2023a ] ) and demonstrate the existence of distinct scheme theories (this is asserted in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] ) and specialize quickly to holomorphoids of elliptic curves over number fields. In § 3 , I introduce the global data which is called an Initial Theta Data in [ Mochizuki, 2021a ] . § 4 is dedicated to the construction of certain correspondences which are global in nature (local constructions are in [ Joshi, 2023b ] ) and referred to in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] as Θ g ​ a ​ u \Theta_{gau} -Links. In § 5 , I recall some rings of p p -adic Hodge Theory. § 8 provides a ‘Rosetta Stone’ (Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 ) for translating between [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] and the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper). In § 6 , § 9 , I provide a detailed construction of the many flavors Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ \widetilde{\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} , Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} and Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} of the fundamental theta-values set required for [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] . In § 7 , the fundamental estimate i.e. [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] is established for my version Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ \widetilde{\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} of this set and in § 9 , the fundamental estimate is established for Mochizuki’s version Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} and also Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} (this is Corollary 9.11.1.1 ). In § 10 , I establish the claims of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] regarding Frobenioids and Hodge-Theaters especially the existence of many distinct Hodge-Theaters of each type considered in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . In § 11 , I provide a comparison of perfect Frobenioids and perfectoids. In § 12 , I provide a formulation (with proof) of the geometric case of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] in Theorem 12.17.1 . § 1.16 Acknowledgments I thank Taylor Dupuy for reminding me to clarify the relationship between holomorphic hulls in the sense of [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] and convex closures which I use here; Mochizuki has also reminded me of the same point in his recent comments . This is addressed in Proposition 9.10.8.1 . I have also addressed another of Mochizuki’s concern about clarifying the global aspects of my theory (see § 1.7 for details). I thank Peter Scholze for some conversations surrounding this paper. 2 Holomorphoids of a variety over a number field In this paper, I will freely use the results of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] and familiarity with the ideas of [ Joshi, 2019 , Joshi, 2020a ] will also be useful. Familiarity with classical Teichmuller Theory (for example the basic texts [ Imayoshi and Taniguchi, 1992 ] or [ Nag, 1988 ] or [ Lehto, 1987 ] ) is absolutely essential in understanding the above papers–especially an understanding of moduli of curves will not be adequate. The terms anabelomorphism , amphoric and unamphoric , which were introduced in [ Joshi, 2020a , § ] and, will be used here throughout. § 2.1 Holomorphoids The global arithmetic input for the present theory is developed in [ Joshi, 2023a ] and that is a starting point for the present paper. However, I will not recall the definition of Arithmetic Teichmuller Spaces given in [ Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a , Joshi, 2021a ] but work with points of such spaces and these (points) will be precisely defined below and called (global/local) holomorphoids. Let L L be a number field with no real embeddings (this is a simplifying assumption and not a technical restriction). I will use the theory of arithmeticoids of a number field developed in [ Joshi, 2023a ] . For the definitions, notations and the basic properties of arithmeticoids, the reader is referred to [ Joshi, 2023a ] . Especially, parallel reading of [ Joshi, 2023a ] is highly recommended. The adelic Fargues-Fontaine curves 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} were introduced in [ Joshi, 2023a , § ] and these curves and their properties will also be used here throughout. I will also assume throughout this paper that all arithmeticoids 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} are normalized i.e. 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 = 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 n ​ o ​ r \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}=\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}^{nor} [ Joshi, 2023a , § ] i.e. the valuations on each component of 𝐲 {\bf y} are normalized so that the Artin-Whaples product formula ( [ Lang, ] ) holds for each x ∈ L ∗ x\in L^{*} : (2.1.1) ∏ v ∈ 𝕍 L | x | K v = 1 . \prod_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}}\left|x\right|_{K_{v}}=1. An important point demonstrated in [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] is that 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} is equipped with a certain global arithmetic period mapping which is given by the validity of the product formula ( 2.1.1 ) in each arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 n ​ o ​ r \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}^{nor} . As pointed out in [ Joshi, 2023a , Remark ] , the global arithmetic period mapping is non-constant. The validity of the product formula in a normalized arithmeticoid and the global arithmetic period mapping are central in understanding the global arithmetic aspects of this theory. The relationship between the main construction of this paper and the global arithmetic period mapping of [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] is established here in Theorem 4.6.1 . The following definition is motivated by [ Joshi, 2022 , Definition ] , [ Joshi, 2023a ] , and holomorphoid defined in it is a point of the global Arithmetic Teichmuller Space [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] and each local holomorphoid defined below is a point of the local Arithmetic Teichmuller Space of [ Joshi, 2022 , Definition ] (see Lemma 2.1.6 ). Definition 2.1.2 . A holomorphoid of a scheme over a number field , denoted ( X , L ↪ 𝔞 𝔯 𝔦 𝔱 𝔥 ( L ) 𝐲 ) (X,L\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}) (or simply ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}) ), consists of an arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} of a number field L L (with 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 {\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} ) (i.e. one given is a choice of an arithmeticoid 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 {\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} ), and a scheme X / L X/L over L L (viewed as a scheme X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} i.e. as a scheme over L L referencing the arithmetic of L L given by 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} (see [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] ). Write 𝐲 = ( y v ) v ∈ 𝕍 L ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 {\bf y}=(y_{v})_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} , then for each v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} , one is also given a morphism of analytic spaces (2.1.3) M ⁡ ( K y v ) → X L v a ​ n . {M}(K_{y_{v}})\to X^{an}_{L_{v}}. Morphisms of holomorphoids may be defined in the obvious way ( [ Joshi, 2023a ] ). Owing to ( 2.1.3 ), one may think of a holomorphoid as a pointed object and I will use the term pointed holomorphoid when one wants to emphasize this aspect of a holomorphoid. One may also consider holomorphoids ( X , L ↪ 𝔞 𝔯 𝔦 𝔱 𝔥 ( L ) 𝐲 ) (X,L\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}) with 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 𝐦 ​ 𝐚 ​ 𝐱 {\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L}^{max} (resp. 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 ℝ {\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L}^{{\mathbb{R}}} ) which are constructed in [ Joshi, 2023a , Definition ] (resp. [ Joshi, 2023a , Definition ] ). ∙ \bullet Remark 2.1.4 . (1) As will be seen in Lemma 2.1.6 , each local holomorphoid given by a holomorphoid ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}) gives a point of the local Arithmetic Teichmuller Space constructed in [ Joshi, 2022 ] , the holomorphoid ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}) gives a point of the adelic Teichmuller space of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 ] , while 𝐲 {\bf y} is a point of 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} constructed in [ Joshi, 2023a ] which refers to a specific avatar or deformation of the arithmetic of L L . Proof . This is immediate from [ Joshi, 2022 , Definition ] and [ Joshi, 2021a ] . Remark 2.1.7 . For connecting with [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] , let me remark that the passage to tempered fundamental groups is facilitated by geometric base-points at v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} provided by any holomorphoid 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}} of X / L X/L via Definition 2.1.5 (2) . One can also compute the geometric tempered fundamental group by picking a morphism M ⁡ ( K y v ) → X L v a ​ n {M}(K_{y_{v}})\to X^{an}_{L_{v}} which factors as M ⁡ ( K y v ) → X K y v a ​ n → X L v a ​ n {M}(K_{y_{v}})\to X^{an}_{K_{y_{v}}}\to X^{an}_{L_{v}} . ∙ \bullet Remark 2.1.8 . Let me point out the analogy with classical Teichmuller Theory. Let Σ \Sigma be a compact Riemann surface of genus g ≥ 1 g\geq 1 . Let T Σ T_{\Sigma} be its Teichmuller space. Let | Σ | \left|\Sigma\right| be the topological space underlying Σ \Sigma . Then a holomorphoid of | Σ | \left|\Sigma\right| is a point Σ ′ ∈ T Σ \Sigma^{\prime}\in T_{\Sigma} of the Teichmuller space of Σ \Sigma . Notably, a holomorphoid of | Σ | \left|\Sigma\right| comes equipped with its own complex analytic space Σ ′ \Sigma^{\prime} with | Σ ′ | = | Σ | \left|\Sigma^{\prime}\right|=\left|\Sigma\right| i.e. a complex (holomorphic) structure on | Σ | \left|\Sigma\right| . As is seen from Lemma 2.1.6 , in this analogy, each (global) holomorphoid (resp. local holomorphoid) defined in Definition 2.1.2 is a point of the global Arithmetic Teichmuller Space (resp. local Arithmetic Teichmuller Space) of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 ] . ∙ \bullet Remark 2.1.9 . This is an elaboration of [ Joshi, 2022 , Remark ] and will be useful to readers familiar with the Theory of Diamonds [ Scholze, 2017 ] . Let X / L X/L be as in Definition 2.1.2 . Let 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 {\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} . Let v ∈ 𝕍 L n ​ o ​ n v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}^{non} and let p v p_{v} be the residue characteristic of L v L_{v} . Let X L v ◇ X^{\Diamond}_{L_{v}} be the diamond associated by [ Scholze, 2017 , Def. 15.5] with the analytic adic space X L v a ​ d X_{L_{v}}^{ad} associated to X / L v X/L_{v} and let | X L v ◇ | \left|X^{\Diamond}_{L_{v}}\right| denote the underlying topological space of X L v ◇ X^{\Diamond}_{L_{v}} . Then by [ Joshi, 2022 , Remark ] the set of equivalence classes of local holomorphoids of X / L v X/L_{v} i.e. the set { 𝔥 𝔬 𝔩 ( X / L ) y v } / ∼ \left\{\mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{y_{v}}\right\}/\sim (essentially this set is the local Arithmetic Teichmuller Space of [ Joshi, 2022 , Definition ] ) is formally similar and closely related with the set X L v ◇ ​ ( ℂ p v ♭ ) X^{\Diamond}_{L_{v}}({\mathbb{C}}_{p_{v}}^{\flat}) [ Scholze, 2017 , Def. 15.5] . The projection { 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) y v } → y v \left\{\mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{y_{v}}\right\}\to y_{v} corresponds to the projection | X L v ◇ | → | Spd ⁡ ( L v ) | . \left|X^{\Diamond}_{L_{v}}\right|\to\left|{\rm Spd}(L_{v})\right|. The adelic Arithmetic Teichmuller Space of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] deals with the following sort of object: ∏ v ∈ 𝕍 L | X L v ◇ | → ∏ v ∈ 𝕍 L | Spd ⁡ ( L v ) | , \prod_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}}\left|X^{\Diamond}_{L_{v}}\right|\to\prod_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}}\left|{\rm Spd}(L_{v})\right|, which is roughly corresponds to the projection { 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 : 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 } → 𝐘 𝐋 \left\{\mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}}:{\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L}\right\}\to\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} given by 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 ⟼ 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}}\longmapsto{\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} . At the moment there is no good global geometric theory of objects of the form ∏ v ∈ 𝕍 L X L v ◇ . \prod_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}}X^{\Diamond}_{L_{v}}. In the present series of papers one considers this type of object subject to the global symmetries considered in [ Joshi, 2023a ] acting on the base. This complicated object is the type of dynamical arena of Arithmetic Teichmuller Spaces considered in the present series of papers. ∙ \bullet § 2.2 Category of holomorphoids If 𝐲 , 𝐲 ′ ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 {\bf y},{\bf y}^{\prime}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} and if ( X , L ↪ 𝔞 𝔯 𝔦 𝔱 𝔥 ( L ) 𝐲 ) (X,L\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}}) and ( X ′ , L ↪ 𝔞 𝔯 𝔦 𝔱 𝔥 ( L ) 𝐲 ′ ) (X^{\prime},L\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}^{\prime}}) are holomophoids of schemes X , X ′ X,X^{\prime} over L L , then one can talk about morphisms between holomorphoids ( X ′ , L ↪ 𝔞 𝔯 𝔦 𝔱 𝔥 ( L ) 𝐲 ) → ( X , L ↪ 𝔞 𝔯 𝔦 𝔱 𝔥 ( L ) 𝐲 ′ ) (X^{\prime},L\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}})\to(X,L\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}^{\prime}}) in the obvious way. So one has a natural category of holomorphoids of schemes over arithmeticoids of L L . This category comes equipped with the projection ( X , L ↪ 𝔞 𝔯 𝔦 𝔱 𝔥 ( L ) 𝐲 ) ⟼ 𝐲 (X,L\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}})\longmapsto{\bf y} . § 2.3 Holomorphoids and the existence of distinct scheme theories [ The purpose of this section is to establish Mochizuki’s assertion [ Mochizuki, 2021a , § I3] about existence of distinct scheme theories. ] Fix an arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} . Note that, given a holomorphoid ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}) , one can consider the pair ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) s ​ c ​ h (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y})^{sch} obtained by forgetting the geometric base-point datum (given by Definition 2.1.5 ) of the holomorphoid ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}) . The meaning of this notation ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) s ​ c ​ h (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y})^{sch} is as follows: ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) s ​ c ​ h (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y})^{sch} is the scheme X X viewed as a scheme over L L with the arithmetic of L L specified by 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} (in the sense of [ Joshi, 2023a ] ). I will refer to ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) s ​ c ​ h (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y})^{sch} as a scheme over the arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} . One way to think about ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) s ​ c ​ h (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y})^{sch} is that it is a scheme over L L equipped with its private version of the arithmetic of L L (and L ¯ , { L v , L ¯ v } v ∈ 𝕍 L \overline{L},\{L_{v},\overline{L}_{v}\}_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}} ) using which many familiar quantities and structures associated to X X (such as Galois groups, fundamental groups) may be computed. (The holomorphoid ( X , L ↪ 𝔞 𝔯 𝔦 𝔱 𝔥 ( L ) 𝐲 ) (X,L\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y}) , which gives to ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) s ​ c ​ h (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y})^{sch} , also comes equipped with the same private copy of arithmetic. For a fixed 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} , the category, denoted 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathcal{Sch}/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}} , with objects ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ) s ​ c ​ h (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y})^{sch} (and natural morphisms) is equivalent to the category of schemes over L L (by forgetting 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} ). I will refer to 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathcal{Sch}/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}} as category of schemes over the arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} . Note that in general, for 𝐲 ≠ 𝐲 ′ {\bf y}\neq{\bf y}^{\prime} in 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} , there may be no canonical or natural equivalence between the categories 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathcal{Sch}/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}} and 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ′ \mathcal{Sch}/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}^{\prime}} which respects the structures presented by the arithmeticoids 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}} and 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ′ \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}^{\prime}} . More precisely: By [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] , two arithmeticoids 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}} and 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 ′ \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}^{\prime}} may not even be (topologically) equivalent, one may think of { 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 } 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 \left\{\mathcal{Sch}/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}}\right\}_{{\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L}} as providing distinct or (topologically) inequivalent models of the category 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / L \mathcal{Sch}/L of schemes over L L . [This type of assertion occurs without proof in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , § I2] . Notably, [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] frequently refers to distinct miniature models of scheme theory, but omits a precise quantification of these notions.] If one forgets the information of the relevant arithmeticoids, then these categories are, of course, all equivalent. Let me summarize this discussion in the following way: Theorem 2.3.1 . Each member of { 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 } 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 \left\{\mathcal{Sch}/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}}\right\}_{{\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L}} is a category equivalent, via the forgetful functor, to the category of 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / L \mathcal{Sch}/L . But given two distinct arithmeticoids 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 1 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{1}} and 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 2 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{2}} , there may be no equivalence between 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 1 \mathcal{Sch}/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{1}} and 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 2 \mathcal{Sch}/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{2}} which is compatible with the two arithmeticoids. Remark 2.3.2 . Thus the fiber over 𝐲 {\bf y} is 𝒮 ​ 𝒸 ​ 𝒽 / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathcal{Sch}/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}} . Symmetries acting on 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} , detailed in [ Joshi, 2023a ] , move the fibers around. If one wants to work with schemes (as [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] does) then one must work with the fibered category 𝓢 ​ 𝓬 ​ 𝓱 L → 𝐘 𝐋 \boldsymbol{\mathcal{Sch}}_{L}\to\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} together with symmetries of the base category 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} acting on objects of 𝓢 ​ 𝓬 ​ 𝓱 L \boldsymbol{\mathcal{Sch}}_{L} . Presently, I do not know of any general formulation of the theory of fibered categories equipped with symmetry actions given by their respective base categories. ∙ \bullet Remark 2.3.4 . Let me remark that for dim ( X ) = 1 \dim(X)=1 and the genus of X X is g = 1 g=1 (this is the case of interest in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] ), then in this case, the notion of holomorphoids is a more precise version of Mochizuki’s (decorated) Hodge-Theaters. More precisely, given a holomorphoid 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}} , one may construct from it a decorated Hodge-Theater (for each decoration for Hodge-Theaters used in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] ) (for more on Hodge-Theaters see § 10 ). In the context of Mochizuki’s Hodge-Theaters (with a fixed decoration), the role of an arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} is played by a global Frobenioid of L L and the role of the functor considered in Equation 2.3.3 is given by the association { H : H ​ a fixed decoration Hodge Theater } → the global Frobenioid of ​ L ​ given by ​ H . \left\{H:H\text{ a fixed decoration Hodge Theater}\right\}\to\text{the global Frobenioid of }L\text{ given by }H. For details see § 10.11 . ∙ \bullet § 2.4 Holomorphoids of elliptic curves From now on, I will assume that C C is an elliptic curve over an arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) \mathfrak{arith}(L) of L L and write X = C − { O } X=C-\{O\} where O ∈ C ⁡ ( L ) O\in C(L) is the origin of the group law. Observe that X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) X/\mathfrak{arith}(L) is an hyperbolic curve of topological type ( g , n ) = ( 1 , 1 ) (g,n)=(1,1) . I will refer to C / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) C/\mathfrak{arith}(L) (resp. X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) X/\mathfrak{arith}(L) ) a holomorphoid of the elliptic curve C / L C/L (resp. a holomorphoid of the canonically punctured elliptic curve X / L X/L ). To apply the results of my theory (of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] ) to the context of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] , the following will be useful: Theorem 2.4.1 . Let 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( C / L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(C/L)_{{\bf y}} and 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}} be (pointed) holomorphoids of C / L C/L and X / L X/L respectively. Then one has (using the geometric base-points of these holomorphoids to compute absolute Galois groups and various fundamental groups): (1) X / L X/L is a hyperbolic curve of strict Belyi Type. (2) One has for each v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} (a) a surjection of topological groups Π X / L v ; K v t ​ e ​ m ​ p → G L v , K v . \Pi^{\scriptscriptstyle temp}_{X/L_{v};K_{v}}\to G_{L_{v},K_{v}}. (b) Let L ¯ v ⊂ K v \overline{L}_{v}\subset K_{v} be the algebraic closure of L v ⊂ K v L_{v}\subset K_{v} . Then one also has the monoid of non-zero elements of L ¯ v \overline{L}_{v} equipped with natural action of Galois: Π X / L v , K v t ​ e ​ m ​ p → G L v , K v ​ ↷ ​ L ¯ v ∗ , \Pi^{\scriptscriptstyle temp}_{X/L_{v},K_{v}}\to G_{L_{v},K_{v}}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\overline{L}_{v}^{*}, and, (c) the monoid of units of O L ¯ v ∗ {O}_{\overline{L}_{v}}^{*} of O L ¯ v {O}_{\overline{L}_{v}} equipped with its natural action of Galois: Π X / L v , K v t ​ e ​ m ​ p → G L v , K v ​ ↷ ​ O L ¯ v ∗ , \Pi^{\scriptscriptstyle temp}_{X/L_{v},K_{v}}\to G_{L_{v},K_{v}}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}{O}_{\overline{L}_{v}}^{*}, and, (d) the monoid O L ¯ v ⊳ = O L ¯ v − { 0 } {O}_{\overline{L}_{v}}^{\triangleright}={O}_{\overline{L}_{v}}-\{0\} of non-zero elements of O L ¯ v {O}_{\overline{L}_{v}} equipped with natural action of Galois: Π X / L v , K v t ​ e ​ m ​ p → G L v , K v ​ ↷ ​ O L ¯ v ⊳ . \Pi^{\scriptscriptstyle temp}_{X/L_{v},K_{v}}\to G_{L_{v},K_{v}}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}{O}_{\overline{L}_{v}}^{\triangleright}. (3) If X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 1 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{1}} and X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 2 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{2}} are two distinct holomorphoids of X / L X/L then the objects provided by (1)–(2) corresponding to X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 1 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{1}} and X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 2 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{2}} are all abstractly isomorphic but are equipped with distinct arithmetic labels and their arithmetic and geometric properties are quantifiably distinct from each other. Proof . This is an elementary consequence of the results of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] . Remark 2.4.2 . Following remarks will be useful: (1) As is established in § 8 , § 10 , all other objects of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] such as Frobenioids, Hodge-Theaters (of all types), prime-strips etc. can be obtained from every holomorphoid X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} considered here. (2) In [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] , Mochizuki has argued that the logic of his theory ( [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] ) requires treating two isomorphs as being distinct i.e. the logic of the theory is to work with Isomorph A and Isomorph B as opposed to Isomorph A or Isomorph B. The assertion Theorem 2.4.1 (3) (proved in the present paper) provides a clear mathematical reason for doing so, namely, these objects (arising from X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 1 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{1}} and X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 2 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{2}} ) while being (abstractly) isomorphic, they can be treated as distinct because they do indeed have distinct arithmetic and geometric properties. (3) To put it clearly, in my approach, one works with X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 1 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{1}} and X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 2 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{2}} for natural reasons (they are quantifiably distinct, but isomorphic, objects in the theory). No realignment of logic is needed to validate my theory. Moreover, my work provides distinct objects even for [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . Readers should recognize that my work provides the most natural and mathematically precise formulation of the theory created in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] and is fully compatible with the mathematical portions of [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] . ∙ \bullet 3 Fixing the Initial Theta Data à la Mochizuki Let me remark that the main theorems of this paper will require the existence of global arithmetic data called Initial Theta Data (as in the title of this section). The existence of the Initial Theta Data is established in [ Joshi, 2024a , Theorem ] (and in [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] ), so all the principal results of this paper will require the existence of the Initial Theta Data whose definition and properties are detailed in this section. Importantly, the existence of the Initial Theta Data ( [ Joshi, 2024a , Theorem ] ) is a global assertion i.e. requiring one to work over a number field and it cannot be established by local means. From now on, the following assumptions will be in force. These assumptions essentially corresponds to fixing the Initial Theta Data in [ Mochizuki, 2021a ] : § 3.1 Basic Hypotheses (1) Let L / ℚ L/{\mathbb{Q}} be a number field such that L L has no real embeddings. (2) Let X / L X/L be an hyperbolic curve of Strict Belyi Type obtained from a geometrically connected, elliptic curve C / L C/L by setting X = ( C − { O } ) / L X=(C-\{O\})/L , where O ∈ C ⁡ ( L ) O\in C(L) is the origin of the group law of C C . (3) Fix an algebraic closure L ¯ \overline{L} of L L and write G L = Gal ⁡ ( L ¯ / L ) G_{L}={\rm Gal}(\overline{L}/L) . (4) Let L mod L_{\rm mod} be the field of moduli of X / L X/L . If one needs to emphasize the field one is working with, then I will write X L , X L mod X_{L},X_{L_{\rm mod}} , X L ′ X_{L^{\prime}} etc. Frequently one will need to work with all the three simultaneously. § 3.2 Notational conventions I will also fix some notation regarding valuations of L L and L mod L_{\rm mod} : (5) Let 𝕍 L \mathbb{V}_{L} (resp. 𝕍 L mod \mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}} ) be the sets of equivalence classes of non-archimedean valuations of L L (resp. L mod L_{\rm mod} ). (6) Let 𝕍 L mod o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s \mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}}^{odd,ss} be the set of primes of L mod L_{\rm mod} with odd residue characteristics at which X X has bad, semi-stable reduction i.e. split multiplicative reduction. (7) Let 𝕍 L mod g ​ o ​ o ​ d = 𝕍 L mod − 𝕍 L mod o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s \mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}}^{good}=\mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}}-\mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}}^{odd,ss} . (8) One can also define similar subsets of valuations of L L . The following lemma is immediate from the definitions above: Lemma 3.2.1 . With the above assumptions on X X and L L , and notational conventions one has (1) w ∈ 𝕍 L mod g ​ o ​ o ​ d w\in\mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}}^{good} if and only if w | 2 w|2 or X X has good reduction at w w or has bad additive reduction at w w . (2) For any w | 2 w|2 lying in 𝕍 L mod g ​ o ​ o ​ d \mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}}^{good} , X X can have good or bad (additive or multiplicative) reduction at w w . § 3.3 Initial Theta-data Now to the assumptions of § 3.1 , let me add the following. Assume in addition to the assumptions of 3.1 that (9) L / L mod L/L_{\rm mod} is a Galois extension, (10) The field L L contains all points of order 2 ⋅ 3 = 6 2\cdot 3=6 of C / L C/L i.e. L ​ ( C ​ [ 6 ] ​ ( L ¯ ) ) = L L(C[6](\overline{L}))=L . (14) Fix a bijection 𝕍 L mod ≃ 𝕍 ¯ ⊂ 𝕍 L ′ \mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}}\simeq\underline{\mathbb{V}}\subset\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}} . Remark 3.3.1 . Let w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss} be a prime of L ′ L^{\prime} lying over a prime v ∈ 𝕍 L o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}^{odd,ss} of L L . Then C / L w ′ C/L^{\prime}_{w} has a non-trivial point of exact order 2 ⋅ ℓ 2\cdot\ell defined over L w ′ L^{\prime}_{w} i.e. C ⁡ [ 2 ⋅ ℓ ] ​ ( L w ′ ) ≠ 0 . C[2\cdot\ell](L^{\prime}_{w})\neq 0. In particular, if w | v w|v for a prime v | p v|p of L L , then the Tate parameter of C / L v C/L_{v} has an 2 ​ ℓ t ​ h 2\ell^{th} -root in L w ′ L^{\prime}_{w} . The construction of the theta-values locii in § 6 , § 9 will require the existence of a point of exact order 2 ​ ℓ 2\ell . § 3.4.2 Elementary bound For use later on, when one wants to vary C / L C/L , let me record the following elementary consequence of the assumptions § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 . Lemma 3.4.2.1 . Let L L be a number field, L ¯ \overline{L} be a fixed algebraic closure of L L . For any 0 < A ∈ ℝ 0<A\in{\mathbb{R}} , let L ≤ A ⊂ L ¯ L^{\leq A}\subset\overline{L} be the smallest subfield containing all finite extensions L ′ / L L^{\prime}/L of degrees [ L ′ : L ] ≤ A . [L^{\prime}:L]\leq A. Let ℓ \ell be a fixed odd prime number. Suppose that C / L C/L is an elliptic curve equipped with data § 3.1 . Then as C / L C/L varies over M 1 , 1 ​ ( L ) {M}_{1,1}(L) the data § 3.1 for C / L C/L provide a family of finite extensions L ′ / L L^{\prime}/L (as above) (i.e. L ′ L^{\prime} is the fixed field of ker ⁡ ( ρ C / L ; ℓ ) \ker(\rho_{C/L;\ell}) ) which are contained in L ≤ A L^{\leq A} with A = | G ​ L 2 ​ ( ℤ / ℓ ) | ≪ ℓ 4 A=|GL_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell)|\ll\ell^{4} . Proof . Note that L ≤ A / L L^{\leq A}/L is an extension of infinite degree. The proof is clear. The degree of the fixed field of ker ⁡ ( ρ C / L ; ℓ ) \ker(\rho_{C/L;\ell}) is bounded by the order of the Galois group of L ′ / L L^{\prime}/L and by construction of L ′ / L L^{\prime}/L , one has Gal ⁡ ( L ′ / L ) ⊂ G ​ L 2 ​ ( ℤ / ℓ ) {\rm Gal}(L^{\prime}/L)\subset GL_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell) and as ℓ \ell is fixed, the latter group has bounded order A = | G ​ L 2 ​ ( ℤ / ℓ ) | ≪ ℓ 4 A=|GL_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell)|\ll\ell^{4} . § 3.5 Existence of Initial Theta Data Let me say this clearly: the existence of Initial Theta Data [ Joshi, 2024a , Theorem ] is a global assertion i.e. requiring one to work over a number field and with compactly bounded subsets of algebraic points and this existence cannot be proved by p p -adic local means (even for all primes). This existence theorem is necessary for the central constructions and results of the present paper (and [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] ) and hence also for [ Joshi, 2024a ] (and [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] ). The existence of Initial Theta Data satisfying § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 and some additional properties is a global arithmetic assertion established in [ Joshi, 2024a , Theorem ] (resp. in [ Mochizuki, 2021d , Corollary 2.2] ). For ease of comparison between my papers and Mochizuki’s, I have preserved Mochizuki’s content wise order of appearance of results: this means that results which appear in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] appear in this paper and results of [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] appear in [ Joshi, 2024a ] . This does not necessarily reflect the logical dependence of results in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] on the existence of Initial Theta Data which is proved in [ Mochizuki, 2021d , Corollary 2.2] (note that the key Diophantine estimate [ Mochizuki, 2021d , Theorem 1.10] also depends, among other critical assumptions, on the existence of Initial Theta Data). In what follows, I will assume the existence of Initial Theta Data satisfying § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 for the rest of this paper. 4 Construction of Mochizuki’s Adelic Ansatz and Adelic Theta-links In this section I want to provide the construction of the adelic version of certain correspondences (introduced in [ Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] ), which in the terminology of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , are called Θ g ​ a ​ u \Theta_{gau} -links. The construction provided here is the adelic version of the fundamental constructions of [ Joshi, 2023b ] where I gave the construction of these objects for one fixed prime of a number field. § 4.1 The variant 𝐘 𝐋 ′ \mathscrbf{Y}_{L}^{\prime} of 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} Let L L be a number field, which will be assumed to have no real embeddings. Let C / L C/L and X / L X/L be chosen as above and fix an Initial Theta Data § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 . Let L ′ ⊃ L L^{\prime}\supset L be the number field provided by the Initial Theta Data. Since L L has no real embeddings, neither does L ′ L^{\prime} . Let 𝐘 𝐋 ′ \mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}} be the adelic curve defined in [ Joshi, 2023a , § ] using the field L ′ L^{\prime} . In fact it is more convenient to work with the variant 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ \mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime} , defined in [ Joshi, 2023a , Remark ] , of 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} (the latter is defined in [ Joshi, 2023a , Definition ] ). This means that 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ \mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime} is the topological space (4.1.1) 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ = ∏ 𝐩 ∏ 𝐰 | 𝐩 | 𝐘 ℂ 𝐩 ♭ , 𝐋 𝐰 ′ | , \mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime}=\prod_{p}\prod_{w|p}\left|{Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},L_{w}^{\prime}}\right|, the product over p p runs over all rational primes including p = ∞ p=\infty . This is possible because, for every rational prime p p , one has an inclusion of valued fields L ¯ ^ w ′ ⊂ ℂ p \widehat{\bar{L}}_{w}^{\prime}\subset{\mathbb{C}}_{p} for every w | p w|p . I will define the adelic version of Mochizuki’s Ansatz (see [ Joshi, 2023b ] for the local version). Theorem 4.2.2.1 . Let L L be a number field, let C / L C/L and X / L X/L be as above ( § 2.4 ) and an Initial Theta Data § 3.1 , § 3.3 be fixed. Then the set Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} constructed above ( 4.2.3 ) has the following properties. Let 𝐆 L ′ \mathbf{G}_{L^{\prime}} be the group defined in [ Joshi, 2023a , § ] (for L ′ L^{\prime} ). Let Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} be the set constructed above. (1) The set Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} is stable under the natural action of 𝐆 L ′ \mathbf{G}_{L^{\prime}} on ( 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ ) ℓ ∗ \left(\mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)^{\ell^{*}} which is given using the natural action of 𝐆 L ′ \mathbf{G}_{L^{\prime}} on 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ \mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime} (constructed in [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] ). (2) The set Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} is stable under the natural action of the (global) Frobenius 𝝋 \boldsymbol{\varphi} on ( 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ ) ℓ ∗ \left(\mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right)^{\ell^{*}} given using the natural action of the (global) Frobenius 𝝋 \boldsymbol{\varphi} on 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ \mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime} . (3) Hence Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} is also stable under the natural action of ( L ′ ) ∗ (L^{\prime})^{*} on 𝐘 𝐋 ′ \mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}} constructed in [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] . (4) Let ( 𝐲 1 ′ , 𝐲 2 ′ , … , 𝐲 ℓ ∗ ′ ) ∈ Σ ~ L ′ ({\bf y}^{\prime}_{1},{\bf y}_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,{\bf y}_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime})\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} , write, for j = 1 , 2 , … , ℓ ∗ j=1,2,\ldots,{\ell^{*}} , 𝐲 j ′ = ( y j , w ′ ) w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ {\bf y}_{j}^{\prime}=(y^{\prime}_{j,w})_{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}} . For each w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss} . Let ( y w , 1 ′ , y w , 2 ′ , … , y w , ℓ ∗ ′ ) ∈ Y ℂ p ♭ , L w ′ ℓ ∗ (y^{\prime}_{w,1},y^{\prime}_{w,2},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{w,{\ell^{*}}})\in{Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},L_{w}^{\prime}}^{\ell^{*}} be the w w -component of ( 𝐲 1 ′ , 𝐲 2 ′ , … , 𝐲 ℓ ∗ ′ ) ({\bf y}_{1}^{\prime},{\bf y}_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,{\bf y}_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}) . (5) Moreover for each w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ − 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}-\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss} one has the following relationship between their valuations: (4.2.2.3) | − | K y w , j ′ = | − | K y w , 1 ′ for j = 1 , 2 , … , ℓ ∗ . \left|-\right|_{K_{y_{w,j}^{\prime}}}=\left|-\right|_{K_{y_{w,1}^{\prime}}}\text{ for }j=1,2,\ldots,{\ell^{*}}. Proof . The first assertion is a consequence of [ Joshi, 2023b , Proposition ] and [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem ] . The second assertion (2) is immediate from [ Joshi, 2023b , Proposition ] and [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] . Let me give a proof of (3) . This can be proved along the lines of [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem ] , but let me deduce the present case as a consequence of [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem ] . To see this, let w | p w|p . Then one has a quasi-finite morphism ( 4.2.1.2 ) Y ℂ p ♭ , L w ′ → Y ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p {Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},L_{w}^{\prime}}\to{Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} . Let y p , j ∈ Y ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p y_{p,j}\in{Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} be the image of y w , j ′ y_{w,j}^{\prime} under this morphism and let ( y p , 1 , y p , 2 , … , y p , ℓ ∗ ) ∈ Σ ~ ℂ p ♭ ⊂ Y ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p ℓ ∗ (y_{p,1},y_{p,2},\ldots,y_{p,{\ell^{*}}})\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}}\subset{Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{\ell^{*}} be the corresponding point of Y ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p ℓ ∗ {Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{\ell^{*}} . In [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem ] , I have shown that ( 4.2.2.2 ) holds for the residue fields K y p , 1 , K y p , 2 , … , K y p , ℓ ∗ K_{y_{p,1}},K_{y_{p,2}},\ldots,K_{y_{p,{\ell^{*}}}} of y p , 1 , y p , 2 , … , y p , ℓ ∗ y_{p,1},y_{p,2},\ldots,y_{p,{\ell^{*}}} . By [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Proposition 2.3.20(3)] one has an isomorphism of local rings of y p , j y_{p,j} and y w , j ′ y^{\prime}_{w,j} for each j j . Hence ( 4.2.2.2 ) also holds for the residue fields K y w , 1 ′ , K y w , 2 ′ , … , K y w , ℓ ∗ ′ K_{y_{w,1}^{\prime}},K_{y_{w,2}^{\prime}},\ldots,K_{y_{w,{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}}} of y w , 1 ′ , y w , 2 ′ , … , y w , ℓ ∗ ′ y^{\prime}_{w,1},y^{\prime}_{w,2},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{w,{\ell^{*}}} . The last assertion is immediate from the definition of Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} . This completes the proof. The scaling relationship established in Theorem 4.2.2.1 (at the primes of semi-stable reduction) has fundamental consequences for global arithmetic. Mochizuki asserts the content of the next corollary as [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Theorem A(ii)] : Corollary 4.2.2.4 . Suppose that the notations and assumptions of Theorem 4.2.2.1 remain in force.
