Proof . This is clear from Theorem 4.2.2.1 and the global constraint placed by the requirement that the product formula holds. § 4.2.3 Mochizuki’s Ansatz is the set of Θ g ​ a ​ u \Theta_{gau} -Links in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] Definition 4.2.3.1 . Let 𝐲 0 ′ = ( y w ′ ) ∈ 𝕍 L ′ {\bf y}_{0}^{\prime}=(y_{w}^{\prime})_{\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}} be a standard point of 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ \mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime} . Remark 4.4.2 . Let me remark that there are countably infinite possible choices for the standard point 𝐲 0 {\bf y}_{0} (resp. 𝐲 0 ′ {\bf y}_{0}^{\prime} ). This is because the set of valuations 𝕍 L \mathbb{V}_{L} of any number field L L is countable and for each v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} , the set of possible choices of closed classical points y v y_{v} (chosen as above) is countable hence the set of 𝐲 0 = ( y v ) v ∈ 𝕍 L {\bf y}_{0}=(y_{v})_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}} as above is countable. However it is important to recognize that the choice of any of the y v y_{v} ’s, and hence of 𝐲 0 {\bf y}_{0} , is not unique. Any one of the countably many choices for 𝐲 0 {\bf y}_{0} are equally valid choices (for doing arithmetic and geometry). But, as has been established in [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a , Joshi, 2022 ] , each choice sees p p -adic arithmetic (and geometry) slightly differently relative to other possible choices and so one can average over such choices. ∙ \bullet § 4.5 The choice of a standard point of Mochizuki’s Ansatz Now I will choose a standard point of 𝐳 Θ ∈ Σ ~ L ′ \mathbf{z}_{\Theta}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} which one may think of as a standard Θ \Theta -Link (for the purposes of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] ). H_{{\bf y}_{j}}:\deg_{{\bf y}_{j}}((x)_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}})=\sum_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L}}\log\left|x\right|_{K_{y_{j,v}}}=0\qquad(\text{for all }x\in L^{*}). (4) The scaling of valuations by the factor j 2 j^{2} at primes of semi-stable reduction which occurs via Theorem 4.2.2.1 , together with the requirement that the global product formula holds for 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 j n ​ o ​ r \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{j}}^{nor} , means that the local valuations (given by an arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 j \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{j}} ) at primes of non-semistable reduction must also be suitably (re)normalized so that the product formula holds (this is given to us by Corollary 4.2.2.4 ). (5) As the product formula ( 2.1.1 ) holds for each normalized arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 j n ​ o ​ r \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{{\bf y}_{j}}^{nor} , the hyperplane H 𝐲 j H_{{\bf y}_{j}} is stable under multiplication by elements of L ∗ L^{*} . (6) Let ℙ ⁡ ( 𝒱 L ) {\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{V}_{L}) be the projective space of hyperplanes in 𝒱 L \mathcal{V}_{L} . Then one has a natural and non-constant function defined on Mochizuki’s Adelic Ansatz (§ 4.2 ): 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ℓ ∗ ⊇ 𝚺 ~ 𝐋 ′ → ∏ 𝐣 = 𝟏 ℓ ∗ ℙ ⁡ ( 𝒱 𝐋 , 𝐣 ) \mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\ell^{*}}\supseteq\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}}\to\prod_{j=1}^{\ell^{*}}{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{V}_{L,j}) given by Σ ~ L ′ ∋ 𝐳 = ( 𝐲 1 , … , 𝐲 ℓ ∗ ) ⟼ ( H 𝐲 1 , … , H 𝐲 ℓ ∗ ) \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}}\ni\mathbf{z}=({\bf y}_{1},\ldots,{\bf y}_{\ell^{*}})\longmapsto(H_{{\bf y}_{1}},\ldots,H_{{\bf y}_{\ell^{*}}}) . Then ℙ ⁡ ( Σ ~ L ′ ) {\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}}) is equipped with a natural action of all the groups and symmetries given by Theorem 6.3.1 which act up on Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} . Proof . This is clear from [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] , and the properties of Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} established so far and detailed in Theorem 4.2.2.1 , Corollary 4.2.2.4 . Remark 4.6.2 . (1) I expect that the above period mapping is continuous for a suitable topology on the codomain, but I hope to take this up in a separate paper. (2) Mochizuki’s discussion of the role of product formulas is in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Remark 3.9.6] , [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Remark 5.2.1] , [ Mochizuki, 2020 , Page 71] . (3) It is worth noting that in the context of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] Mochizuki considers (without a transparent proof) a version of this construction by means of the theory of realified Frobenioid ℱ ​ 𝓇 ​ ℴ ​ 𝒷 ​ ( L ) ℝ \mathcal{F\!r\!o\!b}(L)^{\mathbb{R}} of L L (see my discussion of this Frobenioid is in § 10 ). Specifically Mochizuki works with the tuple of realified Frobenioids ( ℱ ​ 𝓇 ​ ℴ ​ 𝒷 ​ ( L ) 1 ℝ , … , ℱ ​ 𝓇 ​ ℴ ​ 𝒷 ​ ( L ) ℓ ∗ ℝ ) (\mathcal{F\!r\!o\!b}(L)_{1}^{\mathbb{R}},\ldots,\mathcal{F\!r\!o\!b}(L)_{\ell^{*}}^{\mathbb{R}}) which provides, by the same degree mapping, the hyperplanes considered here. (4) The existence of the phenomenon and properties established by Theorem 4.6.1 is vital to establishing [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] . But this type of proposition is claimed without proof in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] , [ Tan, 2018 ] , [ Yamashita, 2019 ] . Notably, because of the scaling property Theorem 4.2.2.1 , the hyperplane H 𝐲 j H_{{\bf y}_{j}} comes equipped with a scaling factor of j 2 j^{2} (for appropriate coordinate v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} depending on the semi-stable elliptic curve as in [ Joshi, 2024b ] ). The lack of proof of this property in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] leads to the assertions of impossibility of this phenomenon in [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 , Section 2.2, Page 10] . ∙ \bullet 5 Some local preliminaries I will recall a few local preliminaries and constructions which are not covered in [ Joshi, 2023b ] but which will be used in the remainder of this paper. Let p p be a prime. Let ℚ ¯ p ⊃ ℚ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p}\supset{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} be an algebraic closure of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} . Let ℂ p {\mathbb{C}}_{p} be the completion of ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} , and equip ℂ p {\mathbb{C}}_{p} with its canonical action of the absolute Galois group G ℚ p G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} (computed using ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} ). The following result will be used throughout the rest of the paper. § 5.1 Basic rings of p p -adic Hodge Theory For any choice of an algebraic closure ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} one has the construction of p p -adic period rings of [ Fontaine, 1994a ] . Let B d ​ R + B_{dR}^{+} be the ring of p p -adic periods constructed in [ Fontaine, 1994a ] for the (Galois group, field) pair ( G ℚ p , ℚ p ) (G_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}) and let B d ​ R B_{dR} be its quotient field. By the results of several mathematicians, notably, Fontaine, Messing, Faltings and Tsuji, B d ​ R B_{dR} is the ring of de Rham periods of varieties finite type, and defined over finite extensions of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} contained in ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} . The construction of B d ​ R B_{dR} is functorial in the sense of [ Fontaine, 1994a , 1.5.6] . § 5.2 The ring B p = B ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p B_{p}=B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} Let B p = B ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p B_{p}=B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} be the ring constructed in [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Chapitre 2] ; if p p is clear from the context then, I will write B = B p B=B_{p} . By [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Théorème 6.5.2] and [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , 10.1.1] ), there exists an element t ∈ B t\in B which generates the maximal ideal of the canonical point of X ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} . From the point of view of modern p p -adic Hodge Theory (as detailed in [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 ] ), the ring B ⁡ [ 1 t ] B[\frac{1}{t}] is also quite natural. Let B p + = B ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p + B^{+}_{p}=B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}^{+} be the ring constructed in [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , 1.10] ; if p p is clear from the context then, I will write B + = B p + B^{+}=B_{p}^{+} . § 5.2.1 The ring B p + B_{p}^{+} Let me also remark that for the ring B p = B = B ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p B_{p}=B=B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} one has B ⊃ B + ⊃ W ⁡ ( O ℂ p ♭ ) ​ [ 1 / p ] B\supset B^{+}\supset W({O}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}})[1/p] and W ⁡ ( O ℂ p ♭ ) ​ [ 1 / p ] ⊃ W ⁡ ( 𝔽 ¯ p ) ​ [ 1 / p ] = ℚ p u ​ n ​ r ​ a ​ m W({O}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}})[1/p]\supset W({\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}})[1/p]={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{unram} (this is the maximal unramified extension of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} ) and so B p B_{p} and B p + B_{p}^{+} is naturally a module for ℚ p u ​ n ​ r ​ a ​ m {\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{unram} . Hence B B and B + B^{+} are E 0 E_{0} -algebras for any finite unramified extension E 0 / ℚ p E_{0}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} . This remark will be used in what follows. § 5.2.2 The rings B d ​ R + , B d ​ R B_{dR}^{+},B_{dR} By [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Théorème 6.5.2] , the ring B d ​ R + B_{dR}^{+} is the completion of the local ring of X ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} at the canonical point of X ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} and hence the ring B d ​ R + B_{dR}^{+} is a discrete valuation ring. Moreover the maximal ideal of this local ring is generated by the element t t and B d ​ R = B d ​ R + ​ [ 1 t ] B_{dR}=B_{dR}^{+}[\frac{1}{t}] . Remark 5.2.2.1 . On the other hand B B has uncountably many maximal ideals corresponding to closed classical points of Y ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p {Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} and my construction of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}} and Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i {\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} require using the fact that B B has infinitely many maximal ideals. ∙ \bullet § 5.2.3 The ring B E B_{E} Let E E be a p p -adic field and let E 0 ⊂ E E_{0}\subset E be its maximal unramified subfield. By [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Chap 1, 1.6.2 and Chap. 10] , the ring B E B_{E} associated to the datum ( F = ℂ p ♭ , E ) (F={\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E) is the ring B E = B ℂ p ♭ , E B_{E}=B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E} . Lemma 5.2.3.1 . Let E E be a p p -adic field and let E 0 ⊂ E E_{0}\subset E be the maximal unramified subfield of E E . Then one has a natural isomorphism (5.2.3.2) B E ≃ B ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p ⊗ E 0 E . B_{E}\simeq B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\otimes_{E_{0}}E. Proof . This is [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Proposition 1.6.9] . § 5.2.4 The rings B , B d ​ R B,B_{dR} The ring B = B ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p B=B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} is related to B d ​ R , B d ​ R + B_{dR},B_{dR}^{+} as follows. For a p p -adic field E E , let E 0 ⊂ E E_{0}\subset E denote the maximal unramified subfield of E E . Then by [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Proposition 10.2.7] one has an injection (for each finite extension E / ℚ p E/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} contained in ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} ). (5.2.4.1) B E = B ⊗ E 0 E ↪ B d ​ R . B_{E}=B\otimes_{E_{0}}E\hookrightarrow B_{dR}. In particular (5.2.4.2) B = B p ↪ B d ​ R . B=B_{p}\hookrightarrow B_{dR}. § 5.2.5 The ring B ⊗ ℚ p E B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E It will be useful to consider a ring which is bigger than the ring B E B_{E} ( Lemma 5.2.3.1 ) which is better suited for calculations. Consider (5.2.5.1) B ~ = B ⊗ ℚ p E \tilde{B}=B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E then by usual transitivity properties of tensor products with respect to the inclusions ℚ p ⊂ E 0 ⊂ E {\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\subset E_{0}\subset E and so one can write this ring as (5.2.5.2) B ⊗ ℚ p E = ( B ⊗ ℚ p E 0 ) ⊗ E 0 E B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E=(B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E_{0})\otimes_{E_{0}}E and since B B is also an E 0 E_{0} algebra, (5.2.5.3) B ⊗ ℚ p E 0 = B ⊕ ⋯ ⊕ B ⏞ [ E 0 : ℚ p ] f a c t o r s . At any rate, B ~ E = B ⊗ ℚ p E \tilde{B}_{E}=B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E is naturally a finite and free B = B ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p B=B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} -module through this identification. One also has the natural diagonal embedding (5.2.5.5) B E ↪ B ~ E = B ⊗ ℚ p E = B E ⊕ [ E 0 : ℚ p ] . B_{E}\hookrightarrow\tilde{B}_{E}=B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E=B_{E}^{\oplus[E_{0}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]}. § 5.2.6 The ring B e B_{e} Let me also introduce the ring [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Théorème 6.5.2] (5.2.6.1) B e = B + ​ [ 1 / t ] φ = 1 = B ​ [ 1 / t ] φ = 1 = B c ​ r ​ i ​ s φ = 1 . B_{e}=B^{+}[1/t]^{\varphi=1}=B[1/t]^{\varphi=1}=B_{cris}^{\varphi=1}. By [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Théorème 6.5.2] B e B_{e} can be identified with the affine coordinate ring of the curve X ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p − { x c ​ a ​ n } {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}-\{x_{can}\} i.e. X ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p − { x c ​ a ​ n } = Spec ⁡ ( B e ) {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}-\{x_{can}\}={\rm Spec}(B_{e}) . One also has, by [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Theorem 6.5.2] , the inclusions (5.2.6.2) B + \textstyle{B^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces} B c ​ r ​ i ​ s + \textstyle{B_{cris}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces} B c ​ r ​ i ​ s + ​ [ 1 / t ] = B c ​ r ​ i ​ s \textstyle{B_{cris}^{+}[1/t]=B_{cris}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces} B d ​ R . \textstyle{B_{dR}.} These inclusions will allow one to view the theta-value locus as living in each of these period rings. Direct construction of the theta-value locus in B c ​ r ​ i ​ s B_{cris} or B d ​ R B_{dR} does not seem to be possible. Remark 5.2.6.3 . By [ Fontaine, 1994a , Fontaine, 1994b ] , the rings B d ​ R ⊃ B c ​ r ​ i ​ s B_{dR}\supset B_{cris} depend on the choice of a fixed algebraic closure ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} . On the other hand, the rings B , B e , B E B,B_{e},B_{E} of [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 ] do not depend on such a choice. ∙ \bullet § 5.3 Enlarging subsets of B E ℓ ∗ B_{E}^{\ell^{*}} using Aut ⁡ ( G E ) {\rm Aut}(G_{E}) -action, Frobenius action and convex closures Let E E be a p p -adic field. Given a subset S S of the ring B E B_{E} , one can enlarge it in many natural ways. Let me review some of these enlargements which will be applied to obtain the final versions of the theta-values locii in various related rings. Suppose S ⊂ B E S\subset B_{E} (the considerations of this paragraph will be applied with E = E w ′ E=E_{w}^{\prime} for each relevant w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p} ). I will enlarge this set further as follows: § 5.3.1 Aut ⁡ ( G E ) {\rm Aut}(G_{E}) -enlargements Let S ⊂ B E S\subset B_{E} or B E ℓ ∗ B_{E}^{\ell^{*}} , consider the natural action of Aut ⁡ ( G E ) {\rm Aut}(G_{E}) on the data G E ​ ↷ ​ B E G_{E}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}B_{E} by pre-composing the action of G E G_{E} on B E B_{E} with σ ∈ Aut ⁡ ( G E ) \sigma\in{\rm Aut}(G_{E}) . More precisely, if σ ∈ Aut ⁡ ( G E ) \sigma\in{\rm Aut}(G_{E}) then consider the pre-composed or twisted action (5.3.1.1) G E ​ σ ​ G E ​ ↷ ​ B E G_{E}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.68303pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}G_{E}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}B_{E} and let S σ S^{\sigma} be the image of S S under this new action of G E G_{E} on B E B_{E} . This consideration can be applied verbatim to the action G E G_{E} -action on B E ℓ ∗ B_{E}^{\ell^{*}} and subsets in this context. So if S ⊂ B E ℓ ∗ S\subset B_{E}^{\ell^{*}} is any subset then one simply takes the union (5.3.1.2) ⋃ σ ∈ Aut ⁡ ( G E ) S σ ⊂ B E ℓ ∗ . \bigcup_{\sigma\in{\rm Aut}(G_{E})}S^{\sigma}\subset B_{E}^{\ell^{*}}. This provides a Galois enlargement of any subset S ⊂ B E ℓ ∗ S\subset B_{E}^{\ell^{*}} . Remark 5.3.1.3 . (1) By [ Joshi, 2020b ] , the Fargues-Fontaine curve X F , E {X}_{F,E} is not uniquely identified by its étale fundamental group–this group is isomorphic to the absolute Galois group G E G_{E} (this is the failure of the absolute Grothendieck conjecture for Fargues-Fontaine curves which is established in [ Joshi, 2020b ] ). (2) In terminology of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] , taking unions over the images under automorphisms of Aut ⁡ ( G E ) {\rm Aut}(G_{E}) is Mochizuki’s way of applying the indeterminacy Ind1 in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] (my discussion of this is in § 8.11 ). (3) In this sense Mochizuki’s indeterminacy Ind1 arises from the failure of the absolute Grothendieck conjecture for Fargues-Fontaine curves established in [ Joshi, 2020b ] . ∙ \bullet § 5.3.2 Frobenius stablization enlargement Another possible enlargement which can be applied is to replace S ⊂ B E S\subset B_{E} by (5.3.2.1) S ⟼ ⋃ n ∈ ℤ φ n ​ ( S ) , S\longmapsto\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\varphi^{n}(S), where φ \varphi is the Frobenius morphism of B E B_{E} . This obviously applies to subsets of B E ℓ ∗ B_{E}^{\ell^{*}} and variants. This is the Frobenius enlargement of S S . § 5.3.3 Convex closure enlargement Finally one can replace such a Galois and Frobenius enlarged set S S by the closure of its convex hull S ¯ \overline{S} in B E B_{E} with respect to the Frechet topology i.e. one can replace S S by the smallest, closed, convex subset containing S S . This set is closed and convex (see [ Schneider, 2002 , Chapter I] or [ Schikhof, 1984 ] for basic properties of convex sets in p p -adic Banach spaces). Remark 5.3.3.1 . My version of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] and Mochizuki’s version are both about lower bounds and it is important to recognize that enlargements (i.e. adding elements to the set S S ) do not affect lower bounds on the supremum of norms of elements of S S . On the other hand, on a suitably enlarged set, an upper bound may be relatively easier to obtain. This is the rationale for establishing [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] . ∙ \bullet 6 Construction of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i ? {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{?} Let L L , C / L C/L and X / L X/L be as assumed in § 2.4 and § 3.1 , § 3.3 . In this section, subject to these assumptions, I provide the construction of the theta-values locii Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i ? {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{?} (for suitable choice of decorations ? ? ) and Mochizuki’s set Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} (and its variant Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} ). The sets Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} and Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} are needed for the statements of the fundamental estimate i.e the statements of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] in the respective theories. The construction of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} is the central assertion of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] . One of the central difficulties which many readers have faced in ascertaining the veracity of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] has been the lack of a quantitative demonstration of the existence of distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures required for the construction of the set Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} which plays a fundamental role in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] . This difficulty has been overcome through the present series of papers: my demonstration of the existence of arithmetic holomorphic structures is in [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] and my construction of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} is in § 9 . This section provides a construction of a simpler subset Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} which is adequate for establishing [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] . From my point of view the existence of these locii is a consequence of the existence of arithmetic Teichmuller spaces and its various properties established in [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a , Joshi, 2023b ] (and the present paper). Mochizuki’s approach to the construction of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} (modulo the existence of arithmetic holomorphic structures and their properties) is detailed in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] and its proof. The core idea of the construction of these sets is the same as that of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] –namely one collates, in a suitably chosen ambient set, some arithmetic information (Tate parameters or more generally theta-values) arising from many different holomorphoids of X / L X/L . Depending on the choice of ambient set used to collate the theta values, one obtains the version of the locus of that flavor. My construction of Mochizuki’s set Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} is detailed in § 9 . It should be remarked that the verb to collate is not used in the context of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11, Corollary 3.12] , but in my opinion it clarifies immensely what [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] is all about. In fact, this verb is never used in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] , [ Mochizuki, 2020 ] and [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] at all. The approach adopted here is based on [ Joshi, 2023b ] but, like [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , uses the (global) Initial Theta Data § 3.1 , § 3.3 in a crucial way and is a more elaborate one than the local prototype of [ Joshi, 2023b ] which provides a natural demonstration of the structures claimed in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] . A reading of that paper ( [ Joshi, 2023b ] ) is highly recommended along with this one. § 6.1 Descent of holomorphoid data to L mod L_{\rm mod} The following proposition is useful in understanding how one may descend holomorphoid data from L ′ L^{\prime} to L mod L_{\rm mod} using § 3.1 , § 3.3 . Theorem 6.1.1 . Let C , X , L , L ′ C,X,L,L^{\prime} be as in § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 . Let 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ′ ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L^{\prime})_{{\bf y}} be a holomorphoid of X / L ′ X/L^{\prime} and let 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( 𝐲 ) \mathfrak{arith}({\bf y}) be the arithmeticoid of L ′ L^{\prime} given by this holomorphoid. The construction of the adelic set Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i ? {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{?} (and of each Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p ? {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}^{?}} ) requires fixing an Initial Theta Data § 3.1 , § 3.3 . This is also required in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] for the construction of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} . § 6.2.1 Local components of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}} . The construction of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}} is based on the local construction of [ Joshi, 2023b ] . The important advantage of the method of [ Joshi, 2023b ] is that for any prime w | p w|p , it provides theta-values locii Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i B ? {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{B_{?}} using a wide class of rings B ? B_{?} used in p p -adic Hodge Theory, for example B ? = B , B ⁡ [ 1 / t ] , B I ​ ( I ⊂ [ 0 , 1 ] ⊂ ℝ ) , B c ​ r ​ i ​ s ​ and ​ B d ​ R , {B_{?}}=B,B[1/t],B_{I}\ (I\subset[0,1]\subset{\mathbb{R}}),B_{cris}\text{ and }B_{dR}, (see Theorem 6.7.1.1 ). At the same time, by choosing a suitable Galois cohomology group (for each w | p w|p and for each rational prime p p ) to serve as a receiver of theta-values, viewed as cohomology classes via [ Mochizuki, 2009 , Proposition 1.4] , one establishes the construction of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i {\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} along the lines of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] using my canonical approach to arithmetic holomorphic structures. On the other hand working with these rings of p p -adic Hodge Theory provides yet another distinct advantage: one can multiply (and add) together theta-values arising from distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures for distinct primes w w lying over p p . This bundling of theta-values arising from distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures and for distinct primes w | p w|p (for each fixed rational prime p p ) also requires fixing an Initial theta-data § 3.1 , § 3.3 . In [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , Mochizuki works with a Galois cohomology group (for each prime w | p w|p and each prime p p ) and so there is no natural ring structure to multiply theta-values at all. To circumvent this problem, Mochizuki forces multiplication of theta-values by means of his theory of tensor packets [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Section 3] (to achieve this bundling of theta-values). In particular, my approach to the construction of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i ? {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{?} also demonstrates that Mochizuki’s framework of tensor-packets, arises far more naturally from the theory of Arithmetic Teichmuller spaces as documented in the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper). § 6.3 Mochizuki’s Adelic Ansatz and arithmeticoids Let L L , C / L C/L , and X / L X/L be as in § 2.4 . (in the superscripts of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i ? {\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{?} , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i ? {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{?} ) comes into picture. In [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2023b ] , I recognized that one may choose fundamental ring of p p -adic Hodge theory as a common location for comparing theta-values and there I introduced therein the theory of lifting of theta-values (this is recalled here briefly in § 6.4.2 ). Mochizuki’s choice of his common set in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] is based on his theory of Galois Theoretic Theta-evaluation [ Mochizuki, 2009 , Proposition 1.4] (applied to the arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( 𝐲 j ) \mathfrak{arith}({\bf y}_{j}) ) and then observing that the relevant Galois cohomology group provided by any arithmeticoid is amphoric ( [ Joshi, 2023a , Proposition ] ). The method of collation of cohomology classes (in the cohomology of a fixed arithmeticoid) is established here in § 9.7.5 . Let X , L , L ′ X,L,L^{\prime} be as in § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 . Let 𝐳 ∈ Σ ~ L ′ \mathbf{z}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} , and let w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p} for some prime p p . For j = 1 , … , ℓ ∗ j=1,\ldots,{\ell^{*}} let ψ j : B E → K y j \psi_{j}:B_{E}\to K_{y_{j}} be the natural quotient homomorphism arising by taking quotient modulo the (principal) maximal ideal corresponding to the closed classical point y j ′ ∈ Y ℂ p ♭ , E w ′ y_{j}^{\prime}\in{Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E_{w}^{\prime}} . Then for j = 1 , … , ℓ ∗ j=1,\ldots,{\ell^{*}} one has ψ j ​ ( Ξ λ , K y 1 ′ ) = ψ j ​ ( [ z w , j ] ) = ξ 1 , K y j ′ = q X / E w ′ , y j ′ ∈ K y j ′ , \psi_{j}(\Xi_{\lambda,K_{y_{1}^{\prime}}})=\psi_{j}([z_{w,j}])=\xi_{1,K_{y_{j}^{\prime}}}=q_{X/E_{w}^{\prime},y_{j}^{\prime}}\in K_{y_{j}^{\prime}}, where is q X / E w ′ , y j ′ q_{X/E_{w}^{\prime},y_{j}^{\prime}} the Tate parameter of X / E w ′ X/E_{w}^{\prime} in the arithmetic holomorphic structure provided by y j ′ y_{j}^{\prime} . Proof . Let 𝔪 j ⊂ B E \mathfrak{m}_{j}\subset B_{E} be the maximal ideal corresponding to the point y j ′ y_{j}^{\prime} . Then by [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Chap. IV and Chap. VI] 𝔪 j = ( t K y j ′ ) \mathfrak{m}_{j}=(t_{K_{y_{j}^{\prime}}}) is a principal ideal and this generator maps to zero under B E → B E / 𝔪 j = K y j ′ B_{E}\to B_{E}/\mathfrak{m}_{j}=K_{y_{j}^{\prime}} . Hence ψ j ​ ( Ξ λ , K y 1 ′ ) = ψ j ​ ( [ z w , j ] ) . \psi_{j}(\Xi_{\lambda,K_{y_{1}^{\prime}}})=\psi_{j}([z_{w,j}]). The assertion now follows as [ z w , j ] [z_{w,j}] is a Teichmuller lift of ξ 1 , K y j ′ = q X / E w ′ , y j ′ \xi_{1,K_{y_{j}^{\prime}}}=q_{X/E_{w}^{\prime},y_{j}^{\prime}} . § 6.6.1 The theta-values locus in Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i B E {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{B_{E}} Now I am ready to define the basic theta-values locus in B E ℓ ∗ B_{E}^{\ell^{*}} . Definition 6.10.2 . The adelic theta-values locus with values in 𝔹 L ′ \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}} , denoted by Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} , is the set of all 𝚵 𝐳 = ( 𝚵 𝐳 , w ) w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ ∈ 𝔹 L ′ ℓ ∗ , \boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z}}=(\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z},w})_{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}}\in\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}^{\ell^{*}}, for each 𝐳 ∈ Σ ~ L ′ \mathbf{z}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} . Hence (6.10.3) Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ = { ( 𝚵 𝐳 , w ) w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ ∈ 𝔹 L ′ ℓ ∗ : 𝐳 ∈ Σ ~ L ′ } ⊂ 𝔹 L ′ ℓ ∗ . {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}}=\left\{(\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z},w})_{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}}\in\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}^{\ell^{*}}:\mathbf{z}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}}\right\}\subset\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}^{\ell^{*}}. ∙ \bullet By Theorem 6.7.1.1 and ( 5.2.5.3 ) one can also embed Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ ⊂ 𝔹 ~ L ′ ℓ ∗ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}}\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{L^{\prime}}^{\ell^{*}} and I will write (6.10.4) Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 ~ L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{L^{\prime}}} for the image of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} in 𝔹 ~ L ′ ℓ ∗ \widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{L^{\prime}}^{\ell^{*}} . § 6.10.1 Elements 𝚵 𝐳 \boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z}} as Mochizuki’s Θ \Theta -pilot objects Let me remark that each admissible theta-values-lift 𝚵 𝐳 \boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z}} is the additive analog of Mochizuki’s Θ \Theta -pilot object (see [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Definition 3.8] ). Especially, Mochizuki’s theta-values set Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i {\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} , according to [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] , is the “holomorphic hull” of the collection of all possible Θ \Theta -pilot objects- for the definition “holomorphic hulls” see [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Remark 3.9.5(ii)] . In § 9 , I will demonstrate that each 𝚵 𝐳 ∈ Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ \boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z}}\in{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} has a Galois cohomological manifestation and this cohomological object will be a Θ \Theta -Pilot in the sense of [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] . § 6.10.2 Descending to L mod L_{\rm mod} Assume L , C / L , X / L , ℓ , L ′ L,C/L,X/L,\ell,L^{\prime} are as in § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 . The curve X / L X/L is in fact definable over L mod L_{\rm mod} . Hence it will be convenient to provide a theta-values locus which does in fact live over L mod L_{\rm mod} . This is accomplished by means of the bijection 𝕍 L mod ≃ 𝕍 ¯ ⊂ 𝕍 L ′ \mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}}\simeq\underline{\mathbb{V}}\subset\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}} . By taking the image of the theta-values locii under the projection constructed in the proof of Theorem 6.1.1 and the trace morphism 𝔹 L ′ → 𝔹 L mod \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{B}_{L_{\rm mod}} given by taking, for each unique prime w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}} of L ′ L^{\prime} lying over v ∈ 𝕍 L mod v\in\mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}} given by the bijection 𝕍 ¯ ≃ 𝕍 L mod \underline{\mathbb{V}}\simeq\mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}} , the trace morphism B L w ′ → B L m ​ o ​ d , v B_{L_{w}^{\prime}}\to B_{L_{mod,v}} , one obtains the image of theta-values locii in 𝔹 L mod ℓ ∗ \mathbb{B}_{L_{\rm mod}}^{\ell^{*}} which I will refer to as the descent of the theta-value locii to L mod L_{\rm mod} . 7 Proof of Mochizuki’s Corollary 3.12 for Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} § 7.1 Now the stage is set for proving my version of the fundamental estimate known as of Mochizuki’s Corollary 3.12. A prototype version of which was established in [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem ] . The version proved here works in the number field setting and exhibits all features of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] except for the fact that I work with the ring 𝔹 L ′ \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}} defined above. Mochizuki’s version works with Galois cohomology groups and will be established in § 9 . For any w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss} , let p w p_{w} be the rational prime lying below w w . Choose a normalization of the p w p_{w} -adic valuation on, ℂ p w {\mathbb{C}}_{p_{w}} , a completed algebraic closure of ℚ p w {\mathbb{Q}}_{p_{w}} to be such that for any uniformizer π w ∈ O L w ′ \pi_{w}\in{O}_{L_{w}^{\prime}} one has | π w | ℂ p w = p w − 1 . \left|\pi_{w}\right|_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{w}}}=p_{w}^{-1}. Let Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} be the adelic theta-values locus for the ring 𝔹 L ′ \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}} (defined in Definition 6.10.2 ). Then | Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ | 𝔹 L ′ ≥ ∏ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s | q w 1 / 2 ​ ℓ | ℂ p w ℓ ∗ \left|{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}}\right|_{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}}\geq\prod_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}}\left|q_{w}^{1/2\ell}\right|^{\ell^{*}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{w}}} Proof . The set of such rational primes p p is finite (by § 3.1 , § 3.3 , 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s ≠ ∅ \underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}\neq\emptyset if and only if p p is an odd rational prime such that for some w | p w|p the curve X X has split multiplicative reduction), hence the product in the assertion is finite. Recall from § 4.4 and § 4.5 the construction of the standard point 𝐳 Θ ∈ Σ ~ L ′ \mathbf{z}_{\Theta}\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} . I will write 𝐳 = 𝐳 Θ \mathbf{z}=\mathbf{z}_{\Theta} for brevity of notation. (\boldsymbol{\Xi}^{\alpha_{w,1},\alpha_{w,2},\ldots,\alpha_{w,{\ell^{*}}}}_{\lambda,\mathbf{z},w})_{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}}. This proves the assertion. § 7.4 Some corollaries of the fundamental estimates Let me first make the following definition. A subset Φ = ∏ w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ Φ w ⊂ 𝔹 L ′ ℓ ∗ \Phi=\prod_{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}}\Phi_{w}\subset\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}^{\ell^{*}} is said to be a special precompact subset if (1) Φ \Phi contains the lifts of theta values arising from special point 𝐳 Θ \mathbf{z}_{\Theta} constructed in Theorem 7.3.1 , i.e. 𝚵 𝐳 Θ ∈ Φ \boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z}_{\Theta}}\in\Phi , and (2) for every w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss} , the factor Φ w ⊂ B E w ′ ℓ ∗ \Phi_{w}\subset B_{E^{\prime}_{w}}^{\ell^{*}} is precompact i.e. the closure of Φ w \Phi_{w} in B E w ′ ℓ ∗ B_{E^{\prime}_{w}}^{\ell^{*}} is compact (for the Fréchet-topology) and for all other primes w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}} , Φ w = { 1 } \Phi_{w}=\{1\} . Corollary 7.4.1 . With the hypothesis of the Theorem 7.3.1 . Suppose Φ ⊂ Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ \Phi\subset{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} is a special precompact subset of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} in the sense of § 7.4 , then one has ∞ > | Φ | 𝔹 L ′ > ∏ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s ≠ ∅ | q w 1 / 2 ​ ℓ | ℂ p w ℓ ∗ . \infty>\left|\Phi\right|_{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}}>\prod_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}\neq\emptyset}\left|q_{w}^{1/2\ell}\right|^{\ell^{*}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{w}}}. Proof . Let Φ ⊂ Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ \Phi\subset{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} be a special precompact subset. As any finite extension of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} is locally compact, special precompactness of Φ \Phi is equivalent to Φ w \Phi_{w} being a bounded subset of B E w ′ ℓ ∗ B_{E^{\prime}_{w}}^{\ell^{*}} for all w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}} (for the Fréchet topology) and as the set of 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s \underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss} is finite, so sup 0 < ρ < 1 | Φ | 𝔹 L ′ , ρ \sup_{0<\rho<1}\left|\Phi\right|_{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}},\rho} is evidently bounded and hence the second assertion is established. Corollary 7.4.2 . In the notation of Theorem 7.3.1 , and with ρ = 1 \rho=1 , one has 1 ≥ | Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ | 𝔹 L ′ , 1 ≥ ∏ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s | q w 1 / 2 ​ ℓ | ℂ p w . 1\geq\left|{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}}\right|_{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}},1}\geq\prod_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}}\left|q_{w}^{1/2\ell}\right|_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{w}}}. Proof . The upper bound follows from the fact that for each w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss} , and ρ = 1 \rho=1 , one can apply [ Joshi, 2023b , Proposition ] . The lower bound is Theorem 7.3.1 . § 7.5 Prime Bundling rings B ˘ L ′ , p {\breve{B}}_{L^{\prime},p} and 𝐁 ˘ L ′ , p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{L^{\prime},p} One important aspect of working with theta-value locii with values in a ring is that one can use the multiplicative structure of the ring to multiply theta-values arising from distinct arithmeticoids. For example in 𝔹 L ′ \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}} it makes perfect sense to talk about 𝚵 𝐳 ⋅ 𝚵 𝐳 ′ ∈ 𝔹 L ′ , \boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z}^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}, as well as 𝚵 𝐳 + 𝚵 𝐳 ′ ∈ 𝔹 L ′ . \boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z}}+\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z}^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}. Similarly one can also multiply the local components at two distinct primes w 1 , w 2 ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w_{1},w_{2}\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p} lying over a fixed rational prime: ( 𝚵 𝐳 , w 1 ⊗ 1 ) ⋅ ( 1 ⊗ 𝚵 𝐳 , w 2 ) ∈ B L w 1 ′ ⊗ ℚ p B L w 2 ′ , (\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z},w_{1}}\otimes 1)\cdot(1\otimes\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z},w_{2}})\in B_{L_{w_{1}}^{\prime}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}B_{L_{w_{2}}^{\prime}}, as well as 𝚵 𝐳 , w 1 ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ 𝚵 𝐳 , w 2 ∈ B L w 1 ′ ⊗ ℚ p B L w 2 ′ . \boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z},w_{1}}\otimes 1+1\otimes\boldsymbol{\Xi}_{\mathbf{z},w_{2}}\in B_{L_{w_{1}}^{\prime}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}B_{L_{w_{2}}^{\prime}}. This observation allows me to demonstrate the tensor packet structure of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Section 3] which plays in the main theorem of [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] . I call this bundling of theta-values . The purpose of this bundling is to arrive at a minimal theta-values locus (this becomes important in providing tight bounds) and this together with the fact that 𝕍 ¯ ≃ 𝕍 L mod \underline{\mathbb{V}}\simeq\mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}} , one can expect to descend these data back to L mod L_{\rm mod} . In this section I will describe this bundling structure explicitly. One needs to work with several Fargues-Fontaine rings B E B_{E} (for varying E E ) simultaneously and leads to some auxiliary rings which I call bundling rings . The definition of these bundling rings is the main content of this section. The notations and assumptions of § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 will strictly be in force. Following [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , I will work with all the primes w | p w|p in 𝕍 ¯ \underline{\mathbb{V}} simultaneously. § 7.5.1 More bundling rings For simplicity, I will write (7.5.1.1) 𝕍 L ′ , p \displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime},p} = { w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ : w | p } , \displaystyle=\{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}:w|p\}, (7.5.1.2) 𝕍 ¯ p \displaystyle\underline{\mathbb{V}}_{p} = { w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ : w | p } , and \displaystyle=\left\{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}:w|p\right\},\text{ and} (7.5.1.3) 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s \displaystyle\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p} = { w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s : w | p } . \displaystyle=\left\{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}:w|p\right\}. The local components, { Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i B E w ′ : w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s } \left\{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{B_{E^{\prime}_{w}}}:w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}\right\} , of the theta-torsion values locus Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} , can be viewed as subsets of the following direct product of rings: (7.5.1.4) ( ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s B E w ′ ) ℓ ∗ = ∏ j = 1 ℓ ∗ ( ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s B E w ′ ) \left(\bigoplus_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}}B_{E_{w}^{\prime}}\right)^{\ell^{*}}=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell^{*}}\left(\bigoplus_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}}B_{E_{w}^{\prime}}\right) where for notational continuity I will write E w ′ = L w ′ E_{w}^{\prime}=L^{\prime}_{w} . More generally, let (7.5.1.5) B ˘ L ′ , p = ( ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ , p B E w ′ ) . {\breve{B}}_{L^{\prime},p}=\left(\bigoplus_{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime},p}}B_{E_{w}^{\prime}}\right). The right hand side obviously depends on L ′ L^{\prime} and hence the notation includes L ′ L^{\prime} dependence. If L ′ L^{\prime} is not in doubt, I will simply write B ˘ p = B ˘ L ′ , p {\breve{B}}_{p}={\breve{B}}_{L^{\prime},p} . Then one can write the ring 𝔹 L ′ \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}} as (7.5.1.6) 𝔹 L ′ = ∏ p ∈ 𝕍 ℚ B ˘ L ′ , p . \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}=\prod_{p\in\mathbb{V}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}}{\breve{B}}_{L^{\prime},p}. Remark 7.5.1.7 . For w | p w|p , one can think of elements of B ˘ p ℓ ∗ {\breve{B}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}} as ℓ ∗ {\ell^{*}} -tuples of elements of B ˘ p {\breve{B}}_{p} indexed by j = 1 , … , ℓ ∗ j=1,\ldots,{\ell^{*}} . Each element of B ˘ p {\breve{B}}_{p} is an element of the B p B_{p} -algebra in the parenthesis and so one can think of elements of B ˘ p {\breve{B}}_{p} as having coordinates indexed by the set (7.5.1.8) { E w ′ : w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ , p } . \left\{E_{w}^{\prime}:w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime},p}\right\}. ∙ \bullet Lemma 7.5.1.9 . For each p p , B ˘ L ′ , p {\breve{B}}_{L^{\prime},p} is a finite module over B p B_{p} . Proof . For w | p w|p , one has B E w ′ = B p ⊗ E w , 0 ′ E w ′ B_{E_{w}^{\prime}}=B_{p}\otimes_{E_{w,0}^{\prime}}E_{w}^{\prime} where E w , 0 ′ ⊂ E w ′ E_{w,0}^{\prime}\subset E_{w}^{\prime} is the maximal absolutely unramified subfield of E w ′ E_{w}^{\prime} . So B ˘ L ′ , p {\breve{B}}_{L^{\prime},p} is a B p B_{p} -algebra through this identification and for each w | p w|p this B E w ′ B_{E_{w}^{\prime}} is a finite module over B p B_{p} and so the assertion follows. § 7.5.2 The ring 𝐁 ˘ p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p} To understand my next definition, let me provide some motivation. Suppose for some prime p p , each w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ , p w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime},p} the absolute residue degree, f ⁡ ( w | p ) f(w|p) , of E w ′ / ℚ p E_{w}^{\prime}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} is one i.e. f ⁡ ( w | p ) = 1 f(w|p)=1 for all w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ , p w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime},p} . Then the maximal unramified subfield E w , 0 ′ ⊂ E w ′ E_{w,0}^{\prime}\subset E_{w}^{\prime} satisfies E w , 0 ′ = ℚ p ​ for all ​ w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ , p . E_{w,0}^{\prime}={\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\text{ for all }w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime},p}. Then we may write B ˘ L ′ , p = B ⊗ ℚ p ( ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ , p E w ′ ) . {\breve{B}}_{L^{\prime},p}=B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\left(\bigoplus_{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime},p}}E_{w}^{\prime}\right). In general this condition on the absolute residue degree does not hold. This presents some difficulty in understanding B ˘ L ′ , p {\breve{B}}_{L^{\prime},p} transparently and motivates my next definition. For simplicity let me write: (7.5.2.1) 𝐁 ˘ p = ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s ( B ⊗ ℚ p E w ′ ) , {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}=\bigoplus_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}}\left(B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{E_{w}^{\prime}}\right), Then using the fact that B E ↪ B ⊗ ℚ p E B_{E}\hookrightarrow B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E one has (7.5.2.2) B ˘ p ℓ ∗ = ( ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s B E w ′ ) ℓ ∗ ↪ 𝐁 ˘ p ℓ ∗ {\breve{B}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}}=\left(\bigoplus_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}}B_{E_{w}^{\prime}}\right)^{\ell^{*}}\hookrightarrow{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}} The theta-values locus obviously embeds in 𝐁 ˘ p ℓ ∗ {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}} by means of the diagonal embedding. Lemma 7.5.2.3 . One has the simplification (this is the reason for working with 𝐁 ˘ p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p} as opposed to B ˘ p {\breve{B}}_{p} ): 𝐁 ˘ p = B ⊗ ℚ p ( ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s E w ′ ) {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}=B\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\left(\bigoplus_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}}{E_{w}^{\prime}}\right) Proof . The proof is immediate from the definition ( 7.5.2.1 ). § 7.5.3 Bundling theta-values: the locus Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B ˘ p {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{{\breve{B}}_{p}} Now let (7.5.3.1) Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B ˘ p = ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p E w ′ ↪ B ˘ p = ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s B E w ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{{\breve{B}}_{p}}=\bigoplus_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}}{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{E_{w}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow{\breve{B}}_{p}=\bigoplus_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p}}B_{E_{w}^{\prime}} Thus Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B ˘ p {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{{\breve{B}}_{p}} assembles together the collection of all possible lifts (as constrained in [ Joshi, 2023b , § ] ) viewed as lifts in B E w ′ ⊂ B ˘ p B_{E_{w}^{\prime}}\subset{{\breve{B}}_{p}} for each w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}_{p} . § 7.5.4 theta-values locii in variants of the B B -rings For each prime p p , one can construct bundling rings using other rings considered here: B p , B p ​ [ 1 / t ] , B e , B ˘ p , B ˘ p ​ [ 1 / t ] , 𝐁 ˘ p , 𝐁 ˘ p ​ [ 1 / t ] , B d ​ R , B_{p},B_{p}[1/t],B_{e},{\breve{B}}_{p},{\breve{B}}_{p}[1/t],{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p},{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}[1/t],B_{dR}, and hence bundled sets such as Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B ˘ p {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{{\breve{B}}_{p}} can be constructed using these rings. I will indicate the choice of the ring for the construction by using the notation for the ring in the superscript, for example, as Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B p , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B ˘ p ​ e ​ t ​ c . {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{B_{p}},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{{\breve{B}}_{p}}\ etc. Thus one has constructed the theta-values locii (7.5.4.1) Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B ⁡ [ 1 / t ] , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B ˘ , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B ˘ ​ [ 1 / t ] , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p 𝐁 ˘ , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p 𝐁 ˘ ​ [ 1 / t ] , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B e , ⋯ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{B},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{B[1/t]},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{{\breve{B}}},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{{\breve{B}}[1/t]},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}[1/t]},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{B_{e}},\cdots So my point is that the locii of the sort constructed by Mochizuki can be constructed as soon as one has the Arithmetic Teichmuller spaces of [ Joshi, 2021a ] , [ Joshi, 2023b ] and the present paper. At any rate one has proved the following theorem: Theorem 7.5.4.2 . Fix an elliptic curve C / L C/L as in § 3.1 . Then using the choice of the theta function as in [ Mochizuki, 2009 , Proposition 1.6] or [ Joshi, 2023b , § ] , [ Joshi, 2023b , § ] at each w | p w|p and a choice of a ring, say B ? B_{?} , from the set { B = B p , B ⁡ [ 1 / t ] , B ˘ , B ˘ ​ [ 1 / t ] , 𝐁 ˘ , 𝐁 ˘ ​ [ 1 / t ] , B e , B d ​ R } ​ ⋃ { B I : I ⊂ [ 0 , 1 ] ⊂ ℝ } \left\{B=B_{p},B[1/t],{\breve{B}},{\breve{B}}[1/t],{\breve{\mathbf{B}}},{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}[1/t],B_{e},B_{dR}\right\}\bigcup\left\{B_{I}:I\subset[0,1]\subset{\mathbb{R}}{\vphantom{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}[1/t]}}\right\} and the corresponding bundling ring, one has a naturally defined theta-values locus viewed in bundling rings (stated here for B E B_{E} ): Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p B ˘ ⊂ B ˘ p ℓ ∗ . {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}^{\breve{B}}\subset{\breve{B}}^{\ell^{*}}_{p}. By passage to the product over all primes p p : Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ = ∏ p Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p ⊂ ∏ p B ˘ p ℓ ∗ = 𝔹 L ′ ℓ ∗ . {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}}=\prod_{p}{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}}\subset\prod_{p}{\breve{B}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}}=\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}^{\ell^{*}}. Using the diagonal inclusion B ˘ p ↪ 𝐁 ˘ p ℓ ∗ {\breve{B}}_{p}\hookrightarrow{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}} considered above, one can consider the theta-value locus Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐁 ˘ ⊂ ∏ p 𝐁 ˘ p ℓ ∗ . {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}}\subset\prod_{p}{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}}. § 7.6 Norms on tensor product of Banach spaces Familiarity with the archimedean theory of tensor products of Banach spaces is useful but not essential–for the archimedean theory see [ Kaniuth, 2009 ] or [ Diestel et al., 2008 ] . I will use the construction of tensor product of Banach spaces over ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} for which [ Schneider, 2002 , Chap IV, § 17] is a convenient reference. To apply these results (of [ Schneider, 2002 ] ) one needs to remember that the Banach spaces B E B_{E} over E E and as the p p -adic field E E is discretely valued, E E is a spherically complete field. § 7.6.1 Basic examples Let { V α } \{V_{\alpha}\} be a finite multi-set of (1) finite dimensional ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} vector spaces or (2) ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -Banach spaces of infinite dimensions. Two collections of such will be important to us. The first is the collection of p p -adic fields which occur above (7.6.1.1) { V α } = { E w ′ : w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s , w | p } \{V_{\alpha}\}=\{E_{w}^{\prime}:w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss},w|p\} viewed as a collection of finite dimensional ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} vector spaces which are complete with respect to their usual p p -adic norms | − | E w ′ \left|-\right|_{E_{w}^{\prime}} and hence are Banach spaces with these norms. Second is the collection (7.6.1.2) { V α } = { B E w ′ : w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s , w | p } \{V_{\alpha}\}=\{B_{E_{w}^{\prime}}:w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss},w|p\} of infinite dimensional Fréchet algebras over ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} equipped with Fréchet norms | − | ρ \left|-\right|_{\rho} for every ρ ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] ⊂ ℝ \rho\in[0,1]\subset{\mathbb{R}} constructed in [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 ] and hence can be treated as Banach spaces with respect to any of the Fréchet norms. § 7.6.2 Tensor products and the cross-norm property Let E E be a p p -adic field for rational prime p p or let E = ℝ E={\mathbb{R}} or ℂ {\mathbb{C}} i.e. the archimedean case is allowed in this discussion. In general, there are many distinct tensor products of a pair of Banach spaces over E E . Let ( V , | − | V ) , ( W , | − | W ) (V,\left|-\right|_{V}),(W,\left|-\right|_{W}) be Banach-spaces over E E . Suppose one has a tensor product ( V ⊗ E W , | − | V ⊗ W ) (V\otimes_{E}W,\left|-\right|_{V\otimes_{W}}) of ( V , | − | V ) (V,\left|-\right|_{V}) and ( W , | − | W ) (W,\left|-\right|_{W}) . Then the following property, if true, of this tensor product will be called the cross-norm property (of this tensor product): (7.6.2.1) | v ⊗ w | V ⊗ E W = | v | V ⋅ | w | W ∀ v ∈ V , w ∈ W . \left|v\otimes w\right|_{V\otimes_{E}W}=\left|v\right|_{V}\cdot\left|w\right|_{W}\qquad\forall v\in V,w\in W. In the archimedean case, by [ Grothendieck, 1953 ] or [ Diestel et al., 2008 ] , any tensor product of Banach spaces (over E = ℝ , ℂ E={\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}} ) sandwiched between the injective tensor product and the projective tensor product has the cross-norm property ( 7.6.2.1 ). More precisely, in the archimedean case, by [ Grothendieck, 1953 ] or [ Diestel et al., 2008 ] , the projective tensor product is the greatest cross-norm, while the injective tensor product is the smallest cross-norm. Now assume E E is non-archimedean. I will not recall here the definition of the projective tensor product | − | V ⊗ E W \left|-\right|_{V\otimes_{E}W} given in [ Bosch et al., 1984 , Chapter 2, 2.1.7] or [ Schneider, 2002 , Chapter 4, § 17] . Then one has cross-norm property ( 7.6.2.1 ) for the projective tensor Banach spaces over the field E E ( [ Schneider, 2002 , Proposition 17.4] ). Note that since the p p -adic field E E is complete and discretely valued field, so it is spherically complete [ Schneider, 2002 , § 1, Lemma 1.6] . As a consequence of spherical completeness of E E , one has by [ van der Put and van Tiel, 1967 , Theorem 1] that the injective and projective tensor products coincide and so one may speak of the unique tensor product. [One of the difficulties of working with fields which are not spherically complete is that a Banach space may have no non-zero continuous linear forms on it; on the other hand, from the point of view of p p -adic integration (and measures), the existence of linear forms is desirable in any case.] Moreover under spherical completeness of E E , one has additionally the nice properties [ Schneider, 2002 , Proposition 17.8, 17.9] of the tensor product. This remark applies to all Banach spaces over p p -adic fields considered here and the following summarizes this discussion: Theorem 7.6.2.2 . Let E E be a p p -adic field or let E = ℝ , ℂ E={\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}} . (1) If E = ℝ , ℂ E={\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{C}} , then the projective and the injective tensor products have the cross-norm property ( 7.6.2.1 ) (and so does any norm sandwiched between these two norms) ( [ Grothendieck, 1953 ] or [ Diestel et al., 2008 , Theorem 1.1.3 ] ). (2) If E E is a p p -adic field, then projective tensor product and the injective tensor products coincide (and one can speak of the tensor product of Banach spaces over a p p -adic field E E ) ( [ van der Put and van Tiel, 1967 , Theorem 1] ) and, (3) the cross-norm property ( 7.6.2.1 ) holds for any pair of Banach spaces over any p p -adic field E E . (4) Especially, for E = ℚ p E={\mathbb{Q}}_{p} , one has | v ⊗ w | V ⊗ ℚ p W = | v | V ⋅ | w | W . \left|v\otimes w\right|_{V\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}W}=\left|v\right|_{V}\cdot\left|w\right|_{W}. (5) In particular, for p p -adic fields E 1 , E 2 ⊇ E E_{1},E_{2}\supseteq E , the tensor product B E 1 ⊗ E B E 2 B_{E_{1}}{\otimes}_{E}B_{E_{2}} has the cross-norm property for any choice of norms on each of its factors. § 7.6.3 Passage to completed tensor products While passage to completed tensor product is unnecessary for what I will do here, it is worth remarking that by [ Schneider, 2002 , Chapter 4, Proposition 17.6] , if V , W V,W are Fréchet spaces over ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} then completed tensor product V ​ ⊗ ^ ℚ p ​ W V\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}W is a Fréchet space. In particular, for p p -adic fields E 1 , E 2 E_{1},E_{2} , the tensor product B E 1 ​ ⊗ ^ ℚ p ​ B E 2 B_{E_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}B_{E_{2}} is a Fréchet space. § 7.6.4 Tensor products again The discussion of this paragraph provides an analog of the above equality for the example of Banach algebras { B E : E ​ a p-adic field } \{B_{E}:E\text{ a p-adic field}\} equipped with their multiplicative norms i.e. valuations { | − | B E ; ρ } for ρ ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] ⊂ ℝ \{\left|-\right|_{B_{E};\rho}\}\text{ for }\rho\in[0,1]\subset{\mathbb{R}} constructed in [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Chapitre 2] . The following lemma is a standard consequence of valuation theory and is easily established: Lemma 7.6.4.1 . Let E 1 , E 2 E_{1},E_{2} be two p p -adic fields, let ρ 1 , ρ 2 ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] ⊂ ℝ \rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in[0,1]\subset{\mathbb{R}} . Write B 1 = B E 1 , B 2 = B E 2 B_{1}=B_{E_{1}},B_{2}=B_{E_{2}} and equip them with the multiplicative norms | − | B i ; ρ i \left|-\right|_{B_{i};\rho_{i}} for i = 1 , 2 i=1,2 . Then the algebra B 1 ⊗ ℚ p B 2 B_{1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}B_{2} is equipped with a valuation i.e. a multiplicative norm | − | B 1 ⊗ B 2 ; ρ 1 , ρ 2 \left|-\right|_{B_{1}\otimes B_{2};\rho_{1},\rho_{2}} such that for every b 1 ∈ B 1 b_{1}\in B_{1} and for every b 2 ∈ B 2 b_{2}\in B_{2} one has | b 1 ⊗ b 2 | B 1 ⊗ B 2 ; ρ 1 , ρ 2 = | ( b 1 ⊗ 1 ) ⋅ ( 1 ⊗ b 2 ) | B 1 ⊗ B 2 ; ρ 1 , ρ 2 = = | ( b 1 ⊗ 1 ) | B 1 ⊗ B 2 ; ρ 1 , ρ 2 ⋅ | ( 1 ⊗ b 2 ) | B 1 ⊗ B 2 ; ρ 1 , ρ 2 = | b 1 | B 1 ; ρ 1 ​ | b 2 | B 2 ; ρ 2 . \left|b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\right|_{B_{1}\otimes B_{2};\rho_{1},\rho_{2}}=\left|(b_{1}\otimes 1)\cdot(1\otimes b_{2})\right|_{B_{1}\otimes B_{2};\rho_{1},\rho_{2}}=\\ \qquad\qquad=\left|(b_{1}\otimes 1)\right|_{B_{1}\otimes B_{2};\rho_{1},\rho_{2}}\cdot\left|(1\otimes b_{2})\right|_{B_{1}\otimes B_{2};\rho_{1},\rho_{2}}=\left|b_{1}\right|_{B_{1};\rho_{1}}\left|b_{2}\right|_{B_{2};\rho_{2}}. especially | b 1 ⊗ b 2 | B 1 ⊗ B 2 ; ρ 1 , ρ 2 = | b 1 | B 1 ; ρ 1 ​ | b 2 | B 2 ; ρ 2 . \left|b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\right|_{B_{1}\otimes B_{2};\rho_{1},\rho_{2}}=\left|b_{1}\right|_{B_{1};\rho_{1}}\left|b_{2}\right|_{B_{2};\rho_{2}}. I will refer to | − | B 1 ⊗ B 2 ; ρ 1 , ρ 2 \left|-\right|_{B_{1}\otimes B_{2};\rho_{1},\rho_{2}} as the tensor product norm of ( B 1 , | − | B 1 ; ρ 1 ) (B_{1},\left|-\right|_{B_{1};\rho_{1}}) and ( B 2 , | − | B 2 ; ρ 2 ) (B_{2},\left|-\right|_{B_{2};\rho_{2}}) . § 7.6.5 Another variant Here is a Banach theoretic version of the above lemma which I could not find in existing literature and of which I will provide a proof. Lemma 7.6.5.1 . Let E 1 , E 2 E_{1},E_{2} be two p p -adic fields, let ρ 1 , ρ 2 ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] ⊂ ℝ \rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in[0,1]\subset{\mathbb{R}} . Write B 1 = B E 1 , B 2 = B E 2 B_{1}=B_{E_{1}},B_{2}=B_{E_{2}} and equip them with the multiplicative norms | − | B i ; ρ i \left|-\right|_{B_{i};\rho_{i}} for i = 1 , 2 i=1,2 . Proof . By [ Lang, 2002 ] , there is a natural way to define an algebra structure on the tensor product B 1 ⊗ ℚ p B 2 B_{1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}B_{2} compatible with the algebra structures of each of the factors, namely ( b 1 ⊗ b 2 ) ⋅ ( c 1 ⊗ c 2 ) = ( b 1 ​ c 1 ) ⊗ ( b 2 ​ c 2 ) . (b_{1}\otimes b_{2})\cdot(c_{1}\otimes c_{2})=(b_{1}c_{1})\otimes(b_{2}c_{2}). Then | ( b 1 ⊗ b 2 ) ⋅ ( c 1 ⊗ c 2 ) | B 1 ⊗ ℚ p B 2 = | ( b 1 ​ c 1 ) ⊗ ( b 2 ​ c 2 ) | B 1 ⊗ ℚ p B 2 \left|(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})\cdot(c_{1}\otimes c_{2})\right|_{B_{1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}B_{2}}=\left|(b_{1}c_{1})\otimes(b_{2}c_{2})\right|_{B_{1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}B_{2}} and by [ Schneider, 2002 , Chap 4, Proposition 17.4] | ( b 1 ​ c 1 ) ⊗ ( b 2 ​ c 2 ) | = | b 1 ​ c 1 | B 1 ​ | b 2 ​ c 2 | B 2 \left|(b_{1}c_{1})\otimes(b_{2}c_{2})\right|=\left|b_{1}c_{1}\right|_{B_{1}}\left|b_{2}c_{2}\right|_{B_{2}} and as the norms on B 1 , B 2 B_{1},B_{2} are multiplicative, one gets | ( b 1 ​ c 1 ) ⊗ ( b 2 ​ c 2 ) | = | b 1 ​ c 1 | B 1 ​ | b 2 ​ c 2 | B 2 = | b 1 | ​ | c 1 | ​ | b 2 | ​ | c 2 | = | b 1 ⊗ c 1 | ​ | b 2 ⊗ c 2 | \left|(b_{1}c_{1})\otimes(b_{2}c_{2})\right|=\left|b_{1}c_{1}\right|_{B_{1}}\left|b_{2}c_{2}\right|_{B_{2}}=\left|b_{1}\right|\left|c_{1}\right|\left|b_{2}\right|\left|c_{2}\right|=\left|b_{1}\otimes c_{1}\right|\left|b_{2}\otimes c_{2}\right| and therefore | ( b 1 ​ c 1 ) ⊗ ( b 2 ​ c 2 ) | = | b 1 ⊗ c 1 | ​ | b 2 ⊗ c 2 | , \left|(b_{1}c_{1})\otimes(b_{2}c_{2})\right|=\left|b_{1}\otimes c_{1}\right|\left|b_{2}\otimes c_{2}\right|, and the assertion of the lemma is immediate from this formula by considering the special case ( b 1 ⊗ 1 ) ​ ( 1 ⊗ b 2 ) = b 1 ⊗ b 2 (b_{1}\otimes 1)(1\otimes b_{2})=b_{1}\otimes b_{2} . This proves the assertion. § 7.6.6 The lower bound lemma The proof of the following lemma is now self-evident: Lemma 7.6.6.1 . Suppose one is given a finite collection of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -Banach spaces { V α } α \{V_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha} and suppose that one is also given an element m α ∈ V α m_{\alpha}\in V_{\alpha} for each V α V_{\alpha} . Suppose for every prime p p one has a finite collection of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -Banach spaces { V α , p } \{V_{\alpha,p}\} (the indexing set for the α \alpha may depend on p p but I am suppressing the notation for the indexing set) and suppose S p ⊆ ⨂ V α , p S_{p}\subseteq\bigotimes V_{\alpha,p} is a subset. Then consider the product S = ∏ p S p ⊂ ∏ p ⨂ α V α , p . S=\prod_{p}S_{p}\subset\prod_{p}\bigotimes_{\alpha}V_{\alpha,p}. Define | S p | = sup { | s p | ⊗ α V α , p : s p ∈ S p } , \left|S_{p}\right|=\sup\{\left|s_{p}\right|_{\otimes_{\alpha}V_{\alpha,p}}:{s_{p}\in S_{p}}\}, and define (with the convention that ∞ ⋅ ∞ = ∞ , 0 ⋅ ∞ = ∞ \infty\cdot\infty=\infty,0\cdot\infty=\infty ) | S | = ∏ p | S p | ∈ ℝ ∪ { ∞ } . \left|S\right|=\prod_{p}\left|S_{p}\right|\in{\mathbb{R}}\cup\{\infty\}. § 7.6.8 Basic examples The principal cases of interest here are S p = Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p S_{p}={{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},p}} and S p = Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i , p S_{p}={\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki},p} for all rational primes p p lying below the primes in 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s \underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss} and for the remaining primes S p = { 1 } S_{p}=\{1\} or S p S_{p} is the unit ball in the p p -component. In this case, the sets S p S_{p} , satisfy the additional condition that | S p | = 1 \left|S_{p}\right|=1 for all but finitely many primes p p and for the remaining finitely many primes, one expects | S p | < ∞ \left|S_{p}\right|<\infty . So | S | < ∞ \left|S\right|<\infty in the principal case of interest. § 7.6.9 Sets of adelic and bounded adelic types The above example leads to the following definition. (1) I will say that S S as above is a set of adelic type if | S p | = 1 \left|S_{p}\right|=1 for all but finitely many primes p p , and (2) I will say that S S is a set of bounded adelic type if S S is a set of adelic type and | S p | < ∞ \left|S_{p}\right|<\infty for every prime p p . One may also extend the definition of these rings primes lying over all rational primes. § 7.7.3 The fundamental estimate Theorem 7.7.3.1 . Let C / L C/L be an elliptic curve and suppose ℓ \ell is an odd prime such that § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 hold for C , L , ℓ C,L,\ell . Choose a normalization of the p w p_{w} -adic valuation on, ℂ p w {\mathbb{C}}_{p_{w}} , a completed algebraic closure of ℚ p w {\mathbb{Q}}_{p_{w}} to be such that for any uniformizer π w ∈ O L w ′ \pi_{w}\in{O}_{L_{w}^{\prime}} one has | π w | ℂ p w = p w − 1 . \left|\pi_{w}\right|_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{w}}}=p_{w}^{-1}. Let Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝔹 L ′ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{\mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}}} be the adelic theta-values locus for the ring 𝔹 L ′ \mathbb{B}_{L^{\prime}} (defined in Definition 6.10.2 ). Then | Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i B ˘ ⊗ | B ˘ ⊗ ≥ ∏ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ o ​ d ​ d , s ​ s | q w 1 / 2 ​ ℓ | ℂ p w ℓ ∗ , \left|{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{{\breve{B}}^{\otimes}}\right|_{{\breve{B}}^{\otimes}}\geq\prod_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}^{odd,ss}}\left|q_{w}^{1/2\ell}\right|^{\ell^{*}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{w}}}, and a similar assertion for | Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐁 ˘ ⊗ | 𝐁 ˘ ⊗ \left|{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\otimes}}}\right|_{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\otimes}}} . Proof . Now the claim of Theorem 7.3.1 follows from Lemma 7.6.6.1 . This proves the assertion. § 7.8 The theta-values locii in 𝐁 ˘ p ⊗ B p 𝐁 ˘ p ⊗ B p ⋯ ⊗ B p 𝐁 ˘ p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}\otimes_{B_{p}}{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}\otimes_{B_{p}}\cdots\otimes_{B_{p}}{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p} and in 𝐁 ˘ p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p} One may further exploit multiplicative properties one has available in the situation at hand but which is not utilized in the construction of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i B ˘ ⊗ ⊂ ( 𝐁 ˘ ⊗ ) ℓ ∗ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{{\breve{B}}^{\otimes}}\subset\left({\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\otimes}}\right)^{\ell^{*}} given above. This construction does not explicitly demonstrate the tensor packet structure which is asserted in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Section 3] . I will now demonstrate this as follows. § 7.8.1 Multiplication of lifts of theta-values Since 𝐁 ˘ p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p} is a ring, one can also multiply elements in it. More precisely given a tuple of elements ( b ˘ 1 , … , b ˘ ℓ ∗ ) ∈ 𝐁 ˘ p ℓ ∗ (\breve{b}_{1},\ldots,\breve{b}_{\ell^{*}})\in{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}} one can also multiply them in 𝐁 ˘ p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p} i.e. consider the mapping 𝐁 ˘ p ℓ ∗ → 𝐁 ˘ p , {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}}\to{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}, given by ( b ˘ 1 , … , b ˘ ℓ ∗ ) ⟼ b ˘ 1 ⋅ b ˘ 2 ⋯ b ˘ ℓ ∗ . (\breve{b}_{1},\ldots,\breve{b}_{\ell^{*}})\longmapsto\breve{b}_{1}\cdot\breve{b}_{2}\cdots\breve{b}_{\ell^{*}}. This is multi-linear in each coordinate. This multi-linear mapping factors though the tensor product. In this case it is convenient to treat 𝐁 ˘ p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p} as a finite B p B_{p} -algebra and consider the natural factorization (7.8.1.1) 𝐁 ˘ p ℓ ∗ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}^{\ell^{*}}} 𝐁 ˘ p ⊗ B p 𝐁 ˘ p ⊗ B p ⋯ ⊗ B p 𝐁 ˘ p {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}\otimes_{B_{p}}{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}\otimes_{B_{p}}\cdots\otimes_{B_{p}}{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}} 𝐁 ˘ p . {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}.} where the first mapping is ( b 1 , … , b ℓ ∗ ) ⟼ b 1 ⊗ b 2 ⊗ ⋯ ⊗ b ℓ ∗ (b_{1},\ldots,b_{\ell^{*}})\longmapsto b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes b_{\ell^{*}} and the second is the natural mapping b 1 ⊗ b 2 ⊗ ⋯ ⊗ b ℓ ∗ ⟼ b 1 ⋅ b 2 ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ b ℓ ∗ b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes b_{\ell^{*}}\longmapsto b_{1}\cdot b_{2}\cdot\cdots\cdot b_{\ell^{*}} . The advantage of these considerations becomes clear from the following elementary decomposition lemma: Lemma 7.8.1.2 . One has the decomposition of the B p B_{p} -algebra: 𝐁 ˘ p ⊗ B p 𝐁 ˘ p ⊗ B p ⋯ ⊗ B p 𝐁 ˘ p = B p ⊗ ℚ p ( ⨁ α E α ′′ ) {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}\otimes_{B_{p}}{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}\otimes_{B_{p}}\cdots\otimes_{B_{p}}{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}=B_{p}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\left(\bigoplus_{\alpha}E_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}\right) where the sum on the right runs over a finite multi-set of finite extensions E α ′′ E_{\alpha}^{\prime\prime} of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} contained in ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} . Proof . This is immediate from the definition of 𝐁 ˘ p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p} and properties of tensor products. Indeed, since (7.8.1.3) 𝐁 ˘ p = B ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p ⊗ ℚ p ( ⨁ w | p , w ∈ 𝕍 L mod E w ) {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}=B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\left(\bigoplus_{w|p,w\in\mathbb{V}_{L_{\rm mod}}}E_{w}\right) and as the tensor product 𝐁 ˘ p ⊗ B p 𝐁 ˘ p {\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p}\otimes_{B_{p}}{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}_{p} reduces to computing tensor products of a finite collection of p p -adic fields, and so the assertion of the lemma follows. Remark 7.8.1.4 . In [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Section 3] , especially [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Remark 3.1.1, Remark 3.9.1] , and [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] , Mochizuki forces the tensor product structure and arrives essentially at the above lemma.
