Especially if this is true then any upper bound for it can serve as a uniform upper bound for Θ ^ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐁 ˘ ​ ( X / L ) {{\widehat{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}^{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}}(X/L) for every X / L ∈ M 1 , 1 ​ ( L ) X/L\in{M}_{1,1}(L) . However eq. ( 7.9.2 ) is too optimistic. One reason for this is that elliptic curves over L L acquire semi-stable reduction over fields which need not have bounded degrees. But fixing initial theta data as in § 3.1 means one is considering elliptic curves over L L which acquire semi-stable reduction over extensions of L ′ / L L^{\prime}/L of bounded degree (the field L ′ L^{\prime} will vary with the curve in general, but its degree remains bounded by Lemma 3.4.2.1 ). On the other hand such bounds may exist for arbitrary choice of compactly bounded subsets of M 1 , 1 ​ ( ℚ ¯ ) {M}_{1,1}({\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}) (see [ Mochizuki, 2010 ] for definition of compactly bounded subsets). Let ℚ ¯ ≤ d {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}^{\leq d} be the union of all finite extensions of ℚ {\mathbb{Q}} (all contained in ℚ ¯ {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}} ) of degrees bounded by the d d (specified by for example by Lemma 3.4.2.1 ). For K ⊂ M 1 , 1 ​ ( ℚ ¯ ) {K}\subset{M}_{1,1}({\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}) a compactly bounded subset supported at a finite set of rational primes Σ \Sigma one can expect to define Θ ^ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐁 ˘ ​ ( K ∩ M 1 , 1 ​ ( ℚ ¯ ≤ d ) ) {\widehat{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}^{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}}({K}\cap{M}_{1,1}({\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}^{\leq d})) and one can expect to prove that (7.9.3) | Θ ^ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐁 ˘ ​ ( K ∩ M 1 , 1 ​ ( ℚ ¯ ≤ d ) ) | 𝐁 ˘ < ∞ . \left|{\widehat{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}^{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}}({K}\cap{M}_{1,1}({\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}^{\leq d}))\right|_{{\breve{\mathbf{B}}}}<\infty. Very roughly this is the idea behind the computations of [ Mochizuki, 2021d , Corollary 2.2] 8 A ‘Rosetta Stone’ for [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] and [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] Let me now provide a fundamental ‘Rosetta Stone’ (Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 ) for the readers, so that they are able to translate objects and concepts between the theories of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] and [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . Once a reader has assimilated and understood the contents of the Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 , hopefully, the readers will be persuaded that there exists corresponding objects in [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] which allows one to establish the proofs of the relevant claims of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] with complete clarity. § 8.1 How to use the Rosetta Stone for reading [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] The Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 must be read along with my discussion of other objects of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] such as Frobenioids and Hodge Theaters provided here in § 10 . My work together with the (Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 ) provides an adequate toolkit for the reader to be able to navigate [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . [The existence of distinct such objects is asserted without proof in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] .] § 8.1.1 Notation Notations and assumptions of § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 will strictly be in force for full compatibility with [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . This means L L is a number field with no real embeddings, C / L C/L is an elliptic curve and X = C − { O } / L X=C-\{O\}/L is the hyperbolic curve (of strict Belyi Type by Mochizuki’s definition) of topological type ( 1 , 1 ) (1,1) . Notably, the isomorphism class [ X / L ] [X/L] of X / L X/L defines a moduli point in the moduli stack M 1 , 1 {M}_{1,1} and the field of definition of this moduli point is L mod L_{\rm mod} by § 2.4 , § 3.1 , § 3.3 . This data, by the results of the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper), provides the category 𝔍 ⁡ ( X / L ) \mathfrak{J}(X/L) of the adelic arithmetic Teichmuller space associated to X / L X/L [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 ] . Each object of this category, by means of [ Joshi, 2023a , Proposition ] , provides an arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) \mathfrak{arith}(L) of L L . As is demonstrated in [ Joshi, 2023a ] , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) \mathfrak{arith}(L) is a deformation of the arithmetic of number field L L . Working with holomorphoid ( X , 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) ) (X,\mathfrak{arith}(L)) ( Definition 2.1.2 ) allows us to view X X viewed as a scheme for the version of arithmetic of L L provided by the particular arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) \mathfrak{arith}(L) . As a scheme X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) X/\mathfrak{arith}(L) is, of course isomorphic to X / L X/L . For the properties of arithmeticoids see [ Joshi, 2023a , § ] . To be sure, a holomorphoid provides a global arithmetic structure and by Definition 2.1.5 , it also provides local arithmetic holomorphic structures at all primes v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} . For the properties of arithmetic holomorphic structures and arithmetic teichmuller spaces see [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2023a , Joshi, 2022 ] . § 8.2 Section 8.3.5 : Arithmetic Holomorphic Structures An idea central to both the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper) and especially [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , is the notion of arithmetic holomorphic structures. § 8.2.1 Arithmetic Holomorphic structures in [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] I will take the definition of arithmetic holomorphic structures as given in [ Joshi, 2022 , Definition 4.1] [ Joshi, 2022 , Definition ] (which sets out the local theory at each prime of L L ) and demonstrates that this provides all corresponding local data of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . [Existence of such structures is not demonstrated in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] .] The global theory of arithmetic holomorphic structures is detailed in [ Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] . § 8.2.2 The role of geometric base-points in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] Mochizuki’s [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] , is founded upon Mochizuki’s Key Principle of Inter-Universality , which is stated in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , § I3, Page 25] . According to this principle, Mochizuki’s [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] requires one to work with arbitrary geometric base-points. Furthermore, as Mochizuki reminds us [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Page 26] (and in many other places in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] and [ Mochizuki, 2020 ] ), the domains and codomains of all the key operations of his theory, namely 𝔩 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔤 \mathfrak{log} -links and Θ g ​ a ​ u \Theta_{gau} -links, refer to distinct geometric base-points. The reader may ask: why is it important to work with arbitrary geometric base-points? Working with arbitrary geometric base-points is the anabelian proxy for deformations of algebraic varieties. In [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] the idea is to use arbitrary geometric base-points (in the theory of tempered fundamental groups) as giving deformations of arithmetic. However, the exact mechanism by which one obtains such deformations was not established in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] but has been precisely quantified in [ Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2023a ] . Let me note that distinct geometric base-points also play an important role in the Chabauty-Kim Theory of [ Kim, 2005 ] (i.e. usage of arbitrary geometric base-points is important in other Diophantine contexts). My definition of arithmetic holomorphic structures [ Joshi, 2022 , Definition ] (and also see Definition 2.1.5 ) includes geometric base-points and hence my approach is fully compatible with the approach taken in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . The following table compares the notion of geometric base-points in the theory of étale and tempered fundamental groups. Comparison of geometric base-points for étale and tempered fundamental groups étale fund. group tempered fund. group Input datum ( X / ℚ p , ∗ K : Spec ( K ) → X ) (X/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},*_{K}:{\rm Spec}(K)\to X) where X / ℚ p X/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} is a scheme, K ⊃ ℚ p K\supset{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} is an alg. closed field, and ∗ K : Spec ( K ) → X *_{K}:{\rm Spec}(K)\to X is a K K -geom. base point of X X ( X ℚ p a ​ n , ∗ K : M ( K ) → X ℚ p a ​ n ) (X^{an}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},*_{K}:{M}(K)\to X^{an}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}) where X ℚ p a ​ n X^{an}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} is an analytic space over ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} , K ⊃ ℚ p K\supset{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} an alg. closed, perfectoid field, and ∗ K : M ( K ) → X ℚ p a ​ n *_{K}:{M}(K)\to X^{an}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} is a K K -geom. base point of X ℚ p a ​ n X^{an}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} . isom. class of alg. closed fields K ⊃ ℚ p K\supset{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} is determined by the cardinality of K K isom. class of alg. closed, perfectoid fields K ⊃ ℚ p K\supset{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} is not determined by cardinality of K K . Remark 8.2.2.1 . (1) While Mochizuki recognized the centrality of this principle for [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] , Mochizuki’s approach to his theory is group theoretic and stays focused on utilizing the group theory surrounding the tempered fundamental group as the key tool in building his theory, however, his approach ignores the rich arithmetic, algebra and geometry which arbitrary geometric base-points for tempered fundamental groups provide (as can be seen in the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper)). This is an important difference in our respective viewpoints. (2) Let me remark that the anabelian reconstruction techniques of [ Mochizuki, 2012 , Mochizuki, 2013 , Mochizuki, 2015 ] are of considerable importance in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . However, [ Mochizuki, 2015 , Theorem 1.9] (which is the main theorem of that paper) does not determine the geometric base-point datum while, in accordance with Mochizuki’s Key Principle of Inter-Universality, this (undetermined) datum is of central importance in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . (3) Let me also say that, as far as I am aware, though Mochizuki’s statement of this principle has appeared in all versions of [ Mochizuki, 2021a ] (for example it is included in the hard copy of RIMS-Preprint from 2012), neither this principle nor the centrality of the role of arbitrary geometric base-points (in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] ) finds any mention in [ Fesenko, 2015 ] , [ Tan, 2018 ] , [ Scholze and Stix, 2018 ] , [ Yamashita, 2019 ] , [ Dupuy and Hilado, 2020b , Dupuy and Hilado, 2020a ] , [ Scholze, 2021 ] , [ Saïdi, 2022 ] . ∙ \bullet § 8.2.3 Arithmetic Holomorphic Structures in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] One notable problem with the claims of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] is that the existence of distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures is not established in it and my reading of [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] is that this issue cannot be resolved by methods of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . My central observation is that arithmetic holomorphic structures provided here in § 8.2.1 also provide arithmetic holomorphic structures in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . My comparison of arithmetic holomorphic structures in the two theories is given in Section 8.3.5 . § 8.2.4 Fundamental Dichotomy of an Arithmetic Holomorphic Structure Arithmetic holomorphic structures in both the theories ( [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] and [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] ) consists of two parts: (1) The first portion is tuned into arithmetic and topology of the field, and geometry of the variety while (2) the second portion tracks independent changes of valuations and hence requires recording the value group. I call this the fundamental dichotomy of an Arithmetic Holomorphic Structure . Importantly, arithmetic holomorphic structures in both the theories exhibit such a dichotomy. The non-triviality of the theories rests on exhibiting the existence of plurality of such data. This is a central problem for [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] which is not resolved by the artificial “resetting of logic” recommended in [ Mochizuki, 2022 ] ; on the other hand the theory of the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper) provides a natural plurality of such data from the very beginning and these data can be applied to exhibit the corresponding data in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . § 8.2.5 Let X / 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 X/\mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} be a holomorphoid of X / L X/L . In [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 ] the fundamental dichotomy arises from the data of the arithmeticoid 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} . Let 𝐲 = ( K v , K v ♭ ≃ ℂ p v ♭ ) v ∈ 𝕍 L ′ ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ {\bf y}=(K_{v},K_{v}^{\flat}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{\flat}_{p_{v}})_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime} provided by 𝔞 ​ 𝔯 ​ 𝔦 ​ 𝔱 ​ 𝔥 ​ ( L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{arith}(L)_{\bf y} . [Recall that by [ Joshi, 2023a , Proposition ] , each arithmetic holomorphic structure in the sense of [ Joshi, 2022 ] provides, the datum of the point 𝐲 {\bf y} .] The portion of the datum given by the perfectoid field K v K_{v} (with an embedding of L v L_{v} ) provides the geometric data of the analytic spaces ( X / L v , X / K v , X K v a ​ n → X L v a ​ n ) (X/L_{v},X/K_{v},X^{an}_{K_{v}}\to X^{an}_{L_{v}}) and these provide preferred isomorphs of the tempered fundamental group of X / L v X/L_{v} , the geometric tempered fundamental group of X / K v X/K_{v} and the absolute galois group of L v L_{v} computed for the algebraic closure of L v L_{v} in K v K_{v} (see [ Joshi, 2022 , Theorem ] ). The second portion is the data of the isometry K v ♭ ≃ ℂ p v ♭ K_{v}^{\flat}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{\flat}_{p_{v}} which records valuation specific information of L v ⊂ L ¯ v ⊂ K v L_{v}\subset\overline{L}_{v}\subset K_{v} in one fixed value group, namely | ℂ p v ♭ | \left|{\mathbb{C}}^{\flat}_{p_{v}}\right| , even as the untilt ( K v , K v ♭ ≃ ℂ p v ♭ ) v ∈ 𝕍 L ′ (K_{v},K_{v}^{\flat}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{\flat}_{p_{v}})_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}} moves (as 𝐲 {\bf y} moves). § 8.3 Mochizuki’s Dichotomy: Étale-pictures and Frobenius-pictures via [ Joshi, 2021a ] Mochizuki has a similar dichotomy for arithmetic holomorphic structures. The two portions of Mochizuki’s dichotomy for arithmetic holomorphic structures are termed in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] as (1) an “étale-picture” (see [ Mochizuki, 2021a , § 3, Corollary 3.9] ) and, (2) a “Frobenius-pictures” (see [ Mochizuki, 2021a , § 3, Corollary 3.8] ). The relationship between Dichotomy of Arithmetic Holomorphic Structures sss:fund-dichotomy and Mochizuki’s dichotomy of étale picture (or étale like data) and Frobenius-picture or (Frobenius-like data) is described for each prime v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} separately. Hence fix a v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} and let 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 X / L v ​ ( F v ) y v \mathfrak{hol}_{X/L_{v}}(F_{v})_{y_{v}} be an arithmetic holomorphic structure on X / L v X/L_{v} . Let y v = ( K v , i K v : K v ♭ ≃ F v ) y_{v}=(K_{v},i_{K_{v}}:K_{v}^{\flat}\simeq F_{v}) be point of Y F v , L v {Y}_{F_{v},L_{v}} provided by 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 X / L v ​ ( F v ) y v \mathfrak{hol}_{X/L_{v}}(F_{v})_{y_{v}} . Then I claim that Mochizuki’s Dichotomy of étale-picture and Frobenius-pictures arises from the Dichotomy of Arith Holomorphic Structure given by the datum of the local holomorphoid 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 X / L v ​ ( F v ) y \mathfrak{hol}_{X/L_{v}}(F_{v})_{y} as by Proposition 8.3.1.1 and Proposition 8.3.2.1 and is summarized in Section 8.3.5 : § 8.3.1 Mochizuki’s Étale Picture or étale-like data Mochizuki’s étale-picture consists of the group theoretic data provided by the proposition below: Proposition 8.3.1.1 . Each holomorphoid 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}} of X / L X/L provides, for each v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} , Mochizuki’s étale-like picture, labeled by the geometric data of the (local) holomorphoid of 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 X / L v ​ ( F v ) y v \mathfrak{hol}_{X/L_{v}}(F_{v})_{y_{v}} from which it arises and consists of the following data: (1) the algebraically closed perfectoid field K v K_{v} which contains an isometrically embedded ℚ p v {\mathbb{Q}}_{p_{v}} and hence K v K_{v} provides an algebraic closure of any finite extension L v L_{v} of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} . Thus K v K_{v} provides a preferred algebraic closure L v ⊂ L ¯ v ; K v ⊂ K v L_{v}\subset\overline{L}_{v;K_{v}}\subset K_{v} of L v L_{v} and hence provides a preferred isomorph G L v ; K v = Gal ⁡ ( L ¯ v / L v ) ≃ G L v G_{L_{v};K_{v}}={\rm Gal}(\overline{L}_{v}/L_{v})\simeq G_{L_{v}} of absolute Galois group of L v L_{v} ; (2) the Galois category Temp X / L v ; K v \mathrm{Temp}_{X/L_{v};K_{v}} (as defined in [ André, 2003a , Chap. III, 2.1.4] ) of tempered coverings of X L v a ​ n X^{an}_{L_{v}} which are defined over finite extensions of L v L_{v} contained in K v K_{v} ; (3) and a preferred isomorph Π X / L v ; K v t ​ e ​ m ​ p \Pi^{\scriptscriptstyle temp}_{X/L_{v};K_{v}} of the tempered fundamental group of X / L v X/L_{v} (see [ Joshi, 2022 , Theorem ] ). (4) Importantly, in my theory, this data appears with the distinguishable label given by the holomorphoid 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 X / L v ​ ( F v ) y v \mathfrak{hol}_{X/L_{v}}(F_{v})_{y_{v}} from which this “étale-picture” arises. Proof . The proof is clear. Remark 8.3.1.2 . There are other étale like data in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , these may be also obtained in a similar manner as above. ∙ \bullet § 8.3.2 Frobenius-picture or Frobenius-like data Now I claim that 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 X / L v ​ ( F v ) y v \mathfrak{hol}_{X/L_{v}}(F_{v})_{y_{v}} also provides Mochizuki’s Frobenius-picture or Frobenius-like data. Proposition 8.3.2.1 . Let 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}} be a holomorphoid of X / L X/L and let 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 X / L v ​ ( F v ) y v \mathfrak{hol}_{X/L_{v}}(F_{v})_{y_{v}} be the local holomorphoid of X / L v X/L_{v} (for any v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} ) given by 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}} . Then (1) the isometry i K v : K v ♭ ≃ F v i_{K_{v}}:K_{v}^{\flat}\simeq F_{v} identifies the value group of K v K_{v} with the value group of F v F_{v} [ Scholze, 2012 ] ; (2) in particular for all anabelomorphic holomorphoids of X / L v X/L_{v} in the category 𝔍 ​ ( X / L v ) F v \mathfrak{J}(X/L_{v})_{F_{v}} , one has a fixed value group–namely the value group of F v F_{v} , to compare valuations. (3) let L ¯ v ⊂ K v \overline{L}_{v}\subset K_{v} be the algebraic closure of L v L_{v} in K v K_{v} and O L ¯ v ⊳ = O L ¯ v ⊳ − { 0 } ⊂ O K v ⊳ − { 0 } {O}_{\overline{L}_{v}}^{\triangleright}={O}_{\overline{L}_{v}}^{\triangleright}-\{0\}\subset{O}_{K_{v}}^{\triangleright}-\{0\} be the multiplicative monoid of the ring of integers O L ¯ v ⊂ L ¯ v ⊂ K v {O}_{\overline{L}_{v}}\subset\overline{L}_{v}\subset K_{v} . Then O L ¯ v ⊳ {O}_{\overline{L}_{v}}^{\triangleright} is a Frobenius-like (monoidal) data of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , [ Mochizuki, 2020 ] . Proof . This is clear. The value group information provided by this proposition is Mochizuki’s Frobenius picture [ Mochizuki, 2021a ] . Remark 8.3.2.2 . Let me remark that the definition of K ♭ K^{\flat} [ Scholze, 2012 ] shows how Frobenius i.e. p t ​ h p^{th} -powers play a central role in the construction of K ♭ K^{\flat} . So the term Frobenius-like or Frobenius-picture is quite reasonable in this context. One important point to be noted is that even if K K is fixed, there are many inequivalent untilts, ( K , K ♭ ≃ F ) (K,K^{\flat}\simeq F) , of F F and hence there are many inequivalent Frobenius-like pictures. There are other types of Frobenius-like data in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] . These are also provided by the datum 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 X / E ​ ( F ) y \mathfrak{hol}_{X/E}(F)_{y} . ∙ \bullet § 8.3.3 Existence of Étale and Frobenius Pictures Since the geometric data of holomorphoids of X / L X/L provide étale-pictures and Frobenius-pictures of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] one has now established the following: Theorem 8.3.3.1 . Each holomorphoid 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}} of X / L X/L , provides, for each v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} , an arithmetic holomorphic structure in the sense of [ Joshi, 2022 ] and also provides Mochizuki’s Dichotomy of an arithmetic holomorphic structure: namely an “Étale picture” and a “Frobenius picture” in the sense of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , which is indexed by the holomorphoid 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L)_{{\bf y}} which it arises from. In particular, there exist many inequivalent étale and Frobenius pictures in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . Remark 8.3.3.2 . I will not use the terminology of étale and Frobenius pictures, but the above results make it clear that my approach does indeed have all the properties claimed by Mochizuki in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] where he has asserted the existence of such inequivalent data without proofs. ∙ \bullet § 8.3.4 L ∗ L^{*} -action on Étale and Frobenius pictures There is natural action of L ∗ L^{*} on the set of pairs consisting of (étale,Frobenius)-pictures and the justification of the existence of this action is inadequate (in my opinion) in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , though Mochizuki asserts the principal the property of this action [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Remark 1.2.3(ii)] , namely that if α ∈ L ∗ \alpha\in L^{*} acts trivially, then α \alpha is a root of unity. In [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] , I demonstrate the existence of the natural multiplicative action of L ∗ ​ ↷ ​ 𝐘 𝐋 L^{*}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} . From the above description of (étale,Frobenius)-pictures, one sees at once, that L ∗ ​ ↷ ​ 𝐘 𝐋 L^{*}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\mathscrbf{Y}_{L} provides the required action on the sets of such pairs (arising, as above, from arithmeticoids in 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} ). The fundamental property of this action alluded to in the above paragraph is proved in [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] . § 8.3.5 Section 8.3.5 Before exhibiting Section 8.3.5 , let me recall some of Mochizuki’s notation [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Figure I1.2, Page 9] (Mochizuki’s base number field is F F while mine is L L ): • v v is a prime of a fixed number L L , • L v L_{v} is its completion at v v , • O v {O}_{v} is its ring of integers, • L ¯ v \overline{L}_{v} is an algebraic closure of L v L_{v} and • L ¯ v × = L ¯ v ∗ = L ¯ v − { 0 } \overline{L}_{v}^{\times}=\overline{L}_{v}^{*}=\overline{L}_{v}-\{0\} ; • O ¯ v ⊂ L ¯ v \overline{{O}}_{v}\subset\overline{L}_{v} is the integral closure of O v {O}_{v} in L ¯ v \overline{L}_{v} ; • O ¯ v × ⊂ L ¯ v × \overline{{O}}_{v}^{\times}\subset\overline{L}_{v}^{\times} is group of units; • Π v \Pi_{v} is the tempered fundamental group of a fixed hyperbolic curve X / L v X/L_{v} . X / L v X/L_{v} ) and its stack quotients by { ± 1 } \{\pm 1\} . § 8.5 Section 8.5 : Prime-strips In Section 8.5 , I will consider holomorphoids ( X / E , X / K ) ∈ 𝔍 ⁡ ( X , E ) (X/E,X/K)\in\mathfrak{J}(X,E) with the restriction K ♭ = ℂ p ♭ K^{\flat}={\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat} . This restriction corresponds to working with the category 𝔍 ​ ( X , E ) ℂ p ♭ \mathfrak{J}(X,E)_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}} of [ Joshi, 2021a , § ] and places cardinality restriction on K K (because K K and K ♭ K^{\flat} have the same cardinality). Cardinality restrictions are missing in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . [By [ Scholze, 2012 ] , fixing the cardinality of the tilt K ♭ K^{\flat} to be that of ℂ p ♭ {\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat} implies the perfectoid field K K provided by any untilt of ℂ p ♭ {\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat} has the cardinality of ℂ p ♭ {\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat} . The latter has the cardinality of ℂ p {\mathbb{C}}_{p} which has the cardinality of the continuum.] This is cardinality restriction poses no problems in the context of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] (the theory of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] is far more general). While the notion of prime-strips of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , § I1, Page 9] is not used in the theory of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] , prime-strips are certainly available in the theory of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] and there is a correspondence between prime-strips of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] and those of [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] . This correspondence between prime-strips of the two theories may be transcribed using Section 8.5 (reading it from left to right) from the corresponding object in the rightmost column (which provides objects in the theory of the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper)). But Section 8.7.1 shows that there is however a more natural and even canonical object which serves this role and is given by (8.7.1) G L v ​ ↷ ​ 𝔾 ^ m ​ ( O ℂ p ♭ ) ≃ G L v ​ ↷ ​ B φ = p . G_{L_{v}}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}({O}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}})\simeq G_{L_{v}}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}B^{\varphi=p}. Remark 8.7.4 . The fundamental prime-strip at p p ( 8.7.1 ) provides a number of advantages over Mochizuki’s definition of prime-strips ( 8.6.2 ). Notably, I demonstrate (in Theorem 8.8.3 ) that the set of prime-strips in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] is equipped with a natural action of G L v G_{L_{v}} which corresponds to the galois action on the set of arithmetic holomorphic structures via its action on Y ℂ p ♭ , L v {Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},L_{v}} i.e. the natural operations of the theory permute the prime strips of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . Let me describe Mochizuki’s gluing of two prime-strips of the type: (8.8.1) F M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i ⊢ ▶ × μ = G v ↷ O ¯ v × μ × q ℕ {F}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\vdash\blacktriangleright\times\mu}=G_{v}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\overline{{O}}_{v}^{\times\mu}\times q^{\mathbb{N}} the core idea of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , especially see [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Page 23] , is that group theoretic data of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] i.e. two isomorphs ( Π 1 , Π ¯ 1 ) (\Pi_{1},\overline{\Pi}_{1}) and ( Π 2 , Π ¯ 2 ) (\Pi_{2},\overline{\Pi}_{2}) of ( Π , Π ¯ ) (\Pi,\overline{\Pi}) are glued along an isomorphism of the copies of F M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i ⊢ ▶ × μ = G v ↷ O ¯ v × μ × q ℕ . {F}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\vdash\blacktriangleright\times\mu}=G_{v}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\overline{{O}}_{v}^{\times\mu}\times q^{\mathbb{N}}. provided by each of these pairs of fundamental groups. Remark 8.8.2 . [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] attempts to accomplish this gluing [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Page 23] without explicitly exhibiting the existence of distinct primes-strips, i.e. prime-strips in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] do not have any arithmetically (or any natural or Teichmuller Theoretic) distinguishable labels. Mochizuki’s gluing claim [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Page 23] is established here in Theorem 8.8.3 by means of the theory developed in the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper) and applies to context of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . ∙ \bullet Prime-strips are local objects so in the next theorem I work with a fixed prime v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} . So this gluing does not really represent the creation of a new geometric object by gluing (as asserted in [ Mochizuki, 2022 , Example 2.4.7] ). That is why I have disagreed with claims of [ Mochizuki, 2022 , § 3.4] and [ Mochizuki, 2022 , Example 2.4.7] . More precisely, for a workable Teichmuller Theory of the sort one wants to establish here, one must demonstrate that both sides of the above diagram exist for intrinsic reasons and hence the analogy with projective line [ Mochizuki, 2022 , Example 2.4.7] is highly misleading. ∙ \bullet § 8.9 Mochizuki’s log-links The local theory of Mochizuki’s 𝔩 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔤 \mathfrak{log} -links is detailed in [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem 10.15.1] [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem ] . In [ Joshi, 2023b , Appendix] , I establish the existence of theta-links in the context of proofs of the Geometric Szpiro Inequality [ Amorós et al., 2000 ] , [ Zhang, 2001 ] . § 8.11 Mochizuki’s “Three Indeterminacies” [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] As I have pointed out in [ Joshi, 2021b ] , from the point of view of classical Teichmuller spaces and Arithmetic Teichmuller spaces, if one holds the (tempered) fundamental group fixed, then the holomorphic structure (i.e. the holomorphoid) giving rise to the said group, is necessarily indeterminate i.e the mapping 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L v ) y v ⟼ Π X / L v t ​ e ​ m ​ p \mathfrak{hol}(X/L_{v})_{y_{v}}\longmapsto\Pi^{\scriptscriptstyle temp}_{X/L_{v}} does not determine the holomorphoid i.e. the arithmetic holomorphic structure uniquely. More precisely, if the topological isomorphism class of the fundamental group Π X / L v t ​ e ​ m ​ p \Pi^{\scriptscriptstyle temp}_{X/L_{v}} is held fixed, then a holomorphoid 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L v ) y v \mathfrak{hol}(X/L_{v})_{y_{v}} giving rise to this group is necessarily an indeterminate quantity as there are many distinct holomorphoids 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L v ) y v \mathfrak{hol}(X/L_{v})_{y_{v}} (in the classical case, there are many distinct complex structures) which provide the said group. § 8.11.1 So in my view, Mochizuki’s Three Indeterminacies (see [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] ) which underlie his theory, are necessarily consequences of the existence of Arithmetic Teichmuller Spaces. Let me illustrate how this remark allows one to arrive at Mochizuki’s three indeterminacies. I am listing them as they appear in the theory in the order they get applied in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11 and Corollary 3.12] (the perspective is my own, Mochizuki’s perspective is discussed in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] ). Ind3 In [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem A (aka Theorem 3.11), Page 425] , this indeterminacy arises from the upper semi-compatibility of 𝔩 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔤 \mathfrak{log} -Links. From my point of view, this indeterminacy arises by [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem ] (reader may recall that in [ Joshi, 2023b , § ] , I have established that working with the Frobenius orbit of a point on Y L ¯ ^ v ♭ , L v {Y}_{{\widehat{\overline{L}}_{v}}^{\flat},L_{v}} corresponds to working with a column of 𝔩 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔤 \mathfrak{log} -links). To understand this, let 𝐲 0 {\bf y}_{0} be the standard point § 4.4 . This choice is not canonical and one can also use 𝝋 ⁡ ( 𝐲 0 ) \boldsymbol{\varphi}({\bf y}_{0}) as a standard point. Write 𝐲 0 = ( y v ) v ∈ 𝕍 L ′ {\bf y}_{0}=(y_{v})_{v\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}} . One wants to understand how the choice of 𝐲 0 {\bf y}_{0} over 𝝋 ⁡ ( 𝐲 0 ) \boldsymbol{\varphi}({\bf y}_{0}) affects arithmetic holomorphic structures and related Galois cohomology calculations. The local aspect of this, at each v v , is explicated in [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem ] . To recall what [ Joshi, 2023b , Theorem ] provides, let p v p_{v} be the residue characteristic of v v . Then at each v v , the choice of y v y_{v} over φ v ​ ( y v ) \varphi_{v}(y_{v}) means that the homomorphism ℤ p v ​ ( 1 ) → B ℂ p v ♭ , ℚ p v {\mathbb{Z}}_{p_{v}}(1)\to B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{v}}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p_{v}}} which send a generator to the element t ∈ B ℂ p v ♭ , ℚ p v t\in B_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{v}}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p_{v}}} is uniquely determined only up to a p p -multiple. Ind2 This indeterminacy arises as a consequence of [ Joshi, 2021a , Theorem ] , [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] and is the indeterminacy of the point 𝐲 ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 ′ {\bf y}\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}} which provides the arithmetic holomorphic structure at each v ∈ 𝕍 L ′ v\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}} . One can write this Mochizuki style as indeterminacy with respect to ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -linear isomorphisms σ : 𝔾 ^ m ​ ( O ℂ p v ♭ ) → 𝔾 ^ m ​ ( O ℂ p v ♭ ) \sigma:\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}({O}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{v}}^{\flat}})\to\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}({O}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{v}}^{\flat}}) ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Theorem ] and [ Joshi, 2023a , Theorem ] which demonstrate how such an isomorphism changes arithmetic holomorphic structures). In other words, Mochizuki’s Ind2 is the indeterminacy with respect to the choice of a point of the space of arithmetic holomorphic structures provided by the relevant Fargues-Fontaine curve X ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} . The precise demonstration of the existence of Ind2 is an important contribution of [ Joshi, 2021a ] . As remarked in [ Joshi, 2021a , § ] and [ Joshi, 2023a , Remark ] , this indeterminacy is the arithmetic analog, at all primes, of the Virasoro action in the Geometric Langlands Program [ Beilinson and Drinfel’d, 2000 ] . Ind1 This indeterminacy is what I have called anabelomorphy in [ Joshi, 2020a ] . Mochizuki’s contributed [ Joshi, 2020a , § ] to clarify the role of anabelomorphy in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] and [ Joshi, 2020a ] provides other arithmetic consequences of anabelomorphy. The precise origins of Ind1 are quite arithmetic-geometric and not group theoretic as one might be led to believe because of the group theoretic methods of [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] . This indeterminacy Ind1 arises as follows. By [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] , one may view the Fargues-Fontaine curve X ℂ p v ♭ , L v {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{v}}^{\flat},L_{v}} (and especially Y ℂ p ♭ , L v {Y}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},L_{v}} ) as a sort of moduli for arithmetic holomorphic structures. By [ Joshi, 2020b , Theorem 2.1] , this moduli is not uniquely determined by the absolute Galois group G L v = π 1 e ​ t ​ ( X ℂ p v ♭ , L v ) . G_{L_{v}}=\pi_{1}^{et}({X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{v}}^{\flat},L_{v}}). So one must consider all Fargues-Fontaine curves X {X} with π 1 e ​ t ​ ( X ) = G L v \pi_{1}^{et}({X})=G_{L_{v}} as providing arithmetic holomorphic structures which must be included to ensure the largest collection of arithmetic holomorphic structures over which one calculates averages in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] . One way to understand this is that in [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023a ] , one may think of X ℂ p v ♭ , L v {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{v}}^{\flat},L_{v}} as a parameter space for arithmetic holomorphic structures up to an action of Frobenius, then X ℂ p v ♭ , L v {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p_{v}}^{\flat},L_{v}} is itself not uniquely identified by its étale fundamental group! [Also see [ Joshi, 2023a , Remark ] .] The following proposition is useful: Proposition 8.11.1.1 . Let E E be a p p -adic field contained in an algebraic closure ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} . Then there are only finitely many p p -adic fields E ′ ⊂ ℚ ¯ p E^{\prime}\subset{\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} such that E ′ E^{\prime} is anabelomorphic to E E . In particular, for a fixed geometric point ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} (fixed for computing all the relevant fundamental groups), there are only finitely many isomorphism classes of Fargues-Fontaine curves X ℂ p ♭ , E ′ {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E^{\prime}} which are anabelomorphic to X ℂ p ♭ , E {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E} i.e such that π 1 e ​ t ​ ( X ℂ p ♭ , E ′ ) = G E . \pi_{1}^{et}({X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},E^{\prime}})=G_{E}. Proof . By [ Joshi, 2020a ] , if E E is a p p -adic field, then its absolute degree [ E : ℚ p ] [E:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}] is an amphoric quantity i.e. for any p p -adic field E ′ E^{\prime} which is anabelomorphic to E E , one has [ E : ℚ p ] = [ E ′ : ℚ p ] [E:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}]=[E^{\prime}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}] . By standard results–for example Krasner’s Lemma, there are only a finitely many subfields of ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} of a bounded degree over ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} . So the assertion follows from the main theorem of [ Joshi, 2020b ] . § 8.11.2 Canonical Interpretation of Mochizuki’s Indeterminacies One may describe these indeterminacies in the following canonical way: Crystalline Indeterminacy or Mochizuki’s Ind3 The homomorphism ℤ p ​ ( 1 ) → B → B c ​ r ​ i ​ s {\mathbb{Z}}_{p}(1)\to B\to B_{cris} is determined uniquely only up to a p p -multiple. 𝐈 M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i ℚ , 𝐈 J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , 𝐈 J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i ℚ \boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{{\mathbb{Q}}},\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}},\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}^{{\mathbb{Q}}} ). § 9.1 Bloch-Kato subspaces in Galois cohomology Readers are advised to read [ Joshi, 2023a , § ] before reading this section. As established there, Galois cohomology of a number field depends on the choice of an arithmeticoid. This point will be used throughout. Mochizuki’s construction of the codomain 𝐈 M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i \boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} for constructing Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} is based on his notion of log-shells [ Mochizuki, 2015 , Definition 5.4(iii)] . Here I will provide a more intrinsic description of log-shells than is done in [ Joshi, 2020a ] and is based on Bloch-Kato subspaces in Galois cohomology. To do this I recall the definitions of Bloch-Kato subspaces in Galois cohomology. § 9.1.1 The non-archimedean case Let E ⊃ ℚ p E\supset{\mathbb{Q}}_{p} be a p p -adic field. Fix an algebraic closure E ¯ \overline{E} of E E , let G E = Gal ⁡ ( E ¯ / E ) G_{E}={\rm Gal}(\overline{E}/E) and let E ¯ ^ \widehat{\overline{E}} be its completion equipped with its canonical action of G E G_{E} . By well-known classical results the prime p p , and the topological groups O E ∗ ⊂ E ∗ {O}_{E}^{*}\subset E^{*} are amphoric (see [ Joshi, 2020a , Theorem 3.3] or [ Jarden and Ritter, 1979 ] ). My next point is important. By [ Mochizuki, 2004 , Proposition 1.2.1] or [ Hoshi, 2019 , Proposition 4.2] one sees that the triple G E ​ ↷ ​ μ ​ ( E ¯ ) G_{E}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\mu(\overline{E}) consisting of the absolute Galois group G E G_{E} , the subgroup μ ⁡ ( E ¯ ) ⊂ E ¯ ∗ \mu(\overline{E})\subset\overline{E}^{*} consisting of the roots of unity in E ¯ \overline{E} equipped with the natural action of G E G_{E} , is amphoric and any isomorphism of topological groups G E 1 ≃ G E 2 G_{E_{1}}\simeq G_{E_{2}} of local fields E 1 , E 2 E_{1},E_{2} (with chosen algebraic closures E ¯ 1 , E ¯ 2 \overline{E}_{1},\overline{E}_{2} ) provides an isomorphism of the corresponding triples ( G E 1 ​ ↷ ​ μ ​ ( E ¯ 1 ) ) ≃ ( G E 2 ​ ↷ ​ μ ​ ( E ¯ 2 ) ) \left(G_{E_{1}}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\mu(\overline{E}_{1})\right)\simeq\left(G_{E_{2}}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\mu(\overline{E}_{2})\right) and hence an isomorphism ( G E 1 ​ ↷ ​ ℤ ^ ​ ( 1 ) E 1 ) ≃ ( G E 2 ​ ↷ ​ ℤ ^ ​ ( 1 ) E 2 ) . \left(G_{E_{1}}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{E_{1}}\right)\simeq\left(G_{E_{2}}\overset{}{\curvearrowright}\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}(1)_{E_{2}}\right). This should help understanding how one approaches Galois cohomology from the point of Arithmetic Teichmuller Spaces (for more details see [ Joshi, 2023a ] ). Now to my third point. One has an isomorphism of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -vector space ( [ Perrin-Riou, 1994 ] ): (9.1.1.1) Ext G E 1 ​ ( ℚ p ​ ( 0 ) , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) = H 1 ​ ( G E , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) . {\rm Ext}\,^{1}_{G_{E}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(0),{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))=H^{1}(G_{E},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1)). Remark 9.1.1.2 . By [ Mochizuki, 2009 , Proposition 1.4] , Mochizuki’s Galois theoretic theta classes live in Ext G E 1 ​ ( ℚ p ​ ( 0 ) , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) = H 1 ​ ( G E , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) {\rm Ext}\,^{1}_{G_{E}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(0),{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1))=H^{1}(G_{E},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1)) . The archimedean case is discussed below. ∙ \bullet Recall further that one has the decomposition of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -vector spaces (see [ Perrin-Riou, 1994 ] ): (9.1.1.3) H 1 ​ ( G E , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) ≃ H f 1 ​ ( G E , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) ⊕ ℚ p ≃ E ⊕ ℚ p . From [ Bloch and Kato, 1990 , Nekovář, 1993 , Perrin-Riou, 1994 ] one further has: (9.1.1.4) H e 1 ​ ( G E , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) = H f 1 ​ ( G E , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) ⫋ H s ​ t 1 ​ ( G E , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) = H g 1 ​ ( G E , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) = H 1 ​ ( G E , ℚ p ​ ( 1 ) ) . The first equality will play an important role in what follows. § 9.1.2 The archimedean case [ It will be useful to read my discussion of Schottky uniformization in § 12 . Mochizuki does not use the Schottky uniformization approach for his archimedean calculations, but my view is that working with Schottky uniformization at the archimedean primes sets the archimedean and the non-archimedean primes of split-multiplicative reduction on par because both have the same type of uniformization i.e. the Schottky uniformization at archimedean primes corresponds to Tate uniformization at non-archimedean primes of split multiplicative reduction. This paragraph provides the detail of this assertion. § 9.3.1 The subspace B φ = p B^{\varphi=p} Let me begin by recalling from [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 ] : Theorem 9.3.1.1 . (1) One has an isomorphism of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -Banach spaces 𝔾 ^ m ​ ( O ℂ p ♭ ) = 1 + 𝔪 ℂ p ♭ {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}({O}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}})=1+\mathfrak{m}_{{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}}}} B φ = p {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\varphi=p}} ≃ \scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq} given by 1 + x ⟼ log ⁡ ( [ 1 + x ] ) 1+x\longmapsto\log([1+x]) . (2) The ring B B is generated as a ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -algebra by B φ = p B^{\varphi=p} . Proof . These are established in [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Proposition 4.4.6] and [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Théorème 6.2.1] respectively. § 9.3.2 It is precisely because of the Theorem 9.3.1.1 and Mochizuki’s definition of the log-shell that I have asserted in [ Joshi, 2021b ] that B φ = p B^{\varphi=p} is the analog of Mochizuki’s log-shell in [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2021b ] . Here is a more precise construction. Definition 9.3.2.1 . For any prime p < ∞ p<\infty , let (9.3.2.2) I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p = 1 t ​ B φ = p . {I}_{joshi,p}=\frac{1}{t}B^{\varphi=p}. Then I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p {I}_{joshi,p} will be referred to as the log-shell for B ⁡ [ 1 / t ] B[1/t] . ∙ \bullet From § 5.2.6 recall the ring B e = B ​ [ 1 / t ] φ = 1 B_{e}=B[{1}/{t}]^{\varphi=1} , that is, B e B_{e} is the ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -algebra of Frobenius-invariant elements of B ⁡ [ 1 / t ] B[{1}/{t}] (generated by 1 t ​ B φ = p \frac{1}{t}B^{\varphi=p} ). The geometric meaning of I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p {I}_{joshi,p} is explicated by the following. Lemma 9.3.2.3 . (1) I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p = log ⁡ ( 1 + 𝔪 ℂ p ♭ ) t . {I}_{joshi,p}=\frac{\log(1+\mathfrak{m}_{{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}}})}{t}. (2) For all n ∈ ℤ n\in{\mathbb{Z}} one has φ n ​ ( I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p ) ⊂ I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p . \varphi^{n}({I}_{joshi,p})\subset{I}_{joshi,p}. (3) One has I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p = 1 t ​ B φ = p = B e d ​ e ​ g ≤ 1 . {I}_{joshi,p}=\frac{1}{t}B^{\varphi=p}=B_{e}^{deg\leq 1}. (4) Notably, the log-shell I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p {I}_{joshi,p} consists of functions on X ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} of degree ≤ 1 \leq 1 which are inariant under the Frobenius morphism of B ⁡ [ 1 / t ] B[1/t] . Proof . The first assertion is immediate from Theorem 9.3.1.1 and the definition of I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p {I}_{joshi,p} . It is enough to prove the second assertion for n = 1 n=1 . First note that if b t ∈ 1 t ​ B φ = p \frac{b}{t}\in\frac{1}{t}B^{\varphi=p} then (9.3.2.4) φ ⁡ ( b t ) = φ ⁡ ( b ) φ ⁡ ( t ) = p ⋅ b p ⋅ t = b t \varphi(\frac{b}{t})=\frac{\varphi(b)}{\varphi(t)}=\frac{p\cdot b}{p\cdot t}=\frac{b}{t} and so b t \frac{b}{t} is a φ \varphi invariant or Frobenius fixed element of B B (also see [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 , Théorème 6.5.2] ). Thirdly, by [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 ] , X ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}} can be obtained as X ℂ p ♭ , ℚ p = Proj ⁡ ( B e ) {X}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat},{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}={\rm Proj}(B_{e}) and one has the equality (9.3.2.5) I j ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , p = 1 t ​ B φ = p = B e d ​ e ​ g ≤ 1 . {I}_{joshi,p}=\frac{1}{t}B^{\varphi=p}=B_{e}^{deg\leq 1}. Hence the assertion is clear. Remark 9.3.2.6 . 𝐈 M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i ℚ \boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{{\mathbb{Q}}} ) for Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i ​ I \widetilde{\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}{I} Let me briefly describe Mochizuki’s choice of codomain or container for theta-values. Mochizuki’s formalism of tensor packets is discussed in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , § 3] . Assumptions § 2.4 , § 3.1 will now be strictly in force. In [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , Mochizuki bundles theta-values arising primes w | p w|p by means of tensor products of log-shells. This is the analog in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] of the formalism of bundling rings considered earlier in § 7.5 . The formalism of bundling rings allows us to use the ring structure to multiply the theta-values at primes w | p w|p in a common ring. The formalism of tensor products and tensor packets [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Section 3] provide this functionality in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] . § 9.4.1 Let 𝐲 0 = ( y w ) w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ {\bf y}_{0}=(y_{w})_{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}} be the chosen standard point (as chosen in § 4.4 ). The proof is clear from the constructions, the definitions of these objects and standard properties of Galois cohomology. § 9.4.3 Mochizuki’s method in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] relies on anabelian reconstruction theory and the proof requires recovering a lot of information about X / L X/L purely from the tempered fundamental groups of X / L v X/L_{v} for v ∈ 𝕍 L v\in\mathbb{V}_{L} . This necessitates tracking a lot more information than is needed for my methods above. This is not required in my approach. Instead of appealing to Mochizuki’s Anabelian Reconstruction [ Mochizuki, 2012 , Mochizuki, 2013 , Mochizuki, 2015 ] , one can use the Rosetta-Stone § 8.5 to translate the required objects from my theory and Mochizuki’s Reconstruction Theory is not needed. Nevertheless to be fully compatible with [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] , I will work with Mochizuki’s setup for defining the codomain for his theta-values locus. § 9.4.4 I will follow [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Section 3] closely for the most part. It will be convenient to work with the graph of the correspondence which gives rise to Mochizuki’s Adelic Ansatz § 4 instead of working with Mochizuki’s Adelic Ansatz. This means that instead of working with 𝐳 = ( 𝐳 1 , … , 𝐳 ℓ ∗ ) ∈ Σ ~ L ′ \mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{z}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{z}_{\ell^{*}})\in\widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} one works with the ℓ ∗ + 1 {\ell^{*}}+1 -tuple ( 𝐳 0 , 𝐳 1 , … , 𝐳 ℓ ∗ ) (\mathbf{z}_{0},\mathbf{z}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{z}_{\ell^{*}}) where 𝐳 0 ⟼ ( 𝐳 1 , … , 𝐳 ℓ ∗ ) \mathbf{z}_{0}\longmapsto(\mathbf{z}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{z}_{\ell^{*}}) is the correspondence on 𝐘 𝐋 \mathscrbf{Y}_{L} giving rise to Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} . Notably, if 𝐳 Θ \mathbf{z}_{\Theta} is the standard point § 4.5 of Σ ~ L ′ \widetilde{\Sigma}_{L^{\prime}} , then one has the ℓ ∗ + 1 {\ell^{*}}+1 -tuple 𝐳 Θ = ( 𝐲 0 ′ , 𝐲 1 ′ , … , 𝐲 ℓ ∗ ′ ) \mathbf{z}_{\Theta}=({\bf y}_{0}^{\prime},{\bf y}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,{\bf y}_{\ell^{*}}^{\prime}) associated with this point. § 9.4.5 Mochizuki’s Procession of prime-strips Following [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Section 3] , for 0 ≤ j ≤ ℓ ∗ 0\leq j\leq{\ell^{*}} , let 𝕊 j + 1 = { 0 , 1 , … , j } \mathbb{S}_{j+1}=\{0,1,\ldots,j\} so that each set 𝕊 j + 1 \mathbb{S}_{j+1} has exactly j + 1 j+1 elements. Remark 9.4.5.2 . Mochizuki’s choice of the sets A = 𝕊 j + 1 A=\mathbb{S}_{j+1} for j = 1 , … , ℓ ∗ j=1,\ldots,{\ell^{*}} is dictated by his reconstruction theoretic approach in which Mochizuki reconstructs a specific instance of X / L w ′ X/L_{w}^{\prime} from anabelian data. Notably in [ Mochizuki, 2021a ] , 𝕊 ℓ ∗ \mathbb{S}_{{\ell^{*}}} is in bijection with the set of cusps of a certain tempered covering constructed from X / L v X/L_{v} at a prime v ∈ 𝕍 L ′ n ​ o ​ n v\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}^{non} . From the point of view of the present series of papers ( [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 , Joshi, 2023b , Joshi, 2023a ] and this paper), anabelian reconstruction arguments are unnecessary and all functionality which is needed in establishing [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] is provided in my approach by working with distinct holomorphoids 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ′ ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L^{\prime})_{{\bf y}} . ∙ \bullet § 9.4.6 Tensor products of Mochizuki’s log-shells Given 𝐳 = ( 𝐳 0 , 𝐳 1 , … , 𝐳 ℓ ∗ ) ∈ 𝐘 𝐋 ′ ′ \mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{z}_{0},\mathbf{z}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{z}_{\ell^{*}})\in\mathscrbf{Y}_{L^{\prime}}^{\prime} and if A A is one of the sets 𝕊 j + 1 ​ ( 𝐳 ) \mathbb{S}_{j+1}(\mathbf{z}) for 1 ≤ j ≤ ℓ − 1 1\leq j\leq\ell-1 , obtained using this ℓ ∗ + 1 {\ell^{*}}+1 -tuple of arithmeticoids, let (9.4.6.1) I p A ​ ( L ′ ) = ⨂ a ∈ A I p ​ ( L ′ ) = ⨂ a ∈ A ( ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p I ​ ( L w ′ ) 𝐳 a ) {}^{A}{{I}_{p}}(L^{\prime})=\bigotimes_{a\in A}{I}_{p}(L^{\prime})=\bigotimes_{a\in A}\left(\bigoplus_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}_{p}}{I}({L_{w}^{\prime}})_{\mathbf{z}_{a}}\right) and (9.4.6.2) I ℚ p p A ​ ( L ′ ) = ⨂ a ∈ A I p ℚ p ​ ( L ′ ) = ⨂ a ∈ A ( ⨁ w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ p L w ′ ) 𝐳 a . {}^{A}{{I}}_{p}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(L^{\prime})=\bigotimes_{a\in A}{{I}}_{p}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(L^{\prime})=\bigotimes_{a\in A}\left(\bigoplus_{w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}}_{p}}L_{w}^{\prime}\right)_{\mathbf{z}_{a}}. Remark 9.4.10.2 . In particular, this remark proves my assertion that tensor packet structure in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Section 3] is to compensate for the missing ring structure (in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] ) needed to take products of the theta-values at distinct primes w | p w|p . From my point of view this is directly available through the product structure of the period ring B p B_{p} of [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 ] . ∙ \bullet § 9.4.11 These construction lead to the following definition Definition 9.4.11.1 . (1) This construction is carried out in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Proposition 3.1] and 𝐈 𝐌 ​ 𝐨 ​ 𝐜 ​ 𝐡 ​ 𝐢 ​ 𝐳 ​ 𝐮 ​ 𝐤 ​ 𝐢 \mathscrbf{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} is called the holomorphic log-shell . (3) In both the instances, Mochizuki’s notation is a bit more complex and I will not use it here. ∙ \bullet § 9.5 Mochizuki’s evaluation of theta-values in log-shells The assumptions § 2.4 and § 3.1 , § 3.3 will now be in force. Let 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L ′ ) 𝐲 \mathfrak{hol}(X/L^{\prime})_{{\bf y}} be a holomorphoid of X / L ′ X/L^{\prime} . Let w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}} . Let 𝐲 = ( y w ) w ∈ 𝕍 L ′ {\bf y}=(y_{w})_{w\in\mathbb{V}_{L^{\prime}}} and let 𝔥 ​ 𝔬 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( X / L w ′ ) y w \mathfrak{hol}(X/L_{w}^{\prime})_{y_{w}} be the local holomorphoid of X / L X/L at w w given by 𝐲 {\bf y} . § 9.5.1 By properties of L ′ / L L^{\prime}/L (available by § 2.4 and § 3.1 , § 3.3 ) one sees that the entire 2 ​ ℓ 2\ell -torsion of C / L ′ C/L^{\prime} is defined over L ′ L^{\prime} and hence the 2 ​ ℓ 2\ell -torsion of C / L w ′ C/L_{w}^{\prime} is defined over L w ′ L_{w}^{\prime} . So one can apply Mochizuki’s Galois theoretic evaluation of theta-values ( [ Mochizuki, 2009 , Proposition 1.4] ) to deduce that these classes, say one of them is ξ w \xi_{w} , lands in ξ w ∈ H 1 ​ ( G L w ′ ; K w , ℤ p w ​ ( 1 ) K w ) \xi_{w}\in H^{1}(G_{L_{w}^{\prime};K_{w}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p_{w}}(1)_{K_{w}}) where ℤ p w ​ ( 1 ) K w {\mathbb{Z}}_{p_{w}}(1)_{K_{w}} is the G L w ′ ; K w G_{L_{w}^{\prime};K_{w}} -module of p w p_{w} -power roots of unity contained in K w K_{w} . However it is more useful for [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c ] to construct cohomology classes in H e 1 ​ ( G L w ′ ; K w , ℤ p w ​ ( 1 ) K w ) H^{1}_{e}(G_{L_{w}^{\prime};K_{w}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{p_{w}}(1)_{K_{w}}) (with B c ​ r ​ i ​ s B_{cris} constructed using the completed algebraic closure of L w ′ L_{w}^{\prime} provided by K w K_{w} ). § 9.5.2 Let me say that one cannot view the classes ξ w \xi_{w} provided by the Tate parameter in H e 1 H^{1}_{e} . So [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] resorts to another approach to this which consists of considering multiplicative action of ξ w \xi_{w} on the log-shell see for instance [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Figure 3.1] and [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11(b)] . This action emerges naturally in my approach in the proof of Theorem 9.9.1 . This is remedied (in § 9.6 below) by explicitly constructing Frobenius trivial, crystalline cohomology classes associated to any Tate parameter and hence also theta-values on any holomorphoid of any Tate elliptic curve. These classes do live in Mochizuki’s log-shells! § 9.6 Construction of crystalline, Frobenius invariant Galois cohomology class associated to a Tate elliptic curve Here by crystalline and Frobenius invariant Galois cohomology classes , I mean elements of H e 1 ​ ( − , − ) H^{1}_{e}(-,-) . Explicitly e B ​ K e_{BK} is the homomorphism x ⟼ e p m ⋅ x p m m ≫ 0 . x\longmapsto\frac{e^{p^{m}\cdot x}}{p^{m}}\qquad m\gg 0. Let log p \log_{p} denote the principal branch of the p p -adic logarithm (i.e. log p ⁡ ( p ) = 0 \log_{p}(p)=0 ). Remark 9.7.1.2 . (1) It is important to recall a basic fact about the p p -adic exponential function. Let me remind the reader that the power series giving the p p -adic exponential function e x = ∑ n = 0 ∞ x n n ! e^{x}=\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{x^{n}}{n!} (here one can take x ∈ ℂ p x\in{\mathbb{C}}_{p} , with the usual normalization | p | ℂ p = 1 p \left|p\right|_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}=\frac{1}{p} ) has radius of convergence | x | ℂ p < | p | ℂ p 1 / ( p − 1 ) = ( 1 p ) 1 / ( p − 1 ) \left|x\right|_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}<\left|p\right|^{1/(p-1)}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}=\left(\frac{1}{p}\right)^{1/(p-1)} (see [ Koblitz, 1984 , Chapter IV, Section 1] ). Hence for p = 2 p=2 the disk of convergence is | x | ℂ 2 < | 2 | ℂ 2 = 1 2 \left|x\right|_{{\mathbb{C}}_{2}}<\left|2\right|_{{\mathbb{C}}_{2}}=\frac{1}{2} while for p p odd, the disc of convergence strictly contains the open disk | x | ℂ p < 1 p \left|x\right|_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}<\frac{1}{p} . This should help clarify the difference in the formulae given below when w | 2 w|2 . (2) Importantly, the p p -adic exponential does not converge on p p -adic units or even on 1 1 -units but the series defining e p m ⋅ u e^{p^{m}\cdot u} converges for any p p -adic unit u u for suitably large m m . Proof . This is proved exactly as [ Joshi, 2023a , Proposition ] . § 9.8 The definition of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} and Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} My definition of the theta-values locus Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}} is here § 6.2.1 and of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i {\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} is here § 9.8 . Let me make the following remarks which will help clarify the construction: (1) One important point to be noted is that Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} and Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} refer respectively to slightly different codomains 𝐈 𝐌 ​ 𝐨 ​ 𝐜 ​ 𝐡 ​ 𝐢 ​ 𝐳 ​ 𝐮 ​ 𝐤 ​ 𝐢 \mathscrbf{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} and 𝐈 𝐉 ​ 𝐨 ​ 𝐬 ​ 𝐡 ​ 𝐢 \mathscrbf{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}} . Mochizuki uses Anabelian Reconstruction Theory in [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] , and this approach requires tracking a lot of additional data and this reflected in 𝐈 𝐌 ​ 𝐨 ​ 𝐜 ​ 𝐡 ​ 𝐢 ​ 𝐳 ​ 𝐮 ​ 𝐤 ​ 𝐢 \mathscrbf{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} . My approach is construction as opposed to reconstruction and requires less information be tracked and leads to a simpler codomain 𝐈 𝐉 ​ 𝐨 ​ 𝐬 ​ 𝐡 ​ 𝐢 \mathscrbf{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}} (with a homomorphism 𝐈 𝐌 ​ 𝐨 ​ 𝐜 ​ 𝐡 ​ 𝐢 ​ 𝐳 ​ 𝐮 ​ 𝐤 ​ 𝐢 → 𝐈 𝐉 ​ 𝐨 ​ 𝐬 ​ 𝐡 ​ 𝐢 \mathscrbf{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}\to\mathscrbf{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}} ) for defining Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} . (2) The substantively non-trivial portion of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] is the construction of the Theta-values set Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} in 𝐈 𝐌 ​ 𝐨 ​ 𝐜 ​ 𝐡 ​ 𝐢 ​ 𝐳 ​ 𝐮 ​ 𝐤 ​ 𝐢 \mathscrbf{I}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} (or 𝐈 ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i \widetilde{\mathscrbf{I}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} ). (3) The bound asserted by [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] is natural modulo the existence of this set. (4) Both [ Joshi, 2023b ] and [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] require working with a certain number of copies of a fixed value group (explicitly | ℂ p ♭ | ⊂ ℝ \left|{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}\right|\subset{\mathbb{R}} ) for each prime p p . (5) As discussed in [ Joshi, 2023b ] , in my theory one works with copies of the value group of the tilt | ℂ p ♭ | |{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}| (one can do this by [ Scholze, 2012 ] or [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 ] ) and all the valuation calculations can be made in this chosen, fixed value group | ℂ p ♭ | \left|{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}\right| for each prime p p . One may further assume that | ℂ p ♭ | ⊂ ℝ \left|{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat}\right|\subset{\mathbb{R}} if one needs to work with realified value groups (remember that [ Fargues and Fontaine, 2018 ] works with valuations taking values in ℝ {\mathbb{R}} ). (6) In [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] , neither tilts nor algebraically closed complete valued fields are available, and value groups are tracked separately by means of perfect and realified Frobenioids of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} (for each prime p p ). My discussion of perfect and realified Frobenioids is in § 10 especially § 10.4 . (7) This provides the value group equal to the value group of ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} (which is equal to that of ℂ p {\mathbb{C}}_{p} as ℂ p {\mathbb{C}}_{p} is an immediate extension of ℚ ¯ p {\bar{{\mathbb{Q}}}}_{p} by [ Poonen, 1993 ] ) and ℂ p {\mathbb{C}}_{p} has value group equal to that of ℂ p ♭ {\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\flat} by [ Scholze, 2012 , Lemma 3.4] . (8) The construction of Theta-locus provides the valuation data arising from distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures which are comparable in appropriate common value group at each prime. (9) Without the existence of distinct arithmetic holomorphic structures [ Joshi, 2021a , Joshi, 2022 ] and the construction of [ Joshi, 2023b ] this idea is difficult to understand and one can arrive at incorrect conclusion regarding [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] . § 9.8.1 Now one can define Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i \widetilde{\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} . The construction is similar to that of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i \widetilde{\Theta}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}} (in fact the latter is modeled on the former). Theorem-Definition 9.8.1.1 is essentially [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] . Proof . This is clear from Corollary 9.8.1.3 . Lemma 9.8.2.7 . Let E E be a p p -adic field and let | − | E \left|-\right|_{E} be a p p -adic absolute value on E E . Then for all x ∈ ( p ∗ ⋅ O E ) − { 0 } x\in(p^{*}\cdot{O}_{E})-\{0\} one has | log E ⁡ ( 1 + x ) | E = | x | E . \left|\log_{E}(1+x)\right|_{E}=\left|x\right|_{E}. Proof . Let π ∈ O E \pi\in{O}_{E} be a uniformiser of O E {O}_{E} . By [ Koblitz, 1984 ] , log E ⁡ ( 1 + x ) \log_{E}(1+x) converges for | x | E < 1 \left|x\right|_{E}<1 . As E E is a p p -adic field and | − | E \left|-\right|_{E} is a p p -adic absolute value on E E i.e. E E has a discrete value group, so | x | E ≤ | π | E \left|x\right|_{E}\leq\left|\pi\right|_{E} for all x ∈ 𝔪 E x\in\mathfrak{m}_{E} . Thus this series converges for all x x such that | x | E ≤ | π | E \left|x\right|_{E}\leq\left|\pi\right|_{E} and hence the n t ​ h n^{th} term | x n n | E → 0 \left|\frac{x^{n}}{n}\right|_{E}\to 0 as n → ∞ n\to\infty . So one may approximate log E ⁡ ( 1 + x ) \log_{E}(1+x) by its partial sum ∑ n = 1 N ( − 1 ) n − 1 ⋅ x n n = x + higher order terms \sum_{n=1}^{N}\frac{(-1)^{n-1}\cdot x^{n}}{n}=x+\text{ higher order terms} for some sufficiently large N N and estimate the absolute value of this partial sum. Hence, by the standard properties of p p -adic absolute values, it is enough to show that | x n n | E < | x | E n ≥ 2 . \left|\frac{x^{n}}{n}\right|_{E}<\left|x\right|_{E}\qquad n\geq 2. This is true if | x n − 1 | < | n | E \left|x^{n-1}\right|<\left|n\right|_{E} for all n ≥ 2 n\geq 2 or equivalently | x | E < | n | E 1 / ( n − 1 ) . \left|x\right|_{E}<\left|n\right|_{E}^{1/(n-1)}. So it is enough to verify that | x | E < inf n ≥ 1 { | n | E 1 / ( n − 1 ) } . \left|x\right|_{E}<\inf_{n\geq 1}\left\{\left|n\right|_{E}^{1/(n-1)}\right\}. If p ∤ n p\nmid n then | n | E = 1 \left|n\right|_{E}=1 , and if p | n p|n then write n = p k ⋅ r n=p^{k}\cdot r with k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , p ∤ r p\nmid r , so it is enough to check that | x | E < inf k ≥ 1 , r ≥ 1 { 1 , | p k | E 1 / ( p k ⋅ r − 1 ) } = inf k ≥ 1 , r ≥ 1 { 1 , | p | E k / ( p k ⋅ r − 1 ) } . \left|x\right|_{E}<\inf_{k\geq 1,r\geq 1}\left\{1,\left|p^{k}\right|_{E}^{1/(p^{k}\cdot r-1)}\right\}=\inf_{k\geq 1,r\geq 1}\left\{1,\left|p\right|_{E}^{k/(p^{k}\cdot r-1)}\right\}. For any fixed 0 < t < 1 0<t<1 the continuous function of y y on the open interval ( 0 , 1 ) (0,1) given by f ⁡ ( t ) = t y f(t)=t^{y} is a decreasing function of y y . As k p k ⋅ r − 1 < k p k − 1 \frac{k}{p^{k}\cdot r-1}<\frac{k}{p^{k}-1} for r ≥ 2 r\geq 2 , one may replace the rightmost infimum by | x | E < inf k ≥ 1 , r ≥ 1 { 1 , | p k | E 1 / ( p k ⋅ r − 1 ) } = inf k ≥ 1 { 1 , | p | E k / ( p k − 1 ) } . \left|x\right|_{E}<\inf_{k\geq 1,r\geq 1}\left\{1,\left|p^{k}\right|_{E}^{1/(p^{k}\cdot r-1)}\right\}=\inf_{k\geq 1}\left\{1,\left|p\right|_{E}^{k/(p^{k}-1)}\right\}. Now for k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , k ⟼ k / ( p k − 1 ) k\longmapsto k/(p^{k}-1) is an exponentially decaying, decreasing function and hence k / ( p k − 1 ) < { 1 / ( p − 1 ) if ​ p ≠ 2 , 1 if ​ p = 2 , k/(p^{k}-1)<\begin{cases}1/(p-1)&\text{ if }p\neq 2,\\ 1&\text{ if }p=2,\end{cases} equivalently (for any p p and each k ≥ 2 k\geq 2 ) k / ( p k − 1 ) < 1 / ( p − 1 ) . k/(p^{k}-1)<1/(p-1). So one has | x | E < inf k ≥ 1 , r ≥ 1 { 1 , | p k | E 1 / ( p k ⋅ r − 1 ) } = inf k ≥ 1 { 1 , | p | E 1 / ( p − 1 ) } = | p | E 1 / ( p − 1 ) . \left|x\right|_{E}<\inf_{k\geq 1,r\geq 1}\left\{1,\left|p^{k}\right|_{E}^{1/(p^{k}\cdot r-1)}\right\}=\inf_{k\geq 1}\left\{1,\left|p\right|_{E}^{1/(p-1)}\right\}=\left|p\right|_{E}^{1/(p-1)}. Now by the hypothesis of the theorem x ∈ ( p ∗ ​ O E ) − { 0 } x\in(p^{*}{O}_{E})-\{0\} . Thus | x | E ≤ | p ∗ | E < | p | E 1 / ( p − 1 ) . \left|x\right|_{E}\leq\left|p^{*}\right|_{E}<\left|p\right|_{E}^{1/(p-1)}. Hence under the hypothesis of the lemma, the asserted estimate holds. This proves the lemma. § 9.9 Proof of the fundamental estimate for | Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 | \left|{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}}\right| and | Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 | \left|{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}}\right| . Proof of Theorem 9.9.1 . The strategy of the proof is the same as the local strategy of [ Joshi, 2023b ] . One exhibits an element, namely 𝝃 𝐳 = 𝝃 𝐳 Θ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i \boldsymbol{\xi}_{\mathbf{z}}=\boldsymbol{\xi}_{\mathbf{z}_{\Theta}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}} (resp. 𝝃 𝐳 = 𝝃 𝐳 Θ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i \boldsymbol{\xi}_{\mathbf{z}}=\boldsymbol{\xi}_{\mathbf{z}_{\Theta}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}} ), of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} , Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} , Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} for which the absolute value can be computed and one shows that | 𝝃 𝐳 | \left|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{\mathbf{z}}\right| exceeds the number on the right of the stated inequality. Since | Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 | \left|{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}}\right| (resp. | Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 | \left|{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}}\right| ) on the left of the asserted inequality is a supremum, the inequality claimed in the theorem will follow if the absolute value of this chosen element bounds above the quantity on the right of the asserted inequality in the theorem. Since Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 , Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ~ , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 , Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}},{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}},{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} as defined in § 9.8.1 , Theorem-Definition 9.8.1.1 are adelic, and | Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 | \left|{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}}\right| and | Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 | \left|{{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}}\right| are defined multiplicatively, one may work with components Θ ~ 𝐈 J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i , w {\widetilde{\Theta}^{\mathscrbf{I}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi},w} , Θ ~ 𝐈 M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i , w {\widetilde{\Theta}^{\mathscrbf{I}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki},w} for each w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}} . So it is enough to estimate | Θ ~ 𝐈 M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i , w | \left|{\widetilde{\Theta}^{\mathscrbf{I}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki},w}\right| and then take product over all w ∈ 𝕍 ¯ w\in\underline{\mathbb{V}} . First I will work with the product versions Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} and Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} and then deduce the inequality for the tensor product versions Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} and Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} from this. Note that these products are finite by Corollary 9.8.1.3 . [As remarked earlier, by Lemma 9.8.2.7 , | log p ⁡ ( 1 + p w ∗ ) | L w ′ = | p ∗ | L w ′ \left|\log_{p}(1+p_{w}^{*})\right|_{L_{w}^{\prime}}=\left|p^{*}\right|_{L_{w}^{\prime}} . Now let me say how one may deduce the inequality for Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} from Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} (resp. Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} from Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} ). One may pass from the finite direct product ∏ w | p L w ′ \prod_{w|p}L^{\prime}_{w} (equivalently from the finite direct sum) of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -vector spaces to the tensor product ⊗ w | p L w ′ \otimes_{w|p}L_{w}^{\prime} of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -vector spaces as follows. Consider the mapping ∏ w | p L w ′ ∋ ( a w ) ⟼ ⊗ w | p ( a w ) ∈ ⨂ w | p L w ′ ⟼ | ⊗ w | p a w | ⊗ w | p L w ′ ∈ ℝ , \prod_{w|p}L^{\prime}_{w}\ni(a_{w})\longmapsto\otimes_{w|p}(a_{w})\in\bigotimes_{w|p}L^{\prime}_{w}\longmapsto\left|\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{w|p}a_{w}\right|_{\otimes_{w|p}L_{w}^{\prime}}\in{\mathbb{R}}, where first mapping is the natural homomorphism of ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -vector spaces: ∏ w | p L ′ w → ⊗ w | p L ′ w , \prod_{w|p}L^{\prime}_{w}\to\otimes_{w|p}L^{\prime}_{w}, and the second mapping is the tensor norm. In other words, one considers the image of the theta-values locii in the tensor product (via the first mapping) and then takes supremum over the tensor norms of all the images under the first homomorphism. This is where the cross-norm property § 7.6.2 of the tensor product norms comes in to play. This property says one has | ⊗ w | p a w | ⊗ w | p L w ′ = ∏ w | p | a w | L w ′ . \left|\mathop{\otimes}\limits_{w|p}a_{w}\right|_{\otimes_{w|p}L_{w}^{\prime}}=\prod_{w|p}\left|a_{w}\right|_{L_{w}^{\prime}}. Hence one may also work with suprema of absolute values of the images of the theta-locii in the tensor products i.e. one can work with Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} instead of Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} (resp. work with Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ~ , Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} ) and still arrive at the required estimates in the tensor product settings (i.e. for Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} (resp. Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} ) of [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] . Notably, since the tensor product ⊗ w | p L w ′ \otimes_{w|p}L_{w}^{\prime} is constructed using the product ∏ w | p L w ′ \prod_{w|p}L_{w}^{\prime} (more precisely the tensor product is the quotient of a suitable free ℚ p {\mathbb{Q}}_{p} -module on the set underlying this product), one can expect that the theta-values locus in the tensor product setting (i.e. Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 , Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} ) should provide tighter upper bounds than the theta-values locus in the product setting i.e. Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 ~ , Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ~ {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}},{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\tilde{\mathscrbf{I}}} . In any of these cases, each class 𝝃 𝐲 ¯ j \boldsymbol{\xi}_{{\underline{{\bf y}}}_{j}} contributes log B ​ K ⁡ ( 𝝃 𝐲 ¯ j ) \log_{BK}(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{{\underline{{\bf y}}}_{j}}) in L w ; 𝐲 j ′ L^{\prime}_{w;{\bf y}_{j}} and by the definition of this class and, at each w w one has, by ( 9.7.2.2 ) one has: (9.9.2) | log B ​ K ⁡ ( 𝝃 w , j ) | L w ′ , j = | log L w ′ ⁡ ( 1 + p ∗ ⋅ q w ; This proves the theorem. Remark 9.9.7 . It is precisely because of Lemma 9.8.2.7 and the property established by Equation 9.9.2 in the proof of Theorem 9.9.1 that one recovers Mochizuki’s multiplicative action of theta-values on log-shells depicted in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Figure 3.1] , i.e. multiplication by theta-value on the log-shell, used in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Theorem 3.11] for defining Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} instead of constructing crystalline Galois cohomology classes as has been done here. ∙ \bullet § 9.10 Relationship with Mochizuki’s log-volumes and hulls Since Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} (resp. Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} ) are contained in adelic compact sets, one can compute volumes of the smallest compact subset containing these sets. So one needs to define a volume of sets of this type which arise in realizing this idea. This theory is detailed in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Proposition 3.1, Proposition 3.9] and this theory is applied in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] and in [ Mochizuki, 2021d ] for estimating the volume Vol ⁡ ( Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ) {\rm Vol}({\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}}) of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}} . More precisely, Vol ⁡ ( Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ) {\rm Vol}({\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}}) is the volume of the smallest compact set (of a suitable sort chosen below) containing Θ ~ J ​ o ​ s ​ h ​ i 𝐈 {{{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Joshi}}}}^{\mathscrbf{I}} . In [ Mochizuki, 2021a , Mochizuki, 2021b , Mochizuki, 2021c , Mochizuki, 2021d ] one works with LogVol ⁡ ( Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ) = log ⁡ Vol ⁡ ( Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ) ∈ ℝ ∪ { ± ∞ } , {\rm LogVol}({\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}})=\log{\rm Vol}({\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}})\in{\mathbb{R}}\cup\{\pm\infty\}, which is the natural logarithm of Vol ⁡ ( Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ) {\rm Vol}({\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}}) . The fundamental inequality of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] is an assertion about the log-volume LogVol ⁡ ( Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ) {\rm LogVol}({\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}}) of Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 {\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}} . Equivalently, it is an inequality about the volume Vol ⁡ ( Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ) {\rm Vol}({\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}}) . As I have remarked in [ Joshi, 2023c , § 1.12] , the volume computation of [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] is quite similar to the sort of volume computations in classical Teichmuller Theory which, for instance, occur in [ Mirzakhani, 2007 ] . § 9.10.1 As explained in § 1 , my approach to establishing [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] i.e. establishing the fundamental estimate for LogVol ⁡ ( Θ ~ M ​ o ​ c ​ h ​ i ​ z ​ u ​ k ​ i 𝐈 ) {\rm LogVol}({{\widetilde{\Theta}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{Mochizuki}}^{\boldsymbol{\mathscrbf{I}}}}) is different from that of Mochizuki. Though at this point, using the results of the preceding sections along with the Rosetta Stone Fragments 8.3.5 , 8.3.6 , 8.5 , 8.7.1 , 8.11.3 and Theorem-Definition 9.8.1.1 , it is possible to proceed exactly as Mochizuki does, to arrive at Mochizuki’s proof of [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] . However once the principles underlying [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Corollary 3.12] are understood, one can formulate approaches different from that of Mochizuki. I will take this approach to the proof rather than repeat Mochizuki’s approach. § 9.10.2 Let me begin with the theory of volumes described in [ Mochizuki, 2021c ] . The basic properties are summarized in [ Mochizuki, 2021c , Proposition 3.9] and also in [ Mochizuki, 2015 , Proposition 5.7] . Let L ′ L^{\prime} be a number field, and for w | p w|p in L ′ L^{\prime} , one has the p p -adic field L w ′ L_{w}^{\prime} . Let O L w ′ ⊂ L w ′ {O}_{L_{w}^{\prime}}\subset L_{w}^{\prime} be the ring of integers. Since L w ′ L_{w}^{\prime} is a locally compact abelian group, there is a unique normalized translation invariant (real valued) Haar measure on L w ′ L_{w}^{\prime} which I will denote by Vol L w ′ {\rm Vol}_{L_{w}^{\prime}} . If S ⊂ L w S\subset L_{w} , is a measurable subset, for some w w , I will write Vol L w ′ ​ ( S ) {\rm Vol}_{L_{w}^{\prime}}(S) for its normalized Haar measure. Here normalization means the following: Vol L w ′ ​ ( O L w ′ ) = 1 . {\rm Vol}_{L_{w}^{\prime}}({O}_{L_{w}^{\prime}})=1. Let (9.10.2.1) log : ( 0 , ∞ ) → ℝ \log:(0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{R}} be the natural real-valued logarithm function and extend log \log by setting log ⁡ ( 0 ) = − ∞ ​ and ​ log ⁡ ( ∞ ) = ∞ . \log(0)=-\infty\text{ and }\log(\infty)=\infty. This extended logarithm function takes values in ℝ ∪ { ± ∞ } {\mathbb{R}}\cup\{\pm\infty\} . By the log-volume S ⊂ L w ′ S\subset L_{w}^{\prime} , I will mean LogVol L w ′ ​ ( S ) = log ⁡ ( Vol L w ′ ​ ( S ) ) ∈ ℝ ∪ { ± ∞ } . {{\rm LogVol}}_{L_{w}^{\prime}}({S})=\log({\rm Vol}_{L_{w}^{\prime}}(S))\in{\mathbb{R}}\cup\{\pm\infty\}. In particular, this means that one has LogVol L w ′ ​ ( O L w ′ ) = 0 . {{\rm LogVol}}_{L_{w}^{\prime}}({{O}_{L_{w}^{\prime}}})=0. Let π ∈ O E \pi\in{O}_{E} be a uniformizer.
